## Summary

Adaptive Audio & Tells is the layer that turns the ship's published truth into sound: a hiss, drip or clank at each real fault, a distinct running voice for each of the four resource loops, the hull knocking from nowhere as the web notices the crew, and a bounded alarm rhythm when a breach lands. It exists so that a player who cannot look still knows what the ship and the web are doing (audio is the only non-visual channel for the 10 to 30 second warning before a breach) and so that a restrained player hears the web lose interest while a careless one hears their own run. Audio can lie only through one hallucination sound that the Perception Filter requests for one player, always with an audible tell of its own; every other sound is the true state at every sanity stage. At MVP it ships the tells the breach warning needs and the true-state witness sounds; music, material footsteps and final mix polish are Vertical Slice or Alpha.

> **Quick reference** — Layer: `Presentation` · Priority: `MVP` · Key deps: `Life-Support Graph (designed)`, `Attention System (designed in this run)`, `Horror Entities (designed in this run)`, `Sanity & Hallucination Rules (designed in this run)`, `Command & Simulation Layer (designed)`; provisional on undesigned neighbours only *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: `Perception Filter (designed; its Audio rows re-checked at the X-6 scan)`, `Boarding & Salvage (designed; re-checked at the X-6 scan)`, `HUD & Suit Layer (designed)`, `In-World Instruments (designed)`, `Chart Table (designed)`, `Chat/Voice (undesigned)`, `Co-op Session (undesigned)`, `Accessibility Settings (undesigned)` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

## Overview

Adaptive Audio & Tells is the layer that turns the ship's published truth into sound, and the only layer that lets a player who cannot look still know what the ship and the web are doing. In one sentence: **it is a presentation-only service whose decisions are scene-tree free (a `PRESENTATION` subscriber that registers no hook, no state block, no mutator and no command) that reads the Graph's descriptors and node reads, Attention's published tell, Horror's published facts, Suit, Controller, Interaction, Assembly, Body and Web Map events, and the Perception Filter's per-viewer audio requests, and plays each as a cue whose identity, place, level and timing are a pure function of that published state, under one identity table that keeps every family of sound provably separate from every other.**

**Can audio lie? (the answer this GDD is required to give, `IDX:41`).** Audio can lie only through the hallucination family the Perception Filter requests for one viewer, every member of which carries an audible leak; the witness sounds, running-loop sounds, tell and breach audio, Horror's sounds, Suit and body cues, footsteps and foley, the human voice and the P1 proximity cue never lie, are never suppressed, and are identical at every sanity stage. The family is the Filter's `FILTER_AUDIO_CLASSES` namespace (MVP ids `FA_LIE_SIG_GAUGE` and `FA_LIE_SIG_SPRITE`, one class-(b) reading-lie sound signature realised under two surface ids; VS-A ids `FA_PHANTOM_MOVE` and `FA_PHANTOM_PRESENCE`) and nothing else is PERCEIVED. Audio corruption therefore joins the Filter's write set as audio requests, under the same rule as a visual lie (SN-25; PF-20), and the voice channel is never faked either way.

**The data layer.** Audio owns **no simulation state**. It persists nothing, replicates nothing and is not hashed (`state_hash` is unchanged by muting any bus, AUD-9). What it owns is *authored data and presentation memory*: (1) the **master identity table** of every audio family, with one bus and one asset-tag token per family and a build-time lint that proves the families disjoint (AA-6); (2) the **cue-request function**, `cue_requests(published_state, presentation_memory) -> [cue request]`, which is the only thing the rules in this document define and the only thing a headless test needs to exercise (AA-3); (3) *presentation memory*, a handful of last-seen counters per listener (`(tell_epoch, tell_beat)`, `phase`, `transition_seq`, `result_seq`, `last_result.T`, the shared `last_alarm_T`) that make cues fire on transitions only and are rebuilt from published state after any restore (AA-4, AA-5); and (4) the audio-owned constants and the data file `assets/data/audio/tell_audio.json`, all `[PLACEHOLDER]` unless a registry name says otherwise. Playing a request (pool, buses, listener node) is an engine layer and is deliberately kept out of the rules (-> ADR candidates, Open Questions).

**The player-facing effect.** The player does not meet a soundtrack; they meet a ship that tells the truth by sound. Each of the four resource loops has its own running voice, each failure has a dry, specific, physical sound at the place it is happening (a hiss, a drip, a clank, a hum that sags, a seam that creaks) that is there before the mist is lit and in a dark room, and the hull itself starts to knock, deep and from nowhere, ahead of anything on screen when the web notices them. The knocks come closer together while the player keeps moving; if the player goes still, the escalation stops where it is, and then the bed under the knocks sags and lets go, and that is the only reward restraint ever gets. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* If they do not, a bounded, rhythmic alarm marks the breach on a ship with or without a beacon. The player's own run is louder than a walk, which is the only noise meter the game provides. Under hallucination the ear becomes the sense that is worth more, not less: exactly one family of sound can be false, it is always the one with a fringed, breathing signature that no real machine makes, and the real machine's own sound is still playing behind it.

**The contract this GDD owns.** Pillar 2's "a tell a careful player could have perceived (sound, light, gauge drift, hull stress)" is satisfied *through two independent senses* only if the audio channel is complete on its own. By the standing user decision there is no Attention indicator of any kind, including under accessibility settings (`ART:954` item 15), so audio is the **only non-visual Attention channel**; its consequence is that this GDD must (a) repeat Attention's six must-repeat requests M1-M6 and AUD-1 to AUD-10 so that AC-AT-78 can close, (b) make the tell audible to every living player through a non-positional bed with a measured margin, (c) never let the channel become a meter by ear, and (d) say plainly that a deaf or hard-of-hearing player's tell channel is the visual rim plus the opt-in, off-by-default haptic beat pulse of AA-42 (user ruling D51: the same information as the audio beat, so not an indicator). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* The same GDD is the join point of four truth channels (Graph, Attention, Horror, Sanity), so it also owns the identity table and lint that Horror and Attention cite under the since-dangling names "AUD-13" and "AUD-14" (refresh rows in Cross-References).

**What the game loses without it.** Without this GDD a low-vision player has no tell and AC-AT-47's audio half, AC-AT-78 and the TellAudit definition of "perceivable" cannot close; the art bible's HR-9 ("all five loops have distinct running and failing sounds; every Tell has an audio component starting no later than its visual") has no owner; the Graph's closed consequence list has no ear on it, so a hiss in a dark room is a missing feature rather than expected behaviour; Horror has no body to place a creak on; Sanity's HR-5 sound mode (the third leg of the tri-modal leak) cannot be met and a lie becomes falsifiable by gleam and glide alone; and "playing by sound alone, never looking" (`LSG:800`), which the Graph protects as a non-degenerate strategy, has no support.

**MVP slice (run ruling R17, `CON` MVP Definition).** Adaptive audio at final fidelity is *not* MVP (`CON:395`, `CON:479-480`): the MVP bar is *distinctness and truth*, never polish. P0, the Pillar 2 contract, ships first: the full `attn` set (onset carrying beat 0, beats, bed, tier layers, positional creak at the locus, release, breach entry, alarm cadence with fallback, subside, pre-tell creaks). P1: the eight Graph witness classes at descriptor anchors with `alarm_eligible` gating, four running timbres plus the INTEGRITY room tone, footsteps, the class-(b) reading-lie signature, Horror's four cue kinds for `horror_t01`, and the three Suit body cues ruled into MVP (breath cadence, the reserve valve click, the visor-fog step; D92). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* P2 (cut-first at MVP; may fall back to placeholder one-shots if the schedule slips): the remaining Suit body cues, the interaction cue set, assembly outcome cues, pod hop events, minimal UI. Wired at MVP, assets Vertical Slice: `BODY_*` events and the pod leg bed. Vertical Slice or Alpha: music (none at MVP; the ship's sounds are the score), material-dependent footsteps, phantom movement (class a), the P1 proximity cue live (VS-B), Voice bus use (VS-B), caption and settings implementation (Alpha), ring-shifted ambience and voice-pool polish. The full table is AA-7.

**Index wording, reconciled.** `IDX:41` asks for "distinct running and failing sound per loop read directly from Graph state". Four loops (O2, POWER, COOLANT, FUEL) have a running timbre read from `node()`/`loop_component()`; the fifth, INTEGRITY, is *static* (`LSG:66`: it has no running state), so its "running sound" is the room tone of a pressurised, intact hull and its failing sounds are the Graph's CREAK (SEAL_STRAIN) and flow-zero TICK descriptors (AA-26, conflict register CX-A10; no owner action). "Horror and Attention cues" are AA-14 to AA-23 and AA-28, AA-29. "The P1 proximity cue" is AA-39 (a diegetic suit sound, dormant in solo MVP, live at VS-B).

**Dependency status at authoring (Glob of `design/gdd/`, 2026-10-26/27, 23 files).** Designed and locked: Command & Simulation Layer, Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera, Player Controller & Input, Life-Support Graph. Designed in this run and on disk: Attention System, Horror Entities, Sanity & Hallucination Rules (all headed `Designed`), Perception Filter and Boarding & Salvage (both now headed `Designed`; their Audio rows are quoted below at the lines the designed files carry), Emitter State, 2D Lighting, Failure & Trail, Interaction, Suit & Equipment, Item & Module Database, Modular Ship Assembly, Ship Body & Interior Physics, Ship Section & Layout, Save/Load, Derelict Authoring, Web Map & Transit. **In flight (a draft on disk, header `In Design`)**: **Chart Table (Web Map & Menus)** (its Audio asks, `CT:393`, `CT:524` and `CT:570`, are read here). No file on disk: HUD & Suit Layer, In-World Instruments & Inspect View, Debug Overlay (True Values), Chat/Voice/Pings (VS-B), Co-op Session (VS-B), Accessibility Settings (Alpha), Settings & Input Rebinding (Alpha). Every statement about an in-flight or absent neighbour below is marked `(provisional — <dep> in flight)` or `(provisional — <dep> undesigned)` and is re-checked when that neighbour lands; a statement about a neighbour designed in this run is marked `(designed; re-checked at the X-6 scan)`. No ADR exists; implementation shapes are ADR candidates (listed in Open Questions). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Neighbour status re-read 2026-10-27 (all headed `Designed`):** Chart Table (Web Map & Menus), HUD & Suit Layer, In-World Instruments & Inspect View and Debug Overlay are now on disk, so they are no longer in flight or absent; the only neighbours without a file are Chat/Voice/Pings, Co-op Session, Accessibility Settings and Settings & Input Rebinding. The Graph publishes `opening(edge_id)` (LS-19 additions, `LSG:206`); Layout has confirmed this GDD's `hull_anchor` derivation (D.16) as the contract and publishes its inputs (`LY:545`), not a read of its own; refresh rows RF-1 to RF-4 and RF-6 are applied (X-4, X-6).

**Rule ids.** Rules are `AA-n` (not `AUD-n`: Attention's `AUD-1` to `AUD-10` are *requirements on this GDD*, and Horror and Attention still carry dangling cites to `AUD-13` and `AUD-14`, so reusing that namespace would make those cites ambiguous). Formulas are `D.n`, edge cases `E-AA-n`, acceptance criteria `AC-AA-n`, Visual/Audio rows `AAV-n`, open questions `OQ-AA-n`.

## Player Fantasy

**The ship tells the truth by sound.** You cannot always see. The lamp costs you the web's attention, the dark is honest, and half of what keeps you alive is two rooms away. But you can hear it. The scrubber's dry cycling, the coolant pump's slow heavy turn, the power bus's hum: each loop has its own voice, and you learn them the way a night-watch engineer learns a ship. A hiss in a dark room is a real leak at a real place, whether or not anything is lit to show the mist. When the hull knocks, deep and from nowhere, before anything on screen has changed, that is the web noticing; when you go still and the knocking sags and lets go, you hear it lose interest. Nothing tells you a number. Everything tells you a thing.

Your eyes can be lied to. Your instruments can be lied to. The ship's own sounds cannot. Only one kind of sound ever lies, and it always gives itself away: a warm fringe on a reading that no real machine on this ship makes. Walk to the machine and listen, and it tells you what it is really doing.

**The emotion is earned certainty in the dark.** The power is not over the web, which you never silence by listening; it is over doubt. The deeper your sanity goes, the more your ears are worth.

### What this section does not duplicate

Attention already owns the emotion *"It noticed"* and the power fantasy *"I know what makes me loud, and I can choose to be quiet"* (`AT:47-57`, `ART:200`), with tell-and-abort as its anchor moment. Horror owns "weather with intent" (`HE:1271`), Sanity owns "doubt, not horror" (`SN:1260`). This GDD inherits those as the *moods of three families it plays* and restates none of them as its own. It owns one thing none of them owns: **the ear as an instrument of truth.**

### Framing and the power fantasy

**Framing F1 (direct, recommended and adopted): "The ship tells the truth by sound."** The pillar sentence turned into the audio domain: P1's "air hisses" plus P2's "a tell a careful player could have perceived (sound ...)". It gives every family a truth class (AA-2) and makes "can audio lie?" answerable in one sentence. It is falsifiable (design test below). *Rejected F2, "the ship breathes"* (the concept's audio line, `CON:395`): it puts body language on the sane world, which the art bible reserves for the stage-3 lie ("pipes read as veins", `ART` 2.9; Attention rejected the same move for its own framing, `AT:57`); a literally breathing running loop would collide with the `hal` signature's 4 to 6 s amplitude breathing and so stop the leak being a leak; and "breath" is already the Suit's word for the human. What survives of F2 is the concept-level instruction that a running home-ship is never silent (CD-1, resolved by the creative-director at CD-GDD-ALIGN, Cross-References). *Rejected F3, "the listener / sonar operator"*: a listening stance implies a `listen` verb or an audibility boost, which BS-4 forbids (`BS:179`) and which is a meter by ear; its affect survives only as *stillness is how you hear more, because your own footsteps stop* (AA-31).

**Emotion: "I can hear that it's true."** Earned certainty in the dark: the moment a player stops needing to look because the ship is telling them what it is doing and they have learned its language. It is the opposite pole of the game's horror ("the web attacks the crew's ability to know whether the ship is fine", `CON:49-50`); audio is where that ability is defended.

**Power fantasy: the night-watch engineer who knows the ship by ear.** Not a fighter, a hunter or a sonar operator: a keeper. With the lamp off they can name each running loop, hear which compartment is weeping air before the mist is lit, hear the hull start knocking before anything else changes, and hear it let go when they go still. Their power is **discernment, not control**: listening never makes the web quieter, it only lets them know sooner. The vocabulary is learnable and stable (one timbre per loop, eight witness classes, one tell family), so mastery is real and transfers across every derelict (competence); listening is free in resources but costs stillness, nearness and patience, so it is a genuine entry in the verification economy of P4, where "checking costs time, risk or trust" (`CON:263`) (autonomy); and, from Vertical Slice, "do you hear that too?" is a cross-check crewmates can make because every true sound is identical on every client (relatedness). **Playing by ear is protected, not a degenerate strategy** (`LSG:800`: "not degenerate; listed so no one 'fixes' it").

### Anchor moment (audio-owned)

> **Lamp off, derelict B, compartment 3.** The cut is done (channelled verbs are lit-only, so the lamp was on for it); you killed the chest lamp straight after, to let the web's attention settle before the walk back. Now it is properly dark. You stand still and listen. The coolant pump two rooms aft is running: you know it is coolant by its slow, heavy circulation, not the O2 scrubber's dry cycling hiss-and-stop. Ahead and to the left, a thin hiss you cannot see; no mist, because nothing is lit to show it (`FT:207`: expected, not a bug). That is the seam weep you cut past. Then, under everything, from no direction at all, the hull knocks once. Deep, unpitched. Then again. Nothing on screen has changed. You set the cutter down and hold still. The knocks keep coming, but they stop closing in and the hull stops getting heavier: the web has not let go, but it has stopped winding up. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* Then, once your restraint has held (`ABORT_HOLD_TICKS`), the low bed under them sags and lets go (the release, `RELEASE_TICKS`). Quiet. The hiss is still there; it was never going to lie to you. You light the lamp, and the mist is exactly where your ears said it was.

What this moment proves, as testable claims: (1) **audio leads vision** (HR-9; FT-35; AUD-1: beat 0 before the first filament, `AT:1194`); (2) **audio is a complete channel**, the whole tell from onset to release legible with the screen dark (AUD-5: each channel sufficient alone; `ART:954` item 15); (3) **light confirms the ear, never contradicts it** (P1: the hiss and the mist are one descriptor at one anchor, FT AC-137); (4) **restraint is heard as release, not rewarded as victory** (AUD-6; no "danger over" cue, V10).

*(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* **Both decisions this anchor depended on are ruled.** *Hearing range (OQ-AA-32, user ruling (b)).* D.9 is a log-distance law with an audibility floor for running loops, so the machinery is audible out to `R_AUDIBLE_MAX_PX` (609 px, about twelve tiles, two rooms of about six tiles) instead of about one screen; the fantasy text stands and is not rescoped. A hiss the player can place is audible through a closed door at that range (D.8 and D.9 examples); whether a running pump two rooms off is heard over the room tone is not yet shown at the placeholder levels and is the listening-pass check (D.19 R-g, AC-AA-138, OQ-AA-44). Localisation is horizontal pan plus level; whether a vertical cue is added stays open (OQ-AA-43). *Timeline (OQ-AA-34, user ruling, X4).* Escalation is behaviour-keyed: Attention's escalation clock freezes while the Attention level `L` is below `theta_HOLD`, and the audio tiers follow the frozen escalation. The true sequence for a player who goes still before the escalation clock reaches `TIER2_TICKS` is therefore "no worse, no worse, release", which is the anchor above, not a best case; a player who keeps moving still hears tier 2 and tier 3 arrive. This GDD's earlier recommendation to reject freezing (it would leak "being forgiven") is superseded: the freeze is a physical consequence of the player's own stillness, heard as the published escalation holding, with three discrete tiers and no continuous readout, so it does not become a meter by ear.

*Secondary anchor (P4, MVP class b).* The scrubber's gauge reads healthy, and as its needle glides into place there is a faint warm, fringed sound where an honest needle makes none: a sound no real machine on this ship makes. You walk over. The scrubber's own running sound has the stalled cadence your ear already knew. The gauge lied; the machine did not.

### Pillar text served (verbatim) and what audio owes each

> **P2 Dread Before Chaos.** "Every breach is preceded by a tell a careful player could have perceived (sound, light, gauge drift, hull stress). This pillar owns tempo and presentation; Pillar 3 owns causality. *Design test*: breach fires at a hidden threshold vs. breach preceded by a 10-30 second escalating tell → the tell." **PRIMARY.** Audio is the tell's spine and its only non-visual channel.

> **P3 The Web Watches.** "Attention is shown in the world (creaking, flicker, filament movement), never as a hidden meter." **PRIMARY.** "Shown in the world" includes heard in the world: creaks are physical events of the hull, never a readout.

> **P1 The World Never Lies.** "Pipes leak, frost forms, air hisses; the physical world is ground truth. Only instruments and perception can be wrong." "The human channel. Voice/proximity chat ... are never faked." **SERVES.** "Air hisses" is in the pillar's first sentence: the hiss is P1's own example of ground truth, so the witness sounds belong to the inviolable channel.

> **P4 Sanity Rewrites Reality.** "No lie is unfalsifiable: the truth is always available through the physical world, a second instrument, a saner crewmate, the human channel, or time." **SERVES (bounded).** The ear is the "physical world" check that is always available; the one audio lie (the `hal` family) always leaks.

P5 is touched, not served: death is silent (`SL:23`), and a Vertical Slice Depth Ring may shift only pre-tell ambient density, never the tell contract or the beat floor.

### Design test (this system's own)

> **"A sound whose loudness, rate or pitch encodes a hidden number vs. a sound that is the true state of one thing at one place → the thing."**

| Proposal | Hidden number? | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| Creak density rising continuously with `level_pm` | yes (`level_pm`) | Cut (AT-26; stepped `CUE_STEPS` only, floor `AMBIENT_CUE_FLOOR_PM` 800) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)* |
| Hiss volume from the descriptor's true `intensity_pm` at its anchor | no: it is the leak's own size at the leak | Keep (LS-14) |
| A stinger that gets louder as the horror nears | yes (distance) | Cut (`HE:1193`) |
| Horror creep played at `pos_px` with ordinary falloff | no: it is the thing at its place | Keep (AA-28) |
| Heartbeat that quickens with low sanity | yes (own sanity) | Cut (SN-14, AA-38) |
| Footsteps louder when running | no: it is your gait, a true state (PC-10: "own run audible as the only noise meter") | Keep (AA-31) |
| Footsteps louder when Attention is high | yes | Cut |
| P1 proximity cue from a crewmate's suit with physical falloff | no: it is their suit, at their place | Keep (AA-39, VS-B) |

*Secondary test (P4):* **"An audio lie that sounds exactly like a real machine vs. one that carries a fringe no real machine on this ship makes → the fringe."** (AA-35; HR-5.)

### How the fantasy deepens under hallucination (it does not break)

Sanity loss raises the value of the ear rather than eroding it. (1) At every sanity stage 0 to 3 **every TRUE family is bit-identical**: witness classes, running loops, `attn` tell and breach, Horror, Suit body cues, footsteps, foley, interaction, body events, the P1 cue and the voice (SN-27). Stage 1 instruments lie and stage 2 the light lies (`CON:336-339`), so by stage 2 the ear is the most trustworthy sense the player has: the game's arc runs *eyes to ears*. (2) Exactly one family is PERCEIVED, `hal`, ids from the Filter's `FILTER_AUDIO_CLASSES` namespace; at MVP it has one signature, the class-(b) reading-lie sound, which is simultaneously the lie's audio and its SOUND leak mode (HR-5). Phantom movement (class a) is VS-A; class (c) adds no hallucinated sound (the P1 cue is true). (3) The lie can only *add* a fringed sound to a lying reading; it can never remove, mask, duck, delay or imitate a true sound, so the worst a lie does to the ear is add a doubt that announces itself. (4) **No self-sign**: the player never hears their own sanity (no heartbeat, no mix change, no sting at a stage boundary, debunk or recovery; SN-14); they learn their state from the world disagreeing with itself, which is P4's verification economy. (5) The arc for the player: early, the ear is a convenience; mid-game, a shortcut; at stage 2 to 3, the anchor: "the gauge says fine, the light says fine, but I can hear the pump stalling" (`CON:61-64`, delivered by sound). **Falsifiability guarantee:** every `hal` sound is disprovable by walking to the thing and hearing its true running or witness sound, which the lie cannot alter. No audio lie is unfalsifiable.

### Tone and language (enforceable in review)

**Register.** Diegetic, physical, under-stated: every sound in the sane world has a body and a place (a pump, a seam, a plate, a boot, a suit valve). The ship's sounds are the score. **Moods inherited, cited, never restated as this GDD's own:** `attn` "It noticed ... Not yet fear: attention" (`ART:200`), unpitched and structure-borne, knocks and strain, never tones; Horror "patient, wet and structural ... weather with intent" (`HE:1193`), positional and floor-borne, from a place, never a stinger; `hal` "doubt, not horror ... wrong, warm where it should not be, fringed, over-reaching" (`SN:1260`), a fringe on a real-sounding thing, never a scream. **The witness and running families' own mood: *workmanlike honesty*** — steady, specific, unglamorous; a pump sounds like a pump doing a job; failing sounds are dry facts, not drama. **Prose vocabulary:** use *hiss, drip, knock, creak, strain, hum, cycle, weep, settle, let go, sag*; never, for the sane world, *breathes, pulse, heartbeat, veins, groan of the living ship, the ship is alive* (those belong to the stage-3 lie and to the Suit's body cues); never *sting, stinger, hit, reward, fanfare, all-clear, danger over, power-up*. ("Pulse" appears in this document only for the alarm cadence, which the art bible names a pulse.)

### Reference techniques (adopt / reject)

| Reference | Technique | Decision | Reason (locked text) |
|---|---|---|---|
| *Barotrauma* (`CON:360`) | Hull stress and pressure as structure-borne sound that precedes a breach | **Adopt** for the `attn` bed and positional creak layer | P2; AUD-4 |
| *Barotrauma* | Loud klaxon on every flood | **Reject** as the first warning | `LSG:976` "never as the first warning"; AA-20 |
| *Satisfactory* (`CON:358`) | Each machine has a legible running sound | **Adopt** for running loops (HR-9) | P1 |
| *Oxygen Not Included* (`CON:361`) | Gas and flow state audible | **Adopt** narrowly: hiss scales with true `intensity_pm` at the anchor | LS-14 |
| *Project Zomboid* (`CON:357`) | Your own noise draws trouble; no meter | **Adopt** footsteps as the only noise readout | PC-10 |
| *Alien: Isolation* | Presence tracked by its sound | **Cite Horror's adoption** (`HE:1271`), do not re-adopt | AA-28 |
| *Alien: Isolation* | Motion-tracker ping encoding distance | **Reject** | `HE:1193`: a distance-scaled cue is a meter by ear |
| *Amnesia* / *Dead Space* / *Darkest Dungeon* | Heartbeat, breathing, filtering or whispers keyed to sanity or stress | **Reject** | SN-14; AA-38 |
| Survival-horror genre | Music stinger on proximity; tension music rising before an attack | **Reject** | `HE:1193`; AUD-8; AA-40 |
| Film: *Pandorum* (`CON:365`) | Waking into a ship whose machine sounds are the only company | **Adopt as tone** for the opening hours: the cryo bay is quiet, the first loop you start is the first "music" | AA-40 |
| Film: *Beyond the Aquila Rift* (`CON:363`) | "The kindness of the lie": a warm plausible surface over wrongness | **Adopt as the `hal` mood** | `SN:1260` |
| Generic UI | Hit markers, kill and loot chimes, value-tiered pickup sounds | **Reject** | Not a combat game; `IDB:991` |
| Hollywood | "Danger over" sting, death sound, victory fanfare | **Reject** | V10; `SL:23`; no win state |
| Common horror | "Held breath" total silence just before the attack | **Reject** | `AT:1223`: it reduces evidence in the floor window |

### Anti-pillar and user-decision guard rails this system carries

| Anti-pillar / user decision | Rule this GDD carries |
|---|---|
| Not a combat game | No hit, kill, damage-dealt, reward or loot-value sound. Tools that repel sound like tools (foley tier 4), never like weapons landing (AA-34). |
| No intentional PvP; friendly harm is lethal ("death is a bitch") | No sound attributes harm to a player (no "friendly fire" sting). Death plays no sound and shows no screen (`SL:23`); restart from the last autosave is silent. |
| No win state | No fanfare, no "all-clear"; `phase == CALM` is never mapped to safety (AUD-10, AA-21). Abort is heard as release, never as reward (AUD-6, AA-19). |
| Not a freeform builder | Weld and cut sounds are functional confirmations (the bead settle is the single "it is done", `MSA:1092`); no decorative build sounds, no placement chimes (AA-33). |
| No authored story | No narrated audio, no scripted stingers; every sound derives from published state (AUD-1, AA-4). |
| User decision: no Attention indicator anywhere, including accessibility (`ART:954` item 15) | No continuous mapping of `level_pm`; no caption or visual setting keyed to the tell, breach, pre-tell creaks or Horror (AA-41 to AA-43). Audio is the only non-visual Attention channel; its one ruled companion is the opt-in, off-by-default haptic beat pulse for a deaf or muted player, which carries exactly the information of the audio beat and nothing more (AA-42; user ruling D51), and haptics driven only by the player's own true body cues are not Attention indicators (D44). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| User decision: chest lamp, hands-free | Toggling the lamp has a click (PCI) but **light itself is silent** (`LO:429`); a lamp or lantern hum, if any, never varies with Attention. |
| User decision: Zomboid zoom ladder | Nothing a player hears changes with zoom or camera lead; the listener is the avatar (AA-9). |
| P1 human channel | The voice and the P1 proximity cue are never faked, altered, ducked by a lie, or caught by any setting that strips the World group (AA-39, AA-43). |

### We will know this was right if

A playtester with the lamp off names the coolant pump from the scrubber without a prompt; a tester on audio alone (video hidden) counts at least `MIN_BEATS_IN_FLOOR` beats in a 600-tick armed tell and says "the hull is knocking" before anything on screen changes; a tester told nothing about the lie signature identifies the lying instrument from sound alone at 90 per cent or better when already standing within reading range of it (a near-field distinctness test, not remote detection: beyond `HAL_RADIUS_PX` the leak is inaudible); and the negative gate holds *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: no tester can state the Attention level more precisely than one step of the pre-tell creak ladder (AA-22, `CUE_STEPS` 4: the ladder is the intended coarse Pillar 3 readout, "attention is shown by creaking"), no tester can judge a monster's distance from any sound except that monster's own positional creep (AA-28 a, ordinary physical falloff), and no tester can state their own sanity stage by sound. What the vision forbids is a continuous, numeric or non-diegetic readout; a coarse diegetic one is the design.

## Detailed Design

### Core Rules

*Conventions.* Rules are named `AA-n`; each states a **Rule**, a **Why**, a **Pillar** and **Decided by**. Rules state what a player hears, when, from which published fact, at what priority and how a test proves it; node classes, bus effect chains and DSP are engine notes or ADR candidates, never rules. Citation key (`file:N` is a 1-indexed line of the file named; all paths under `design/gdd/` unless prefixed): `KRN` command-simulation-layer · `PIPE` pixel-perfect-render-pipeline-camera · `PCI` player-controller-input · `LSG` life-support-graph · `AT` attention-system · `HE` horror-entities · `SN` sanity-and-hallucination-rules · `PF` perception-filter · `SE` suit-and-equipment · `IA` interaction-system · `MSA` modular-ship-assembly · `WM` web-map-and-transit · `BDY` ship-body-and-interior-physics · `BS` boarding-and-salvage · `IDB` item-and-module-database · `FT` failure-and-trail-system · `EM` emitter-state-and-light-exposure-query · `LO` 2d-lighting-and-occlusion · `LY` ship-section-and-layout-data-model · `SL` save-load-and-versioned-format · `IDX` systems-index · `CON` game-concept · `ART` design/art/art-bible.md. Registry constants are cited **by name only** (line numbers drift) and are never restated with another value; a different value is a CONFLICT to surface, not a silent change (Cross-References, "Conflicts"). Every number this GDD invents is `[PLACEHOLDER]` with a proposed registry name (Tuning Knobs, part 2). A line marked `(provisional — X)` depends on a GDD that is in flight or absent. `-> ADR candidate` marks an implementation pattern deliberately left out of the rule text. Where the two specialist proposals disagreed and no ruling spoke, the choice is written `(orchestrator ruling: ...)` in the rule.

**Reading order.** §A Foundations (AA-1 to AA-13) · §B The tell layer, `attn` (AA-14 to AA-23) · §C The witness and running layers, `wit` and `run` (AA-24 to AA-27) · §D The Horror layer, `hor` (AA-28, AA-29) · §E Body, foley and event sounds (AA-30 to AA-34) · §F The hallucination family, `hal`, the human channel and music (AA-35 to AA-40) · §G Accessibility, settings, data and the band plan (AA-41 to AA-46).

#### A. Foundations (what Audio is)

**AA-1. Can audio lie? One corruptible family, nothing else.**
*Rule.* Audio has exactly one PERCEIVED family, `hal`, played only for the viewer the Perception Filter names, only on a Filter audio request, and only with an audible leak (AA-35). Every other family is TRUE: its cue exists if and only if the published true fact exists, at the true place, with the true parameters, at every sanity stage and Filter state. The one-sentence answer is the Overview's: *audio can lie only through the hallucination family the Perception Filter requests for one viewer, every member of which carries an audible leak; the witness sounds, running-loop sounds, tell and breach audio, Horror's sounds, Suit and body cues, footsteps and foley, the human voice and the P1 proximity cue never lie, are never suppressed, and are identical at every sanity stage.* Audio corruption is inside the Filter's write set as audio requests, under the same rules as a visual lie (SN-25; PF-20). The voice channel (VS-B) and the P1 proximity cue are TRUE and are never altered, faked, silenced or delayed by any Filter or sanity state. At MVP the family has one sound signature, the class-(b) reading-lie signature, realised under the Filter's two MVP ids (AA-35); phantom movement (class a) is VS-A; class (c) adds no hallucinated sound (the P1 cue is true).
*Why.* HR-5 needs a sound signature on every lying reading; SN-25 authorises a hallucination family; `LSG:976` and `LSG:864` say phantom-entity audio is "in its own classes"; AT-17 forbids a false calm or phantom tell at any tier. "Audio never lies" contradicts HR-5 and SN-25; "audio lies anywhere off the consequence channel" breaks the tell and P3.
*Pillar.* P1, P4. *Decided by.* SN-25; PF-20; HR-5 (`ART:976`); AT-17, AUD-7; `LSG:976`; `IDX:41` (the voice channel is never faked).

**AA-2. The truth-class table (required artefact).** Exactly one row is PERCEIVED.

| Family (token) | Trigger fact | Published source | Truth | Tier | Captionable | Slice |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Witness (`wit`) | a live descriptor with `acoustic != NONE` and `medium != NONE` | Graph descriptor fields, `failures(filter)` | TRUE | 1 | Y | MVP |
| Graph alarm-class annunciator (`ann`, klaxon timbre; bus Witness) | a descriptor with `alarm_eligible` | Graph descriptor field | TRUE | 1 | Y | MVP |
| Running (`run`) | node `state` OK or DEGRADED with `served_pm` > 0; INTEGRITY compartment pressurised (the INTEGRITY room tone is `run`, AA-26) | Graph `node(id)`, `loop_component()`, `N/` `L/` keys | TRUE | 1 | Y | MVP |
| Footsteps (`foot`) | Controller gait class WALK, RUN or CARRY plus the odometer cycle position crossing a foot-contact frame (PC-11) | Controller odometer, gait | TRUE | 1 | Y (own-action foley, CX-A7) | MVP |
| Suit body (`suit`) | Suit cue state fields | Suit read view | TRUE | 1 | Y | MVP |
| Tell (`attn`): onset, beats, bed stems, release, breach entry, alarm pulses, subside | `attention.view` deltas | `attention.view` | TRUE | 2 | **N** | MVP |
| Tell (`attn`): positional hull-stress layer | `phase == TELL`, `tier >= 2`, `locus_section_id` | `attention.view` | TRUE | 3 | **N** | MVP |
| Tell (`attn`): pre-tell hull-settling creaks | `phase == CALM`, quantised cue step >= 1 | `attention.view` via `ambient_cue_step` | TRUE | 4 | **N** | MVP |
| Horror (`hor`): strike, REACH resolve | `strike_T`, `reach_end_T` | `horror.view` | TRUE | 2 | **N** | MVP |
| Horror (`hor`): floor-borne creep, ingress/egress creak | `pos_px`, ingress/egress events | `horror.view` | TRUE | 3 | **N** | MVP |
| Interaction (`ia`) | `channel_view` transitions, `result_seq` | Interaction | TRUE | 4 | Y (three terminals, `IA:979`) | MVP |
| Assembly (`asm`) | `last_result` with `outcome_cue_gate`; seam-cut TOOL loop | Assembly | TRUE | 4 | Y | MVP |
| Body and pod (`body`, `pod`) | `BODY_*`, `POD_*`, `BERTHED`, `UNBERTHED` events; the BERTHING state read (berth bed) | Body, Web Map | TRUE | 4 | Y | berth bed and berth cues MVP; `BODY_*` and the pod leg bed wired MVP, assets VS |
| P1 proximity cue (`p1c`) | another seat's `sanity_view.tell_on` | `sanity_view` | TRUE | 3 | **N** | contract MVP, live VS-B |
| Voice (`vox`) | human speech | Chat/Voice (VS-B) | TRUE | outside the world mix | n/a | VS-B |
| Ambience (`amb`) | static room tone | static data | TRUE | 4 | N | MVP minimal |
| UI (`ui`) | HUD and menu events (including `UI_INSTRUMENT_CARD_OPEN` / `UI_INSTRUMENT_CARD_CLOSE`) | HUD, designed (hud-and-suit-layer.md); Instruments (the card events) | TRUE | outside the world mix | Y | MVP minimal |
| Music (`mus`) | none at MVP | none | TRUE | 5 | n/a | VS/Alpha |
| **Hallucination (`hal`)** | a Filter audio request with SOUND in the lie's leak modes | Filter request *(designed; re-checked at the X-6 scan)* | **PERCEIVED** | 3, voice guaranteed (AA-12) | **N** | MVP one signature |

*Why.* A single table makes the lint (AA-6) a lookup and the "can audio lie?" answer checkable by anyone. Hidden real entities keep their sounds (`CON:243`, `SN:306`): a hallucination that hides a horror's body never silences its creep. *Pillar.* P1, P4. *Decided by.* SN-27; AT-17; the routed truth-class requirement.

**AA-3. Audio is a PRESENTATION subscriber that owns no sim state, and every rule is a pure function of published state.**
*Rule.* Audio registers no hook, no state block, no mutator, no command and no `push_stimulus` (KRN H1, H4, `KRN:125`); it persists nothing (cues are transitions-only, `AT:269`); it reads `SIM` and `PERCEPTION_OUT` views and writes audio nodes only (`KRN:110`); the P5 handlers and the frame poll hold no write capability *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (GDScript has no run-time "writes disabled" mode, so none is claimed: a snapshot test and a source lint prove it, AC-AA-84). Attention and the Graph read nothing of Audio: no audio symbol in their source, and muting any bus leaves `state_hash` unchanged (AUD-9; AC-AT-09). Audio never causes a tick, and pausing audio never pauses the sim (CR-11). Audio never reads `sanity.perception` (SN-23, CR-12): it consumes only what the Filter supplies. **Every cue is the output of one pure function**, `cue_requests(published_state, presentation_memory) -> [request]`, testable headless with a dummy audio driver; playing a request is the engine layer. A request carries (the `cue_request` record; registry: `cue_request`):

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `cue_id` | an identity-table row (AA-6); never reused across families |
| `family`, `tier` | the truth class and M3 tier (AA-2, AA-11) |
| `kind` | `ONESHOT` or `SUSTAINED` (States and Transitions, cue kinds) |
| `anchor` | `{body_id, ship-local px}` or NONE (NONE = non-positional: the tell bed, the alarm pulses, UI) |
| `transmission` | AIRBORNE, STRUCTURE or NONE (AA-10) |
| `gain`, `pitch`, `rate` | source-side parameters before attenuation |
| `start_T`, `seek` | the requested start (sim `T`) and a late-start offset (restore only) |
| `listener_scope` | LOCAL, ALL_LIVING (the bed), ALL_EXCEPT_SOURCE (the P1 cue), OWNER_PRIVATE (hal requests, per seat; AA-46) |
| `leak_flags` | `hal` only; SOUND, copied from the Filter's request |

Floats are allowed in the presentation layer (it never feeds the sim, CR-11); *which* cues exist is exact (integers and published counters). -> ADR candidate: presentation-subscriber polling, the request record and the node-layer mapping.
*Why.* KRN CR-10, CR-11, CR-12; headless CI has no audio driver, so audibility is a manual test and cue selection must be automatic. *Pillar.* P1. *Decided by.* KRN H1/H4/H5, AUD-9, SN-23, `current-best-practices.md:181`.

**AA-4. Cue delivery: poll counters, edge-detect, one beat per poll, no catch-up.**
*Rule.* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **One pipeline, two steps; only the second creates requests.** (1) The P5 notification handlers (CR-10) only *record*: each stores the latest published counters and dirty keys into the presentation memory's mailbox. A handler is idempotent (running it once per sim step of a catch-up frame leaves the same mailbox as running it once) and it creates no cue request, schedules nothing and calls no `play()`. (2) One **frame poll** per render frame, ordered after the frame's last sim step and notification and before the presenter's draw of that frame (a call-order guarantee, AA-13), reads the mailbox, classifies the net change and creates the requests. A fact published in a catch-up frame is therefore requested in that same frame and before the presenter draws, never once per sim step. The poll below is that frame poll: it compares published counters with last-seen values held in presentation memory (D.1): `(tell_epoch, tell_beat)` lexicographically, `phase`, `tier`, `tell_outcome`, `transition_seq` and `result_seq`, the `N/<id>`, `L/<loop>/<anchor>`, `C/<cid>`, `B/<cid>`, `F/<id>` dirty keys (Graph, LS-20) and `E/<id>` (Emitter, `EM:338`), `BODY_*` and `POD_*` events, and `horror.view` entry runs. A cue fires on a strictly greater pair, and the last-seen value is then set to the **observed** value whether or not a cue played (never `seen + 1`): at most one beat per poll, no catch-up burst after a hitch, pause or restore (AUD-3; AC-AT-115). A poll classifies the net change from `seen` to `now`; the D.1 rows are mutually exclusive on the published `(ph, e, b)`, so at most one event plays (ONSET, BEAT, RELEASE, BREACH_ENTER or SUBSIDE), and the intermediate transitions of a hitch are not played (D.1, E-AA-67, E-AA-68). The only time-dependent cues are those whose tick was already published (the REACH resolve, AA-28): the frame poll starts one when sim `T` has reached its published start tick and evaluates its position as a function of `T` (no timer exists, so pause freezes it by holding `T`). Timing is never taken from `_process` wall time. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* Dirty keys wake discrete cues (a loop on or off); smooth values (`served_pm`, `intensity_pm`) are polled at render rate (`LSG:201`).
*Why.* AUD-1 forbids a timer in Audio. A `seen + 1` rule would replay skipped beats one per frame; a restore that left `seen` ahead of an earlier published epoch would drop the next real onset, which is a false calm. *Pillar.* P2, P3. *Decided by.* AUD-1, AUD-3; KRN CR-10; `audio.md` (timing from state).

**AA-5. Sustained cues versus one-shot cues; pause, restore, death.**
*Rule.* Cues are of two kinds (States and Transitions, cue kinds). **Sustained** cues (running loops, the INTEGRITY room tone, witness classes of live descriptors, Suit breath loops, the tell bed and its tier layers, the positional hull-stress layer, an in-progress seam-cut TOOL loop, an active lie's SOUND leak, the P1 loop, **and a REACH resolve whose `reach_end_T` is still in the future**) resume after a restore *from published state* at steady level with only a `DECLICK_TICKS` de-click and no attack transient; the exceptions are the tell bed, which fades in over `RESTORE_FADE_TICKS`, and an active lie's leak, which resumes at full leak level on the first frame (the Filter keeps no clean frame, PF-33). **One-shot** cues (onset, beat, release, breach entry, alarm pulse, subside, pre-tell creak, strike, interaction terminals, assembly outcome, `BODY_*`) **never replay** on a restore: the poll primes every last-seen value from the restored state and plays nothing (transitions only, AUD-6, AT-27). Pause holds sim `T`, so every sim-derived sound pauses and UI sounds continue. **Death plays no sound**; the death stub (`restore_reason DEATH_STUB`) is a restore like any other, and no content depends on `restore_reason` (`SL:23`, `SL:36`). Saving plays no sound. *(orchestrator ruling: the REACH resolve is SUSTAINED-with-seek on restore, as the systems-designer proposed: it is a pure function of a published future tick, so dropping it would silence a telegraph the player is owed; replaying a past strike is what is forbidden, and the strike stays one-shot. The sustained part is the non-percussive lead-in only, which ends at `reach_end_T` with no impact, so a restore that seeks into it asserts no strike (AA-28 b; checkpoint ruling 2026-10-26).)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Mechanism** *([UNV]: `docs/engine-reference/godot/modules/audio.md` carries no pause, envelope or voice-pool guidance beyond the pooling pattern; the shape below is the design intent to be checked at the first spike).* Pause is applied by the frame poll setting `stream_paused` on every world-family voice while the kernel state is PAUSED (UI and Voice voices are untouched, and `SceneTree.paused` is not used for audio, so UI sounds continue). **Every world-voice envelope is a pure function of sim `T` written in the frame poll** (`volume_db`, `pitch_scale`): the restore fade, release, tier crossfade, ducking, the slews, the leg and berth beds, the `hal` breathing and the REACH lead-in. **No `Tween`, `Timer` or wall-clock call drives a world voice** (lint L-15, AC-AA-73): a tween would keep running through a pause and would run on frame time, which is the timer AUD-1 forbids.
*Pillar.* P2, P4, P5. *Decided by.* run ruling R18, AT-27, AUD-6, `SL:23`, `SL:36`, PF-33.

**AA-6. The master identity table and the M6 lint.**
*Rule.* Audio owns one identity table of every family. Each asset carries exactly one family token in its name (`sfx_<token>_<context>_<name>_<variant>`) and each family is bound to exactly one bus. The build-time lint, run over `assets/data/audio/tell_audio.json` and the asset directory, fails if: (a) any asset carries two tokens or none; (b) any sound id appears under two tokens; (c) a `hal` asset is bound to a non-`hal` bus or a non-`hal` asset to the `hal` bus; (d) the `wit` token set is not exactly the eight non-NONE LS-14 classes {HISS, DRIP, HUM_DROP, CLANK, WIND, TICK, SIZZLE, CREAK} (the loop variant lives in the `<context>` slot and is not part of the class set, AA-26); (e) any `wit` id exists for NONE or for a WORN descriptor; (f) a `hal` asset carries energy outside the reserved leak signature profile (AA-35) or a non-`hal` asset carries it; (g) any `hal` id is not listed in the Filter's `FILTER_AUDIO_CLASSES`; (h) any `attn`, `hor`, `p1c` or `ann` asset shares a sound id, a source recording or a timbre tag with `wit`, `hal` or each other (so `ann` stays disjoint from `attn`: the klaxon and the breach pulse are two identities on one clock). **Ownership is split once, not shared:** the Filter owns the *id namespace* `FILTER_AUDIO_CLASSES` and the request semantics; Audio owns the assets, mix, bus, the master table and the lint. The `hal` rows cite the Filter's ids by reference; Audio never defines or renames one. This rule is the target that replaces Horror's dangling "AUD-13" and Attention's dangling "AUD-14" cites (CX-A5).
*Why.* M6, AC-HE-71, SN-25, PF-20 ("only if the Filter names the ids can a lint prove disjointness"). One table, one lint, no dual authority over an id. *Pillar.* P1, P3, P4. *Decided by.* M6; PF-20; SN-25; run rulings R4 and R26.

| Token | Family | Bus | Example asset name | Sound identity (adjectives; the sound-designer owns the recipe) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `attn` | Tell, alarm, subside, pre-tell | Tell | `sfx_attn_tell_onset_01` | Unpitched, structure-borne; deep strain, hull knock; never a rising pitch, never a drone |
| `wit` | Graph acoustic classes and annunciator | Witness | `sfx_wit_hiss_loop` | Plant noises of failing hardware: air, water, metal; positional at the descriptor anchor |
| `run` | Running loops, INTEGRITY room tone | Run | `sfx_run_scrubber_loop` | Mid-register machine hum, pump, feed; the room tone is soft and wide, never creaks |
| `ann` | Graph alarm-class annunciator | Witness | `sfx_ann_klaxon_01` | Electromechanical klaxon; regular, on the shared alarm grid; reachable only from a descriptor with `alarm_eligible`; never first warning |
| `hor` | Horror sub-tell | Horror | `sfx_hor_creep_loop` | "Patient, wet and structural, weather with intent"; positional only |
| `suit` | Suit body cues | Body | `sfx_suit_breath_normal_loop` | Close, intimate, hands-free body and equipment sounds |
| `foot` | Footsteps | Foley | `sfx_foot_walk_01` | Gait only |
| `ia` | Interaction | Foley | `sfx_ia_done_01` | Three distinct terminals |
| `asm` | Assembly | Foley | `sfx_asm_bead_settle_01` | The weld bead settle is "it is done" |
| `body`, `pod` | Ship-body and pod events | Foley | `sfx_body_dock_contact_01` | Thuds and clunks, not Attention stimuli |
| `p1c` | P1 proximity cue | Body | `sfx_p1c_suit_irregular_loop` | Plausible regulator irregularity, not a sting |
| `hal` | Hallucination (Filter ids) | Hallucination | `sfx_hal_fa_lie_sig_gauge_01` | Doubt, not horror: wrong, warm, fringed, over-reaching tail |
| `amb`, `ui`, `vox`, `mus` | Ambience, UI, voice, music | Ambience, UI, Voice, Music | none at MVP for `mus` | per family |

**AA-7. The MVP slice table (the scope guard in rule form).**

| Priority | Content | Slice |
|---|---|---|
| P0 | the full `attn` set: onset carrying beat 0, beats, bed, tier layers, positional creak at the locus, release, breach entry, alarm cadence with fallback, subside, pre-tell creaks | MVP, ships before anything else |
| P1 | eight witness classes at anchors with `alarm_eligible` gating; four running timbres plus the INTEGRITY room tone; footsteps walk, run, carry; the class-(b) reading-lie signature; Horror's four cue kinds for `horror_t01`; the three Suit body cues ruled into MVP (breath cadence steps, the reserve valve click, the visor-fog step; D92) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | MVP |
| P2 | the remaining Suit body cues *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; the interaction cue set; assembly outcome cues; pod hop events; the berth bed and the berth contact and undock cues (AA-33 (g)); minimal UI | MVP, **cut-first at MVP (pillar proximity test)** |
| Wired, assets VS | `BODY_DOCK_CONTACT`, `BODY_HULL_SEPARATION`, `BODY_TRANSFER`; the pod leg bed (`LEG_BED_FADE_TICKS`; the berth bed is MVP, AA-33 (g)) | VS |
| VS / Alpha | music; material-dependent footsteps; phantom movement (class a); the P1 cue live (VS-B); Voice bus use (VS-B); caption and settings UI (Alpha); ring-shifted ambience; voice-pool polish | later |

*Rule.* Nothing above the line may be cut to pay for anything below it. **P2 is the cut-first row at MVP** (CD-GDD-ALIGN, solo-developer risk CD-4): it carries no pillar, unlike P0 (P2, P1) and P1 (P1, P4), so if the schedule slips it may be cut back to placeholder one-shots (one short generic cue per event, no per-surface or per-item variants, no berth bed) without a design change; P0 is protected and P1 is accepted as MVP because the locked LS-14 consequence channel, HR-5 with `MODE_DROP_PM` 0 and Horror's acceptance criteria require it. This tag is a logged deviation from the routed brief ('the tells Pillar 2 needs; mark the rest VS/Alpha'), accepted for P1 only. "Final fidelity" is never the MVP bar: the bar is that the vocabulary is **distinct and true**. Asset volume is the solo-developer risk, so MVP families reuse a small set of base samples with parametrised pitch, rate and level (AA-24, AA-26) and defer variants. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The MVP `wit` asset count is capped at `WIT_MVP_ASSET_CAP` (`[PLACEHOLDER]` 16: the eight class samples plus at most eight loop-variant samples, chosen from the HR-9 pairs that AC-AA-126 tests); every other variant is a parametrised pitch, rate or level of a base sample. P2 Suit, interaction, assembly and pod cues may be placeholders at MVP, and their blind-identification criterion (AC-AA-102) is excluded from the MVP gate while placeholders are in use. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* **D92 (user ruling): all three body cues (breath cadence steps, the reserve valve click, the visor-fog step) ship at MVP.** They are P1, are not cut-first and may not be placeholders at the kill/continue playtest, which runs a muted arm and a sound arm, each judged on its own pass criterion (the protocol is HUD's, HS-DT-1 and OQ-HS-67); the other Suit cues stay P2. *Pillar.* P2. *Decided by.* `CON:395`, `CON:479-480`; routed thread T8.

**AA-9. Listener and positional model.**
*Rule.* The listener is the local player's avatar position, never the camera, its lead or its zoom. Positional attenuation and panning are functions of world-px distance and are identical at every step of the integer zoom ladder (k in [1, k_auto+2]) and at every camera lead. Sources on a moving body stay attached to their ship-local anchors, composed each frame through `section_offset` and the body transform. Cue requests are pure functions of published state (AA-3). **Gain is always the manual D.9 value, written per voice as `volume_db` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)***: the built-in `AudioStreamPlayer2D` attenuation cannot express D.9 (a `NEAR_PX` plateau, a linear dB-per-tile slope, door and hull terms), so if 2D players are used their built-in attenuation is disabled and only pan is left to the engine. The spike (OQ-AA-1, AC-AA-81) therefore compares **pan mechanisms only**: `AudioStreamPlayer2D` with an `AudioListener2D` on the avatar *([UNV] inside the 640x360 SubViewport)* against a manual pan, and the manual pan is the named fallback (-> ADR candidate). **Localisation is horizontal pan plus level**; the y position of a source is conveyed by level alone, and whether a vertical cue is added is the residual of the range ruling (OQ-AA-43; the ruling of OQ-AA-32 (b) did not add one, so until it does localisation is pan plus level). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* The non-positional tell bed does not depend on the spike, which is a further reason the bed is contractual. 
*Pillar.* P2. *Decided by.* PIPE (the camera has no audio role, integer ladder); `AT:989`, `AT:1278`; HIGH [UNV] SubViewport listener; reflexion pattern 3.

**AA-10. Transmission through geometry: it attenuates, it never culls a witness, and an unknown door is open.**
*Rule.* Airborne sources are attenuated by distance and by the closed doors and hull boundaries between the listener's room (`room_at`) and the source's room (D.9). Where the Graph publishes a door's true opening state the attenuation follows it continuously (a half-open door softens less than a shut one); **until that read exists an unknown door state is treated as open**, never closed (`opening(edge_id)`, Layout's ask, CX-A4). Transmission attenuates; it never removes a `wit` source with `sev >= 1` that is inside its class audible radius (a per-class floor, `WIT_AUDIBLE_FLOOR_DB`). Structure-borne sources (the positional hull-stress layer, the seam-cut TOOL loop, Horror's ingress and egress creak) ignore door penalties and take a reduced hull penalty; the tell bed is non-positional and exempt from all of it (AA-16) (engine note: no `Area2D` bus-override zones; see Engine feasibility notes).
*Why.* A guessed muffle that hides a real hiss is a suppression by default: it breaks P1 and the `LSG:1110` lint. The seam-cut loop is the far-side victim's tell (ASK AU1, `MSA:1071`). The constants and the formula are Formulas (D.9) and Tuning Knobs.
*Pillar.* P1, P2. *Decided by.* P1; `LSG:1110`; AUD-4; BS-4 (listening is a stance). *(orchestrator ruling: this reverses the brief's interim default "closed unless known", per ruling R10.)*

**AA-11. Mix priority tiers and ducking.**
*Rule.* Five tiers, Attention's M3 verbatim: **(1)** Graph consequence cues (witness and running) and the player's own footsteps; **(2)** tell beats and the breach alarm; **(3)** the positional hull-stress layer; **(4)** ambient and pre-tell cues; **(5)** music. This GDD places the families M3 does not name *without reordering it*: Suit body cues are tier 1 (consequence channel, `SE:1222`); the tell bed stems and Horror's strike and REACH-resolve cues are tier 2 (the strike cue is the witness of strike one, `HE:1285`); Horror's creep and ingress/egress creak, the `hal` leak and the P1 cue are tier 3; foley, interaction, assembly, body events and ambience are tier 4; UI and Voice sit outside the world mix. **Ducking:** a tier-4 or lower-importance bed (ambience, the TOOL channel loops **except the seam-cut TOOL loop, which is never ducked**, the pod leg bed, the berth bed) ducks under a tier-2 impulse cue (a beat, a strike, a breach pulse) by `DUCK_DB` for a short envelope. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Ducking is applied per voice** (the frame poll writes the victim's `volume_db`, D.18), never by a bus effect or a sidechain: the Foley bus carries tier-1 footfalls beside tier-4 interaction and assembly cues, so a bus-level duck would duck a tier-1 cue (L-8). **The tell family, the `hal` leak, Horror's cues, the seam-cut TOOL loop (the far-side victim's tell, AA-33) and every tier-1 cue are never ducked**, and **no compressor, limiter or sidechain may attenuate a tier-1 cue**. Beats never duck under music (music is empty at MVP). The alarm family's duty is capped at `ALARM_DUTY_MAX_PM` of each sweep (D.2). Hallucination cues never duck, delay or mask a true cue (AA-37). *(orchestrator ruling: ducking reaches tiers >= 4 only, the systems-designer's scope, not the audio-director's wider "beds including the hull-stress layer"; a duck on the positional hull-stress layer, a Horror creep or a lie's leak would mask a truth or leak channel, and M3's "beds" is read as the tier-4 beds.)*
*Pillar.* P1, P2. *Decided by.* M3, HE-37, SE-29, SN L-A.

**AA-12. Voice budget, reserved impulse voices, stealing order.**
*Rule.* A fixed pool of `VOICE_POOL_SIZE` voices (`[PLACEHOLDER]` 32, `[UNV]`). `RESERVED_TELL_IMPULSE_VOICES` of them (`[PLACEHOLDER]` 2; floor 1, which is M5's literal "one reserved voice") are reserved for tell and alarm one-shots (onset carrying beat 0, beats, breach entry, alarm pulses, release, subside) and are never lent to another family. The reserve is 2 because a beat's tail can outlast the next beat's start and the breach-entry event overlaps the last beat's tail: `ceil(BEAT_TAIL_MAX_TICKS / BEAT_MIN_GAP_TICKS)` (D.10); M5's "one" is therefore read as a floor. The **tell bed is a persistent looping stream outside the pool (pool-exempt)**, so a full pool can never drop the bed (CX-A12); the **INTEGRITY room tone** is likewise one pool-exempt stream per listener, so a consequence-channel sound cannot be stolen (D.7, D.10). When no voice is free, a request steals from the playing voice of the highest tier number that is strictly lower in importance than the request, then the quietest at the listener, then the oldest; a request that finds no victim is dropped; tier-1 and tier-2 voices are never stolen by tier-3-to-5 requests; the tell family and `hal` voices are PROTECTED, and **the seam-cut TOOL loop is not stealable by tier >= 4 requests**. **A presented lie's SOUND leak is never stolen while the number of lies is within `LIES_MAX`** (Sanity's registered constant, cited by name): a lie that is visually presented but has lost its leak would be unfalsifiable by sound. Pre-tell creaks and foley drop first. A witness is never culled for voice count while any tier >= 2 voice is stealable; a tier-1 steal raises a DIAGNOSTIC counter whose non-zero value in a CI soak is a bug (the audibility cull must trigger before the count cull). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* `WIT_VOICE_CAP` counts voices, each descriptor costing `V_wit` (D.10); among tier-1 voices the victim is the lowest `sev`, then the quietest at the listener; every drop raises a named DIAGNOSTIC; the pool is sized from a measured worst-case scene, not from the sum of the caps. *(orchestrator ruling: `VOICE_POOL_SIZE` stays 32 `[UNV]` as ruling R19 fixed it; the systems-designer's derivation (plausible worst-case demand 37, tiers 1 to 3 alone about 31) is carried to Formulas D.10 as the reason the range is 32 to 64 and to OQ-AA-9 as a recommendation of 48. It is not written as the value. `RESERVED_TELL_IMPULSE_VOICES` 2 is an addition to run ruling R19's list, a refinement of M5 confirmed at the 2026-10-26 checkpoint (OQ-AA-8 closed).)*
*Pillar.* P2, P4. *Decided by.* M3, M5; `LIES_MAX`; routed thread T6.

**AA-13. Audio first: by construction, not by latency.**
*Rule.* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* "Audio no later than visual" (HR-9, AUD-2, AC-AT-24) is a **call-order guarantee plus a measured offset budget**, never an absolute latency. (a) *Call order:* the request for a fact is created, and the engine `play()` is called, in the frame poll (AA-4) of the frame in which the fact's notification arrived, and that poll is ordered before the presenter's draw of the same frame. Beat 0 is published in the `attention.step` run that enters TELL, and the presenter draws nothing for that epoch before it. `HE:1285`'s "strike_T to the positional strike cue starting within 1 tick (17 ms)" is the same ordering guarantee (the strike request is created in the frame poll of the frame whose notification published `strike_T`, positioned to start at `strike_T`; a catch-up frame can carry the fact later than one tick of wall time, so the guarantee is the order and the audible offset is the budget, OQ-AA-37). (b) *Offset budget:* the engine cannot observe the delay from `play()` to the DAC (device buffer, the Windows audio stack), so no rule states an absolute figure. The measured quantity is the **audio-versus-screen delta** of a cue and its visual twin on one clock (AC-AA-6: a loopback capture of the audio output beside a capture of the screen). An optional constant `AUDIO_LEAD_MS` (`[PLACEHOLDER]` 0, range 0 to 100) is the hold a presenter may apply uniformly to all its visual twins so that the measured delta stays non-negative at p95 on the reference machine; it is set only from that measurement, is global and never per cue (M-2), and its presenters are Attention's and Horror's, so it is a routed ask and not an edit here.
*Pillar.* P2. *Decided by.* AUD-2; `HE:1285`; KRN CR-10.

#### B. The tell layer (`attn`): Pillar 2's audio spine

*Attention owns the phases, the schedule, the tiers, the abort and breach machine, the beacon and TellAudit.* The rules below say only what Audio does on each *observed* delta of `attention.view`. Beat times, tier thresholds, the abort rule and every Attention value are cited by name (`beat_schedule`, `ambient_cue_step`, `tell_window_contract`) and never restated.

**AA-14. The onset carries beat 0.**
*Rule.* On `phase` CALM to TELL (a new `tell_epoch` with `phase == TELL`) the onset cue plays on a reserved impulse voice. Beat 0 is published in the same run and is **carried by the onset asset's own attack**: one voice, one event, no second beat-0 sound. The onset is the only `attn` event with a long strain tail and it is unpitched. It begins no later than the first filament (AUD-2). The pre-tell creak recurrence stops at TELL entry (AA-22): no new creak starts, and one already in flight finishes rather than being cut, because a cut at onset would be an audible anomaly ahead of beat 0.
*Why.* Onset and beat 0 occur at the same tick; two sounds cost two voices and read as a double. TellAudit counts beat 0, so the onset counts as the audible beat 0 (five beats in the floor window at jitter 0).
*Pillar.* P2. *Decided by.* AUD-1, AUD-2, AUD-3, AUD-5; AT-9.

**AA-15. Beats: one unpitched hull knock per published beat.**
*Rule.* For each `(tell_epoch, tell_beat)` strictly greater than last-seen there is exactly one beat cue (never more than one per poll). The beat is a hull knock or creak, **unpitched**, drawn from a variant set by a presentation hash of `(tell_epoch, tell_beat)` so no two consecutive beats are identical; its authored loudness does not change with tell progress or beat index (the shrinking interval of `beat_schedule` *is* the escalation: there is no rising pitch and no louder-as-closer). Every legal un-aborted `(tell_len, seed)` publishes at least `MIN_BEATS_IN_FLOOR` beats in the first `TELL_MIN_TICKS`, and Audio plays each of them: **no beat is suppressed before `breach_due_T`**, and Audio never drops a published beat for a pool or ducking reason because the impulse voices are reserved (AA-12). The audible count equals the published count only for frame times under about 500 ms (a hitch can coalesce beats, AA-4); the audible half of AC-AT-47 is therefore captured at nominal frame rate.
*Pillar.* P2, P3. *Decided by.* AUD-3, AUD-5, AUD-8; `beat_schedule` (F-12).

**AA-16. The structure-borne bed: non-positional, every living player, with a measured margin.**
*Rule.* While `phase == TELL` a low structure-borne bed plays for **every living player** on a pool-exempt stream. It is never attenuated by distance to the locus, by transmission (AA-10), by zoom, by camera, by the listener's room, by pod transit or by reduce-flashing or reduce-motion. It is at least `TELL_AUDIBILITY_MARGIN_DB` above the loudest concurrent non-tell bed in the worst allowed concurrent state (the running loops at their per-source ceiling and count cap, a TOOL-class channel loop, the ambience and Suit fan, the INTEGRITY room tone, and up to `WIT_VOICE_CAP` sustained witness classes counted through their in-band energy only, D.6), compared **per band**, for the low structure-borne band and for an upper-partial edge layer that keeps the bed audible on small speakers (D.6, AA-45). The margin is verified at tier 1 (the quietest bed state) by a listening test with a retained capture; it is not headless-testable (M2). Mono downmix keeps the bed. The bed contains no tone that moves with tell progress: it is textural and its only changes are the discrete tier layers (AA-18). The bed exists only while `phase == TELL`: it fades out over `BED_END_TICKS` from the TELL to BREACH tick (AA-20, D.5) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* and decays on abort (AA-19).
*Why.* AT-15 and AUD-4(b): "perceivable" is defined relative to every living player; the bed is what makes a tell at an unoccupied lit derelict fair. "Non-critical bed" in M2 is read conservatively as every concurrent bed that is not the tell family, including tier-1 running loops, which are bounded by a per-source ceiling and a count cap rather than a limiter because AA-11 forbids attenuating tier 1 (conflict CX-A11).
*Pillar.* P2. *Decided by.* AUD-4(b), M2, AT-15, run ruling R24.

**AA-17. The positional hull-stress layer at the locus.**
*Rule.* A positional creak layer plays anchored at the hull anchor of `locus_section_id` (tier 3, structure-borne transmission, AA-10). The hull anchor is derived by Audio (D.16): the centroid of the lowest-id HULL_SEGMENT plate set of that section composed with `section_offset` `(provisional — Layout has no hull_anchor(section_id) read; ask routed, CX-A4)`. Attention publishes no coordinates and none are invented. The layer is additional evidence only: the tell contract is met by the bed and the beats without it. It is gated by `tier`: absent at tier 1, present at tiers 2 and 3 (AA-18). *(This is the one place where the tell is positional; it is the locus section the Attention contract already makes public, and its loudness steps only with `tier`, so it localises the section without becoming a continuous meter. Pre-tell creaks, in contrast, are never placed at the locus, AA-22.)*
*Pillar.* P2. *Decided by.* AUD-4(a), `LY:262-266`.

**AA-18. Tier layering is discrete.**
*Rule.* `tier = 0` outside TELL, otherwise 1 to 3 (Attention's definition; `TIER2_TICKS` and `TIER3_TICKS` cited, measured on Attention's escalation clock, which freezes while `L < theta_HOLD`). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* Bed layers change only at tier steps, crossfaded over `TIER_XFADE_TICKS`: tier 1 is the base bed; tier 2 adds the mid layer and the positional hull-stress layer; tier 3 adds the upper layer. The increase in bed loudness per tier step is `TIER_STEP_DB`. **No audible parameter varies continuously with `tell_elapsed` or with the escalation clock.** The bed margin (AA-16) is verified at tier 1. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* **Frozen escalation (X4; OQ-AA-34, resolved).** Audio reads `tier`, `(tell_epoch, tell_beat)` and `tell_beat_T` as Attention publishes them and computes no tier or beat time of its own. A player who goes still keeps the tier already reached: no step up while the escalation is frozen and no step down either (a published tier never falls inside a TELL; the next audible change is the release, AA-19). A player who keeps moving hears tier 2 and tier 3 arrive on the escalation clock. The freeze has no cue of its own: it is heard as the published escalation holding (the tier layers unchanged, and the beat interval as Attention's re-derived F-12 defines it). It is keyed to the player's behaviour, not to a hidden meter, and the tiers stay three discrete steps, which is why it is not a readout by ear. This GDD's earlier recommendation to reject freezing is superseded by the ruling (creative-director call, coordination rule 3).
*Pillar.* P2, P3. *Decided by.* V5, AUD-8, AT-26.

**AA-19. The abort: the web loses interest (release without reward).**
*Rule.* On TELL to CALM with `tell_outcome == ABORTED` the beats stop and the bed decays over `RELEASE_TICKS` (D.3), with a release cue whose spectral identity is distinct from subside's: an exhale or settling relaxation, with no sting, no reward chime and no "success" sound (relief without reward). The constraint is registered as one formula, `release_cue_relation`: `RELEASE_TICKS < ABORT_HOLD_TICKS` **and** `STRAND_RELEASE_TICKS == RELEASE_TICKS` (Attention owns `STRAND_RELEASE_TICKS`; equality is the requirement); a false relation at data load is a boot error. `TELL_REARM_TICKS` guarantees the release finishes long before any new onset. No beacon and no STRESS on an abort, so no breach cue. A restore into CALM with `tell_outcome == ABORTED` plays nothing (AA-5). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* The release plays from whatever bed level the frozen escalation had reached (`L_bed0`, D.3): for a player who goes still before the escalation clock reaches `TIER2_TICKS` that is the tier-1 level, and no tier-2 layer and no hull-stress layer is heard first (X4; AC-AA-12, AC-AA-150).
*Why.* The player must hear the web lose interest or restraint is never learned (AUD-6, P3).
*Pillar.* P3, P2. *Decided by.* AUD-6; `AT:1150`, `AT:1258`; reflexion pattern 2.

**AA-20. Breach entry and the alarm cadence (the alarm-versus-`alarm_eligible` ruling, written into the live clause).**
*Rule.* On TELL to BREACH (or CALM to BREACH after a hitch that skipped TELL): (a) the **breach-entry event** plays on a reserved impulse voice at the entry notification, at `breach_enter_T`, and **is pulse 0 of the alarm cadence**, always emitted and exempt from the minimum gap by identity, not by tick order (d); its loudness step relative to the last tell beat is bounded by `BREACH_ENTRY_STEP_LU_MAX` (D.5): the tell precedes it, so it must not become a jump scare; (b) later pulses follow **one per `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS`** (M1), each sounding for at most `ALARM_DUTY_MAX_PM` of the sweep (D.2); (c) the pulse clock has two branches. **Aligned:** while `beacon_live` (`exists e in emitters() with e.kind == BEACON and e.body_id == the home-ship body and e.output_pm > 0`, EM-27, debounced by `BEACON_LIVE_HOLD_TICKS`, D.2) and the listener is on the home-ship's body, pulses start at the beam's sweep boundary, `T mod BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS == 0` (`beacon_aim` is a pure function of absolute `T`, so the beam returns to its authored phase exactly there, independent of `breach_enter_T`). **Fallback:** otherwise (listener on a derelict, home-ship `NOT_LIVE`, beacon absent or not yet commanded), pulses start at `(T - breach_enter_T) mod BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS == 0`. Reduce-flashing never changes either branch; (d) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **one precedence rule, by identity (D.2)**: pulse 0 always emits and sets `last_alarm_T`; at most one `attn` pulse and at most one `ann` pulse may start on a tick (a same-tick pair is one rhythm with two stacked timbres, which is the normal case inside BREACH because both identities follow one `hit(T)`); every other pulse needs a gap of at least `ALARM_MIN_GAP_TICKS` (`BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS div 2`) against the latest alarm-family start on an *earlier* tick, so same-tick starters pass or fail together and pulse 0 is never suppressed by a gap against itself. The branch is re-evaluated **only at a pulse boundary** (the first choice is made at `breach_enter_T + BEACON_LIVE_HOLD_TICKS + 2`, after the beacon that is commanded at T+1 can be read), so a branch switch (the listener changes body mid-breach) or an aligned boundary landing soon after the entry event cannot double-sting inside a second, and the gap after a switch stays within `[ALARM_MIN_GAP_TICKS, 3 * ALARM_MIN_GAP_TICKS - 1]` ticks (64 to 191 at the registered sweep). **At entry and exit:** an `ann` pulse that started 1 to `ALARM_MIN_GAP_TICKS - 1` ticks before the entry finishes (no cut, as E-AA-17) and overlaps pulse 0 for at most `W - 1` ticks, and pulse 0 still emits; at BREACH exit the `ann` returns to the absolute grid and its first pulse obeys the same gap test, so its phase jumps at entry and at exit are each bounded to 64 to 191 ticks; (e) this event and cadence belong to the `attn` family, a tell-contract event that is **never "the first warning"** because a tell of at least `TELL_MIN_TICKS` precedes it by construction, so it is **exempt from the `alarm_eligible` gate** that governs the Graph's alarm-class annunciator (AA-27); the exemption is the conflict CX-A1 and its wording refresh is routed to the Graph owner; (f) a lie never uses either identity (L-A, AUD-7); (g) audio carries the breach **alone** on a derelict (AUD-10), since the derelicts have no beacon. The tell bed fades out over `BED_END_TICKS` from the entry tick (no hard stop, so no click; D.5) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; the entry event's own tail (at most `BREACH_ENTRY_TAIL_MAX_TICKS`, lint L-14) carries the transition.
*Why.* Gating the siren on a `sev >= 3` descriptor would leave a breach with no such descriptor yet silent and, on a derelict with no beacon, inaudible: a P2 violation. A breach with no siren fails reduce-flashing players and derelict listeners. *(orchestrator ruling: the two specialist drafts agreed on the aligned phase `T mod BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS` and the fallback `(T - breach_enter_T) mod BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS`; the audio-director's draft let the first aligned pulse follow the entry event by 1 to 128 ticks, the systems-designer's added the minimum-gap suppression. The minimum-gap rule is adopted because M1 says "one pulse per sweep", which an entry event plus an aligned pulse a few ticks later would break. The aligned form needs no `beacon_phase` read, so the systems-designer's gap GAP-E1 does not arise; "beacon live" is read from the Emitter's `emitters()` view, `(provisional — Emitter read of a live BEACON, CX-A15)`.)*
*Pillar.* P2. *Decided by.* AT-18, M1, M4, AUD-10, `LSG:976` (wording refresh owed, CX-A1); `beacon_aim` (EM-16).

**AA-21. Subside is not an all-clear.**
*Rule.* On BREACH to SUBSIDING the alarm cadence stops and **one short collapse** plays ("a short collapse, then silence", V10): no "danger over" cue, no relief sound. `phase == CALM` is **never** mapped to "all clear": no sound, music, ambience or mix change signals safety on entering CALM, and the Graph's failing-loop sounds (`wit`) carry the ongoing wrongness because STRESS can outlive SUBSIDING (`AT:1210`). Pre-tell creaks resume only by their own floor rule (AA-22). Audio reads `phase`, never `breach_phase`.
*Pillar.* P2, P3. *Decided by.* AUD-10, V10, `AT:1210`; AC-AT-67.

**AA-22. Pre-tell ambient creaks: stepped, floor-gated, in the listener's own section, never a meter.**
*Rule.* In `phase == CALM` only, ordinary hull-settling creaks (event ids distinct from tell beats, excluded from TellAudit and from the floor-window count) play at a density selected by the **quantised cue step** of Attention's `ambient_cue_step` (floor `AMBIENT_CUE_FLOOR_PM` 800 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)*, `CUE_STEPS` 4, `CUE_HYST_PM` 25, `CUE_MARGIN_PM` 100; cited, never restated). Step 0 (below the floor) plays none. Steps 1 to `CUE_STEPS` use a four-row table of mean spacing and minimum gap (`CREAK_MEAN_TICKS` `[PLACEHOLDER]` 1800 / 1200 / 780 / 480 ticks, strictly decreasing, each step at least 1.3 times denser than the last; `CREAK_MIN_GAP_TICKS` `[PLACEHOLDER]` 600 / 480 / 360 / 240, each below its mean). **Recurrence is a stateless predicate of published state, never a countdown** (D.13): on each sim tick since the previous poll a creak is *eligible* when `phase == CALM`, the step is at least 1, `T - last_creak_T >= CREAK_MIN_GAP_TICKS[step]` and the shared integer presentation hash of `(T, HASH_SEED, step)` modulo `CREAK_MEAN_TICKS[step] - CREAK_MIN_GAP_TICKS[step]` is zero; at most one creak starts per poll (no catch-up), and evaluating every elapsed tick keeps the density independent of the frame rate. The scan span is **primed to the current `T` on a restore and on a resume from pause** (no backlog scan) and is capped at `CREAK_SCAN_MAX_TICKS` (`[PLACEHOLDER]` 60) ticks per poll (D.13), so a long hitch is a bounded loop, never a catch-up. The hash is the shared presentation hash (`HASH_SEED`, Attention, AT-28; the hash is shared with Lighting, LO-19), **never `rng_derive`** (a sim stream). **Position:** each creak is positional at a plate of **the listener's own section**, chosen by the hash, so a creak never points at the locus and is never louder, closer or pitched by step (a locus-anchored pre-tell creak would localise the future tell and make the step a distance meter by ear). A creak in flight at onset finishes. Pre-tell creaks stop at TELL entry and never play in BREACH or SUBSIDING.
