Four discrete densities, constant within a step: no continuous mapping of `level_pm` to any audible parameter, no pitched tone, no drone (AT-26). Creaks are a different timbre from tell beats (short, small, single-plate settle versus a deep structure-borne knock; lint L-10). `level_pm` appears in Audio only as the argument of `ambient_cue_step`. This rule is the pre-tell cue rule that replaces Attention's dangling "AUD-14" (CX-A5). *(orchestrator ruling: the systems-designer's stateless-hash form is adopted over the audio-director's per-creak sampled delay, because AUD-1 says "never from a timer in Audio" and a sampled delay is a countdown; the audio-director's means (1440 / 960 / 660 / 480) are superseded. The fallback, were AUD-1 read as forbidding even a stateless recurrence, is one creak per step entry plus one per CALM-minute window; OQ-AA-7 was resolved at the 2026-10-26 checkpoint in favour of the predicate. Creaks are deliberately not suppressed in the ticks before a possible onset: a suppression would itself predict the tell.)*
*Why.* AUD-8, AT-26, F-24: a four-step table is the most the guard rail allows, and it is floor-gated so the step-1 cue sits above the lamp's walk-beam reading.
*Pillar.* P3. *Decided by.* AUD-1, AUD-8, AT-26, F-24 (`ambient_cue_step`).

**AA-23. No false calm, no phantom tell, no held breath, no music before beat 0.**
*Rule.* The Filter and Sanity can never filter, delay, mute, double or synthesise tell or breach audio (AUD-7; AT-17). No cue is suppressed during a real tell at any tier, stage or setting; no tell sound is ever played without a published tell. The "held breath" silence before breach is rejected (it would reduce evidence inside the floor window). A caption for tell beats is rejected (AA-41). Adaptive music must not telegraph the tell earlier than beat 0 (there is no music at MVP, AA-40).
*Pillar.* P2, P3, P4. *Decided by.* AUD-7, AUD-8, `AT:1223`, AT-17.

#### C. The witness and running layers (`wit`, `run`): consequence-channel true-state sound

**AA-24. The eight witness classes, played at the descriptor's anchor from the descriptor's true fields.**
*Rule.* For each live descriptor with `medium != NONE`, the Graph's `acoustic` class is played at the descriptor `anchor` (Layout anchor geometry composed with `section_offset`: the same source as the trail) with level and class parameter driven by the true `intensity_pm` (0 to 1000), and `dir` where the class has one. The class set is **exactly the LS-14 non-NONE ids: HISS 1, DRIP 2, HUM_DROP 3, CLANK 4, WIND 5, TICK 6, SIZZLE 7, CREAK 8 (a strained seal, SEAL_STRAIN).** **There is no WHISTLE class and no "loop whisper" class and none is invented** (CX-A2): "hiss, creak and whistle" in `LSG:44` and BS-4, and the routed phrase "hiss, hum, tick, whistle, creak, loop whisper", are read as prose for the enum, with WIND the only candidate for "whistle" and the running-loop sound (AA-26) for "loop whisper", pending the Graph owner's confirmation. The class parameter mapping is behaviour, not DSP (D.8): HISS level and pitch with flow; DRIP rate; HUM_DROP pitch sag with under-service; CLANK an onset one-shot at the descriptor's first step, with no recurrence; WIND level, stereo from `dir`; TICK rate (the flow-zero hull pre-stage TICK is the only cue at zoom k = 1, where the 2 px hairline does not resolve, `FT:1381`; **the flow-zero hull pre-stage TICK is driven by `sev` and `since_steps`, not `iota`**); SIZZLE level; CREAK rate and level. The request depends on the descriptor, **never on light**: a hiss in an unlit compartment, with zero lit emitters, plays at full true level (`FT:207`, E23: expected, not a bug). Audio may lead visuals (FT-35). The class stops when the descriptor stops, with no completion cue ("the reward is the absence", FT E7). The audio position equals the trail source position, both from Layout (FT AC-137). Continuous mapping from Graph **true values** is permitted and desirable (P1); the "no continuous mapping" ban is on `level_pm` only.
*Pillar.* P1. *Decided by.* `LSG:163-168`, `LSG:976`, FT-35, `FT:843-870` (E1 to E15), run ruling R5.

**AA-25. Witnesses are never suppressed, never added; WORN is silent; an alarm class needs `alarm_eligible`.**
*Rule.* No hallucination, sanity stage, ducking, voice stealing (except the announced, deterministic last resort of D.10, which a CI soak of the worst-case scene must never reach) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*, compressor, setting or geometry removes or alters a witness sound whose descriptor has `sev >= 1` and `intensity_pm >= 1` within its audible radius; no hallucination adds a hiss, creak or any `wit`-class sound. Two anti-suppression guarantees make that checkable: a **source-gain floor** for any `sev >= 1` descriptor with `intensity_pm >= 1` at zero distance (`G_SEV1_FLOOR_DB`) and a **post-attenuation audible floor** for a source inside its class radius (`WIT_AUDIBLE_FLOOR_DB`, AA-10), related by the two-clause floors relation that a data load must satisfy (D.8) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. A **flow-fraction** descriptor with `intensity_pm == 0` is silent: that is the **owner's own semantics** (`LSG:752`: a valve closed upstream leaves the LEAK descriptor and its `hp` but sets `intensity_pm` to 0, "the drip stops"), not a suppression, and the `LSG:1110` lint's wording is read with that carve-out. The carve-out does **not** extend to the mode-5 STRUCT pre-stage or to FT-30's nine ids without an `intensity_pm` (SHORT, OVERLOAD, OVERHEAT, SCRAM, FREEZE, ATMO_COLD/HOT/THIN/DECOMP): those have no `iota`, are driven by `sev` (D.8), and are never silent while `sev >= 1` and `medium != NONE`. The same holds for the SEAL_STRAIN HEAT/TICK branch (`LSG:649`): its `intensity_pm` measures |dP|, not its thermal cause, so it is driven by `sev` and never silenced at `intensity_pm` 0. A **WORN** descriptor (`medium NONE`, `acoustic NONE`) is on the module-body channel and **no sound is ever played for it** (`LSG:1111`). The hum and the running "whisper" of a machine are true-state per `LSG:976` (AA-26) and carry the same no-remove, no-add rule. Alarm-class presentation in the Graph's sense (AA-27) plays only when `alarm_eligible = (sev >= ALARM_SEV_MIN) AND gate_open`, never as the first warning. Lint (`LSG:1110`): none suppresses a `medium != NONE` descriptor with `sev >= 1` from its acoustic channel; none plays an alarm class without `alarm_eligible`; Attention's breach event is exempt (AA-20). Alarm-grid snapping applies only to the alarm-class annunciator and the `attn` breach pulse, **never to a descriptor's witness class**, which starts on the poll that sees the descriptor.
*Pillar.* P1. *Decided by.* run ruling R5; `LSG:1110`, `LSG:1111`, `LSG:752`.

**AA-26. Running-loop sounds: one timbre per loop, read from `served_pm`; INTEGRITY is a room tone.**
*Rule.* One distinct running timbre each for O2, POWER, COOLANT and FUEL, driven by the true `served_pm` and node `state` of the published `node()` read (D.7), started and stopped by the `N/<id>` and `L/<loop>/<anchor>` dirty keys and polled at render rate for smooth values (`LSG:201`; the Graph hooks publish at divisor 6, so Audio slews over a short window). Level rises with `served_pm` between a per-source floor and a per-source ceiling; pitch sags with under-service. A node with `state` FAILED, DESTROYED, SALVAGED or ABSENT is silent; DEGRADED plays the running timbre at its true reduced `served_pm` while the descriptor's acoustic class (AA-24) plays the failing variant (HR-9: every loop has a distinct running sound and a distinct failing sound). **INTEGRITY is static** (`LSG:66`): its running sound is the room tone of a pressurised, intact hull (airborne, thinning with `compartment(cid).band_p` (NORMAL, LOW, DECOMP, VACUUM; LS-7; LS-19), woken by the `B/<cid>` key, and silent in VACUUM; **it must not be creaks**, because creaks are already spent on four other families (Attention's pre-tell settling and tell beats, the Graph's CREAK witness for SEAL_STRAIN, Horror's ingress/egress creak) and the identity table keeps all four apart (CX-A10 clarification). Concurrent audible running loops are limited by a **per-source level ceiling** and a **count cap** (`RUN_MAX_VOICES` loudest at the listener after attenuation, with a switching margin and fades, D.7), not by a limiter, so their sum stays below the tell bed (CX-A11, AA-16). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The cap silences a true-state loop for a reason of count, which AA-25 otherwise forbids, so the set is ranked with a `near` priority (a loop within `NEAR_PX` is never displaced by a farther one), the drop is announced (`run_cap_drop`), and the level floor of a stalled machine is set by D.19 R-b so that it stays audible over the room tone. The hum of a running machine is true-state audio in the consequence channel under `LSG:976` but not in `LSG:44`'s enumerated list, and Boarding uses it as true-state corroboration, not the falsifier of record until the Graph rules (C-BS-4); **this GDD states the owner's reading (`LSG:976` wins over a consumer's wording) and Boarding has applied the refresh (`BS:1269` row 20; RF-6 closed); whether a machine's hum is a *witness of record* for the closed list in `LSG:168` remains the Graph's call (CX-A2)**. Audio uses `served_pm` from the published `node()` read; the `duty_pm` ask stays Emitter's (EM-14). Node lanterns hum while their machine runs; light itself is silent (`LO:429`). *(orchestrator ruling: the POWER hum's fundamental is at or above 160 Hz, the systems-designer's band plan, not the audio-director's "50-ish Hz" character, so that it does not sit in the tell bed's band and break the M2 margin; the transformer character is carried by harmonics.)*

| Loop | Running timbre (identity) | Failing class (descriptor acoustic) | Confusable pair and the distinguishing audio property |
|---|---|---|---|
| O2 | Soft air-handler whoosh with slow fan flutter; airy, broadband, mid-high | HISS, WIND (breach or leak), HUM_DROP (at the scrubber) | O2 vs COOLANT: O2 is airy and broadband; COOLANT is liquid and tonal |
| POWER | Transformer-like steady hum, tonal, fundamental at or above the reserved low-band edge | HUM_DROP, TICK, SIZZLE, CLANK | Amber vs FUEL: POWER is a continuous tonal hum; FUEL is a rhythmic mechanical feed |
| COOLANT | Pump with liquid circulation; burbling, a pitched pulse, a periodic cavitation tick | DRIP, HISS, CLANK | O2 vs COOLANT (above) |
| FUEL | Slow feed ratchet; low, irregular impulse train | HISS, DRIP, CLANK | FUEL vs Alarm: FUEL is dull, low, unpitched and irregular; alarm pulses are regular, on the sweep grid, and above the FUEL band |
| INTEGRITY | Room tone of a pressurised hull; wide, soft, no transients | CREAK (SEAL_STRAIN), TICK (flow-zero hull pre-stage) | Chemlight vs O2: the Chemlight glow is silent; its throw, land and wane belong to `suit` (AA-30) |

**Failing distinctness per loop (HR-9).** A loop's failing sound is the descriptor's LS-14 class played in the **loop variant** of its subject (O2 and INTEGRITY air, POWER electrical, COOLANT liquid, FUEL heavy or viscous). The loop is read from the descriptor's subject through `node(id)`/`edge(id)` and the descriptor's `medium`. The variant lives in the `<context>` slot of the `wit` asset name (`sfx_wit_<loop>_<class>_<variant>`), so no class id is invented and lint AA-6 (d) still checks only the eight class tokens. Together with the stalled or sagging running timbre (D.7), each of the five loops has a failing signature no other loop shares.

*The frequency-register separation is a recipe constraint for the sound-designer and the asset spec; the identity-table lint proves only tag disjointness. Blind two-clip identification at or above 90 per cent is the test for O2 vs COOLANT, FUEL vs Alarm and POWER hum vs the tell bed (AC-AA).*
*Pillar.* P1. *Decided by.* run ruling R6, HR-9, `LSG:66`, `LSG:199`, `LSG:201`; `ART:472-488`.

**AA-27. The Graph's alarm-class annunciator is a separate identity sharing the single alarm rhythm.**
*Rule.* When a descriptor is `alarm_eligible` (and only then) the Graph's alarm-class annunciator (token `ann`, bus Witness, an electromechanical klaxon timbre) pulses with the art bible's one alarm rhythm (`ART:845`): one pulse per `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS`, so the ear, the warning mark (paired with shape and sound, HR-4) and the beacon agree. The annunciator pulses on the absolute grid `T mod BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS == 0`, except while `phase == BREACH`, when it follows the listener's S3 branch (ALIGNED: the same absolute grid; FALLBACK: `(T - breach_enter_T) mod BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS`), so inside BREACH the `ann` and `attn` pulses share one `hit(T)`; `last_alarm_T` and `ALARM_MIN_GAP_TICKS` are shared with the `attn` pulses under the one precedence rule of D.2 (AA-20 d) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: at most one start per identity per tick, a same-tick pair is one rhythm with two stacked timbres, and any other start needs the gap against an earlier tick. Every grid pulse asset is at most `W` ticks (L-14), so the steady cadence's duty is `ALARM_DUTY_MAX_PM` (the first sweep after entry and the sweep of a mode switch are bounded separately in D.2). **Listener scope:** the annunciator sounds only for a listener whose avatar is on the body that holds the eligible descriptor (`listener_scope` LOCAL with a body filter); a crewmate on another body does not hear a klaxon for a fault they are not aboard. **Sustained eligibility** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings; OQ-AA-33 resolved, option (b))*: while a `sev >= ALARM_SEV_MIN` descriptor stays eligible the klaxon **keeps its rhythm** (one pulse per sweep, unchanged) and **steps its level down by `ANN_STEPDOWN_DB` after `ANN_STEPDOWN_SWEEPS` sweeps**, and the level is restored to full on a new eligible descriptor or a rise in `sev` (D.20). An unrelenting klaxon every 2.1 s at tier 1, never ducked, was the Barotrauma pattern this GDD names as an anti-reference, and trains players to ignore the one alarm that reduce-motion players depend on. The step is a fixed level policy, a pure function of published state (D.20), so it is rebuilt after a restore (AA-4, AA-5); it is not ducking (no tier-2 impulse drives it, AA-11), not a compressor or a limiter (L-8 is untouched), and not a suppression (the rhythm is intact, every pulse still sounds, the level stays above the room-tone masker by D.19 R-h, and the HUD warning mark still carries the alarm for a reduce-motion or muted player). The annunciator's *first* pulse may wait for the next grid tick (at most `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS` ticks); this is not a suppression, because the descriptor's own witness class and the Suit's body cues sound from the poll that sees it (OQ-AA-6). It never plays in a lie (L-A), and a lie never suppresses it. `(provisional — the grid sentence is a wording refresh owed to the Graph owner, CX-A1)`.
*Pillar.* P1, P2. *Decided by.* AT-18; `ART:845`; `LSG:976`.

#### D. The Horror layer (`hor`)

**AA-28. Four cue kinds, from published facts only.**
*Rule.* For `horror_t01`: (a) **floor-borne creep**, positional from `pos_px` while the entity is moving in LURK, STALK or RETREAT (HE V1, V2, V7; `HE:1193`); (b) **REACH resolve**: a positional, non-percussive lead-in from `locked_pt`, started `REACH_RESOLVE_PRE_TICKS` before `reach_end_T` (`REACH_RESOLVE_PRE_TICKS <= UNFOLD_LO`, so the start is never before the REACH began, for every legal unfold of `UNFOLD_LO` to `UNFOLD_HI` ticks) and ending at `reach_end_T` with **no impact transient** (it then holds its final sustain, with no new energy, for at most `REACH_RESOLVE_HOLD_MAX_TICKS` until the frame poll sees `strike_T` or sees REACH left, D.11) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. The impact is the strike cue (c), requested only by the frame poll that first sees `strike_T`. If `horror.view` leaves REACH without a `strike_T` (a fizzle to RETREAT, HE-15/HE-17, `SUBSIDE_CANCELS_REACH`), the lead-in decays over `DECLICK_TICKS` with no impact: a pre-committed impact would be a true-family sound asserting a strike that never happened. The lead-in is scheduled against sim `T` (frozen by pause) and on a restore into it starts with a `seek` (AA-5, D.11); (c) **strike** at `strike_T`, positional at the seats and hull segments hit, requested by the frame poll of the frame whose notification published `strike_T` (AA-4, AA-13) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; (d) **hull creak** at an ingress or egress segment (structure-borne, `segment_at` anchor). **Marks and L3 dips are silent.** Mood: "patient, wet and structural ... weather with intent". The family is disjoint from `attn`, `wit` and `hal` and from the Graph loop sounds (AA-6; AC-HE-71). The `type_id` and `tell_id` enumeration is kept stable and enumerable for the Vertical Slice Knowledge Codex (HE-37). Phantom horrors (VS-A) use `hal` only and never a Graph witness class. Hidden real entities keep their sounds (`CON:243`, `SN:306`): a hallucination that hides a horror's body never silences its creep.
*Pillar.* P2, P1. *Decided by.* HE-37, `HE:374`, `HE:1193`, `HE:1285`; OQ 25 (`HE:1714`).

**AA-29. No proximity cue, no stinger; Horror never mutes, ducks or filters.**
*Rule.* There is **no non-diegetic proximity cue** for a horror: no sound whose loudness, pitch or rate encodes distance to or state of a horror (a distance-scaled stinger is a meter by ear). Every Horror sound is positional from `pos_px`, the segment or `locked_pt`, with ordinary physical falloff. No state stinger and no music cue. Horror never mutes, ducks or filters anything, and Audio never mutes or ducks a Horror cue at Horror's request. Horror pushes no noise (HE-36). Any conversion of Horror audio to a visual or tactile accessibility mapping is forbidden (`HE:1342-1360`, `HE:1714`); in particular no audio-to-visual mapping of any family may ever reveal a horror's position, state or direction (Horror OQ 25).
*Pillar.* P3, P1. *Decided by.* `HE:1193`, `HE:1714`, HE-36.

#### E. Body, foley and event sounds

**AA-30. Suit body cues (consequence channel).**
*Rule.* Audio owns the sounds for the Suit's named cue states: breath cadence steps (normal, closed-reserve, thin-air, load), valve click on reserve engage and disengage, pack-creak load layer with a footfall-linked exhale, suit rattle (shiver while moving), pack cooling-fan whirr and tick (heat), seam hiss (leak), cell whine at `CELL_WARN_S`, equip clunk, top-up hiss, stagger gasp, glowstick throw, land and wane, and the gasp loop. Each derives from a true state the Suit owns and each starts **no later than its visual**. Load and thin-air breath are separable by cadence direction (a blind two-clip test at or above 90 per cent, AC-SE-42). They are tier 1; a hallucination may neither remove a real valve click nor add one (`SE:1222`). The Suit emits no noise to Attention (SE-31): these sounds are presentation of a true state, not a stimulus. The Chemlight (#86B896) glow is silent; its sounds are throw, land and wane only. Breath cadence derives from the atmosphere bands the Graph publishes (`compartment(cid).band_o2`: NORMAL, THIN, HYPOXIC, ANOXIC; LS-19) via the Suit; Audio redefines none of them. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* **D92 (user ruling):** the three body cues the Player Fantasy depends on, **breath cadence steps, the reserve valve click and the visor-fog step** (the audible component of the Suit's `cue_fog` level steps: a damp, ragged edge on the breath layer, state owned by the Suit, asset the sound-designer's), ship at MVP as P1 with real assets, not as cut-first placeholders (AA-7). The kill/continue playtest runs a **muted arm and a sound arm**, each judged on its own pass criterion (HS-DT-1); Audio's part is that each of the three starts no later than its visual and is a true-state sound, so the muted arm loses no information that a visual does not also carry (AC-AA-151). The other Suit cues stay P2.
*Pillar.* P1. *Decided by.* `SE:474`, `SE:1220-1222`, SE-29, SE-31.

**AA-31. Footsteps: gait class only; the only noise readout.**
*Rule.* A footfall fires when the Controller's cycle position `floor(odometer / (STRIDE[gait] x 256))` (PCI D.11; the odometer is in 1/256 px) crosses a **foot-contact frame** (PC-11: the integer odometer counts pixels actually moved post-collision; `STRIDE_WALK` 10, `STRIDE_RUN` 15, `STRIDE_CARRY` 5 base px over an 8-frame cycle, cited by Controller rule and never restated as Audio values; the contact frames are an art/animation data list `FOOT_CONTACT_FRAMES`, `[PLACEHOLDER]` default frames 0 and 4 of the cycle, owned with the art-director). The cadence is therefore the animated foot's own: the Controller animates at 12 frames per second at every gait (`STRIDE_WALK` 10 at `V_WALK` 2, `STRIDE_RUN` 15 at `V_RUN` 3, `STRIDE_CARRY` 5 at `V_CARRY` 1 are each one frame per 5 ticks), so with contact frames 0 and 4 a footfall lands every 20 ticks walking, running and carrying alike, and a slowed avatar steps proportionally slower (D.14). **Gait changes the level and the timbre, never the rate**: the run is told by `FOOT_RUN_DELTA_DB` and the pack rattle. Walking into a wall adds 0 to the odometer, so it does not step; facing changes do not step; a player standing on a moving body does not step because the odometer counts moved pixels, not frame motion. Timbre is by gait class {WALK, RUN, CARRY}. RUN is `FOOT_RUN_DELTA_DB` louder (the pushed noise ratio `NOISE_RUN : NOISE_WALK`, 4 : 1, presented as a true state) and carries pack rattle. CARRY footfall is flat per tile and **never scales with carried mass** (`IDB:991`). A footstep is **never** a function of `level_pm`, locus distance, `phase`, Horror state or sanity. The player's own run is audible as the only noise meter (`PCI:135`, `PCI:821`). A slowed zone reduces speed and so footfall cadence proportionally; stagger, work, idle and down are time-stepped animation states (PC-11) in which no footfall fires, and the Suit's stagger gasp (AA-30) is that state's sound; facing changes do not step. Material-dependent footsteps are Vertical Slice. `NOISE_WALK`, `NOISE_RUN` and `NOISE_CARRY` are Controller values pushed to Attention (registry: `NOISE_PER_TICK`, walk 1 / run 4 / carry 1, source the Controller), cited by Controller rule (PC-10).
*Pillar.* P3. *Decided by.* PC-10, PC-11, `PCI:821`, `IDB:991`, AT-26.

**AA-32. The interaction cue set.**
*Rule.* Cues are keyed on `channel_view` transitions and `result_seq`: press pre-play, `ia_start`, `ia_loop`, `ia_cancel`, `ia_abort`, `ia_done`, `ia_nothing`, `ia_no`, plus throw, landing, cut-complete, vent and door-motor. Edge-detected from `transition_seq` and `result_seq`; **only the terminal transition's cue plays**; "stopped" (`ia_cancel`), "interrupted" (`ia_abort`) and "nothing happened" (`ia_nothing`) are three different sounds; the TAP sound plays at the Applied result; there is no cue for a deferred rejection until the reveal (`PCI:211`); a restore plays no abort cue. A mute capture of cancel, abort, outcome and done yields four different sequences (AC-IA-100, visual redundancy). Interaction owns the keys' meaning; Audio names the sounds. The channelled-verb loop is lit-only (the lamp is on for it, `ia_loop` is not a light cue).
*Pillar.* P1. *Decided by.* `IA:470`, `IA:977-979`, `PCI:211`.

**AA-33. Assembly, Body, pod and Boarding events.**
*Rule.* A weld or cut outcome plays only when `outcome_cue_gate` holds (D.12); the **bead settle is the single "it is done" for a weld**; the first weld has room in the mix (no escalation window in the bead settle, the first weep hiss and the bar disappearing, `MSA:1412`, OQ 29). The **seam-cut TOOL loop** is a positional, structure-borne sound audible on both sides of a closed seam (door penalties ignored, AA-10), because it is the far-side victim's tell (ASK AU1, MSA-18; the CASCADE cut's 10 to 30 s tell window, co-owned with Assembly, which owns its cue timing, `MSA:273`, `MSA:509`). `BODY_DOCK_CONTACT`, `BODY_HULL_SEPARATION` and `BODY_TRANSFER` are wired at MVP with assets Vertical Slice; a hull thud is a sound, not an Attention stimulus (`BDY:849-851`). `POD_DEPARTED{t0,n}`, `POD_ARRIVED` and `BERTHED` drive the pod hops; clause (g) below gives the berth its own MVP bed; the pod leg bed (a VS asset) fades in and out over `LEG_BED_FADE_TICKS`, non-positional, leaving the tell bed unchanged (V13); `web.leg_timing()` is Web Map's confirmed name (CX-A19) and returns `{t0,n}` only while LEG (on a restore mid-leg without timing the bed starts at steady level). Boarding supplies an event list only (`BS:333`: "owns the names, mix and attack ramp for the stub hiss, the load breath layers (Suit), pickup and set-down, the pod hush, the structure-borne bed; Boarding supplies the event list only"; the structure-borne bed named there is Attention's tell bed (AA-16; AT-15, AUD-4), present from tell beat 0 to the release and decaying on abort, never a Boarding or CALM ambient, and in CALM only AT-26's floor-gated hull creaks play (AA-22); this GDD reads the stub hiss as the Graph LEAK descriptor's HISS at the STUB anchor, AA-24, with no separate stub asset, and an uncapped stub is a Graph LEAK, BS-8) and there is **no `listen` verb and no audibility boost** (BS-4, `BS:179`): listening is a stance, and what there is to hear is what AA-24 to AA-26 already play `(designed; re-checked at the X-6 scan)`.
**(g) The berth bed (MVP).** While Web Map's published transit state is BERTHING (`transit_state`, WM-3 and WM-21; it is a field of `web.view`, `WM:297`, so Audio reads it there and adds no read: confirmed by Web Map, CX-A19), a sustained, non-positional `pod`-family **berth bed** plays (drive hum plus hull strain), for `berth` and for `unberth` alike. It fades in over `LEG_BED_FADE_TICKS` from the edge into BERTHING and fades out over `LEG_BED_FADE_TICKS` from the edge out of it (D.17), and it stops at `BERTHED` (or `UNBERTHED`), where the berth contact cue (or the single undock cue) plays. It reads **no progress datum**: no `t0`, no `n`, no elapsed or remaining ticks (the web read surface has none, `WM:300`), only the state edge; a restore into BERTHING starts the bed at steady level. Its hull-strain timbre tag is disjoint from every `attn` tag (lint L-10 extended) and it is not mistakable for the Tell: it is non-positional where creaks are positional, it carries no beat rate, and it is a continuous bed where the Tell is a rhythm. It is tier 4 and ducks under tier-2 impulses (D.18 victim set), so it never masks a beat. It is a witness of the crossing, never a stimulus (Audio pushes nothing, AA-3). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* **Default applied 2026-10-27, pending user confirmation (D59, Web Map route 1):** the berth verb is declared LOUD (one `U_LOUD` push at the berth apply; the unberth is QUIET), owned by Interaction (IA-40, QUIET to LOUD) and Web Map; the push is the verb's, so the bed itself still pushes nothing. The `-44 dBr` `BERTH_BED_CEIL_DB` was revisited as the ruling asks: the bed now sounds beside a deliberate loud act, but it is a tier-4 bed that ducks under beats, so it is kept at -44 by default. The D.6 worst-case set tolerates up to -36 (replacing the -44 term by -36 gives 2.4223e-3 - 3.98e-5 + 2.512e-4 = 2.634e-3, `L_nc,b` -25.79 dBr, tier-1 margin 7.79 dB, still at least 6), so the level is a listening-pass decision inside -44 to -36, not a D.6 constraint (OQ-AA-44). The pod leg bed (VS) is a separate asset for LEG. This discharges AC-WM-117 (AC-AA-128).
*Pillar.* P2, P1. *Decided by.* `MSA:1092`, `MSA:1071`, `BDY:301`, `WM:103`, `WM:266`, `WM:295`, `BS:179`, `BS:333`.

**AA-34. No value, no reward, no combat, no death sound.**
*Rule.* A sound never encodes value or hidden condition (`IDB:991`): no loot-value chime, no condition tell, carry footfall flat. No hit, kill or reward stinger (not a combat game; tools repel). No sound attributes harm to a player. No sound for saving, no death screen, no death sound (`SL:23`). No sound at a sanity stage boundary, a debunk or a recovery.
*Pillar.* P1, P4, P5 (loss is silent). *Decided by.* `IDB:991`, `SL:23`, `SN:1259-1262`.

#### F. The hallucination family (`hal`), the human channel and music

**AA-35. The `hal` family, its MVP signature and its audio leak (the "violet of sound").**
*Rule.* `hal` ids are the Filter's `FILTER_AUDIO_CLASSES` namespace, cited by reference (PF-20): MVP `FA_LIE_SIG_GAUGE` (needle, readout, card, glyph) and `FA_LIE_SIG_SPRITE` (housing, machine); VS-A `FA_PHANTOM_MOVE` and `FA_PHANTOM_PRESENCE`. **At MVP there is one sound signature, the class-(b) reading-lie signature, realised under two Filter ids by surface type**; the two ids share the reserved signature and differ only in surface-appropriate content (a quiet sound under a needle versus under a lamp housing or machine), and the family is lint-disjoint from every other family (AA-6). *(Ruling R1 wrote "exactly one class"; PF-20 publishes two MVP ids; both are one class-(b) signature, and the wording tension is resolved at the 2026-10-26 checkpoint: CX-F2 and OQ-AA-10 are closed.)* Every `hal` sound is recognisable by a **reserved signature no other family may use**: (i) a detuned fringe partial in a reserved band; (ii) a slow amplitude breathing at the same period as the violet fringe (4 to 6 s per cycle, `ART:563`; cited, not restated); (iii) a tail that over-reaches its source. Mood "doubt, not horror" (`SN:1259-1262`): wrong, warm, fringed; no heartbeat, no tempo change, no sting; the class-(c) victim fringe is silent. The reserved band is an asset-spec and import-tag constraint enforced by the identity-table lint (AA-6 f, AA-45); this GDD states the **perceptual property and the test**, not a filter chain: blind two-clip identification of the lying instrument by sound alone at or above 90 per cent, **a near-field distinctness test** (the AC-SE-42 analogue; the tester stands already within the instrument's reading range, `HAL_RADIUS_PX` and AA-36, so it measures whether the signature is distinct, never whether a lie can be detected from afar) and, with hue removed and with audio removed in turn, a trained tester still identifies the lying instrument above chance (HR-5). The first lie carries all three leak modes (violet gleam, glide, sound); the Filter may drop only the SOUND mode, at stage 2 or 3, never on the first lie; GLEAM and the motion mode are mandatory on every lie (SN-15 L-L; PF-15), and a lie whose SOUND mode is dropped has **no audio request at all**. At MVP `MODE_DROP_PM` is 0, so every MVP lie has a sound.
*Why.* R-6 (`ART:962`): if audio can lie it needs a leak equivalent to violet. A pitch detune alone is confusable with a real groaning pump (P1); a glitch artefact reads as "audio glitch" (`ART:851`), the "scanline for the lie".
*Pillar.* P4, P1. *Decided by.* run ruling R2; HR-5; SN-25; `SN:256`; PF-20.

**AA-36. `hal` transport and states `(designed; re-checked at the X-6 scan)`.**
*Rule.* Audio never reads `sanity.perception` (SN-23, CR-12). It consumes only what the Filter supplies, per local viewer: the request `(viewer, ref or position, intent in {ON, LEVEL(pm), OFF}, class)` of PF-20 and, for a lying instrument, the `leak_modes` of the Filter's `perceived(ref, viewer)` pull (PF-8). **ON** is requested on the first ACTIVE frame, so the sound begins **no later than the glide** (`t_sound_on <= t_glide_visible`, SN:1256, PF-15: "the sound request is emitted on the first ACTIVE frame, not when the needle has visibly moved"); **LEVEL** follows `leak_amplitude_pm` (D.15); **OFF** arrives at CLEAR, and after an entry leaves `sanity.perception` the level follows the remaining discrepancy, then off, **presentation-identical for expired, debunked and invalidated lies with no verdict flash and no relief cue** (PF-12). The audible start of the leak is within `SOUND_LEAD_MAX_MS` (Filter, 100 ms `[PLACEHOLDER]`, PF D.15) of the first ACTIVE frame. The measured device latency is part of spike AC-AA-6 and OQ-AA-4. LEVEL arrives quantised by `AUDIO_LEVEL_STEP_PM` (Filter, 20); Audio slews between received levels over `RUN_SLEW_TICKS` (the one slew window of the mix, D.7; no separate `hal` slew knob exists) and never extrapolates. Where the whole transport lies (a `PERCEPTION_OUT` request view versus the pull query) is the Filter's to fix; this GDD states only the fields it needs and renders neither a sound for a remote viewer (the Filter returns the honest record for any remote PlayerId, PF-10) nor a sound when the SOUND mode is not in force. A ref that resolves to a world surface is positional at that surface; a ref that resolves to a card or HUD reading is non-positional in the viewer's own space `(provisional — HUD and Instruments undesigned)`. Requests are `OWNER_PRIVATE` per seat. **Audible radius (`HAL_RADIUS_PX`, P4 verification economy).** The `hal` leak of a world-surface ref is audible only within `R_hal(ref) = min(HAL_RADIUS_PX, legibility_range_px(ref))` of the viewer's avatar, where `legibility_range_px(ref)` is the range at which the lying instrument's sprite is legible, so the leak can **never be heard from farther away than the instrument can be read**: spotting a lie by ear then costs the same approach (time and risk) as reading it, and checking is never free (P4: "checking costs time, risk or trust"). Beyond `R_hal` the `hal` voice is not requested from the pool (a cull, not a duck), fading in and out over `HAL_FADE_TICKS` at the boundary, with no sting. Only the `hal` voice is bounded: the instrument's true running or witness sound (AA-24, AA-26) is a different family, is untouched by this radius and keeps its own audible floor (AA-11, AA-25), so a lying gauge over a stalling pump still has the pump heard beyond `R_hal` (the falsifier, AA-37 e). A ref that resolves to a card or HUD reading is already non-positional and in the viewer's own space; it has no distance and `HAL_RADIUS_PX` does not apply to it. `legibility_range_px(ref)` is a read-only datum owned by the render and instrument layer `(provisional — Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: In-World Instruments is now designed and cites the datum (`IW:1398`, "never above `legibility_range_px` of the lying surface") without defining or registering it; the ask to publish and register `legibility_range_px(ref)` is routed (OQ-AA-39). Until it is published the rule is R_hal = HAL_RADIUS_PX, with HAL_RADIUS_PX held at the placeholder bound in Tuning Knobs §H as the fallback; once the datum exists, a HAL_RADIUS_PX above a surface's legibility range is applied as the min() and logged once as a DIAGNOSTIC, not refused (E-AA-60))`. If the Filter refuses to emit audio requests, MVP class-(b) lies play **no** reading-lie sound and HR-5's sound mode is unmet. **Disposition (creative-director, CD-GDD-ALIGN):** the Filter must emit audio requests for every MVP class-(b) lie while `MODE_DROP_PM` is 0; a refusal reopens PF-20, not AA-35. Audio does not design around it: it keeps no reading-lie sound and the CONFLICT diagnostic (E-AA-51, AC-AA-60).
*Pillar.* P4. *Decided by.* run ruling R3; PF-20, PF-12, PF-15, PF-10; SN-23.

**AA-37. What a lie may never do to a true sound.**
*Rule.* A `hal` sound (a) never imitates a witness class, a running hum, a tell beat, an alarm or a Horror cue (disjoint ids, AA-6); (b) never removes, delays, ducks, masks or voice-steals a true cue (AA-11, AA-12); (c) never plays an alarm-class sound or the tell family (L-A); (d) never touches the human voice or the P1 cue; (e) is never placed as a lie about a real fault at a descriptor anchor in a way that replaces the true sound: **a real hiss still plays when the instrument falsely reads OK, which is the falsifier** (P4: "no lie is unfalsifiable"; at least one channel of truth remains). A `hal` bus has a maximum below the witness bus nominal by `HAL_BUS_REL_DB` so it cannot out-shout a true witness, and an asset-lint peak `HAL_PEAK_BELOW_BEAT_DB` under the loudest `attn` beat; its voice is exempt from tier ducking so a beat cannot mask the leak.
*Pillar.* P4, P1. *Decided by.* run ruling R1, L-A (`SN:258`), SN-25, SN-27.

**AA-38. Sanity silence and no self-sign.**
*Rule.* Audio reads `sanity_view(player_id)` for exactly **one MVP-dormant purpose**: the P1 cue trigger (AA-39). **No mix, filter, EQ, ducking, loudness or ambience parameter is ever a function of the local player's own sanity stage or level.** No sound at a stage boundary, a debunk or a recovery; no heartbeat; no tempo change. The player must not learn their stage by ear (SN-14, `SN:244-247`). At every stage 0 to 3 the request list of every TRUE family is bit-identical for the same published sim state.
*Pillar.* P4. *Decided by.* SN-14, SN-27, `SN:1259-1262`.

**AA-39. The Voice bus and the P1 proximity cue.**
*Rule.* A `Voice` bus exists in the bus tree now (reserved, empty in solo MVP): no DSP from sanity, stage or Filter, never ducked by hallucination cues, never faked; the speaker indicator is HUD's. The **P1 proximity cue** (the class-(c) victim tell, VS-B; contract plus an identity-table row at MVP) triggers on the source crewmate's `sanity_view.tell_on` (not `harm_eligible`, so it leads eligibility by at least 113.4 s, `SN:296`); it is a physically plausible suit sound (regulator or breath irregularity, `P1_CUE_PERIOD_TICKS`) played at the crewmate's suit position **on every client except the source** (no self-sign, SN-14); **diegetic and positional, taking ordinary physical falloff like any suit sound** (the correction to the brief: what Horror forbids is a *non-diegetic* stinger whose loudness encodes distance to a horror, which this is not); never altered by any listener's sanity; outside the `hor` and `hal` families. Whether the source hears it is routed to the game-designer (`ART:909`; recommendation: no). In the solo MVP it is dormant.
*Pillar.* P1, P4. *Decided by.* SN-11 (`SN:295`), SN-14, `ART:907-909`, HR-7, `IDX:41`.

**AA-40. Music and ambience.**
*Rule.* **No authored music at MVP**; the ship's sounds are the score. VS may evaluate a sparse non-melodic layer that is never keyed to `level_pm`, `phase`, `tier`, sanity stage, Horror state or the tell; adaptive scoring is Alpha. Ambience is a static room tone; the INTEGRITY room tone is `run` (AA-26), not `amb`; no ambience parameter is ever a function of `level_pm`, `phase` or sanity. A static drone is rejected at MVP (it costs headroom against the quantised pre-tell creaks). A running home-ship is never silent (the concept's "the ship must breathe", read as an instruction about audibility and the upkeep fantasy, not a timbre; CD-1 resolved by the creative-director at CD-GDD-ALIGN). A Vertical Slice Depth Ring may shift only the pre-tell creak density table, never the tell contract or the beat floor.
*Pillar.* P3. *Decided by.* `CON:395`, `CON:479-480`, AUD-8.

#### G. Accessibility, settings, data, bands and lints

**AA-41. Captions policy (policy now, implementation Alpha).**
*Rule.* **Captionable, with precise source-naming only:** `wit`, `ann` and `run` sounds, Suit body cues, foley (including the player's own run, provisionally as own-action foley because it names the player's gait, not the web's regard, CX-A7), the three terminal interaction sounds, UI. **Never captioned:** tell onset, beats, bed and release; the breach event and alarm cadence; pre-tell creaks; Horror sounds; the `hal` family; the P1 cue. Consistency check: because `hal` ids are disjoint from every captionable family, an uncaptioned `hal` sound never sits beside a captioned twin, so the caption set is not a lie oracle. A deaf or hard-of-hearing player's tell channel is **visual plus the opt-in haptic beat pulse** (AA-42, user ruling D51); no caption is added, the ruling having chosen the beat pulse, which carries the audio beat's information and nothing more, over a generic `[creaking]` caption. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* R-6: because audio can lie, the leak (AA-35) is the answer; precise source-naming of the captioned sounds is the other branch.
*Pillar.* P3, P4. *Decided by.* R-6 (`ART:962`), `AT:1223`, `HE:1342-1360`, OQ-AT-1.

**AA-42. Tactile channel: none by default; two ruled exceptions (X10; D44, D51).** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *Default.* No rumble for the bed, the tell or Horror: a tell-synced rumble that is on for every player would be an Attention indicator under the standing user decision. *Exception (i), own body cues (D44, user ruling).* Haptics driven only by the player's own true body cues (the Suit's published breath, valve, shiver and stagger states) are not Attention indicators: they are the player's own consequence channel, keyed to Suit state and to nothing else. *Exception (ii), the beat pulse for the deaf or muted player in the dark (D51, user ruling).* An opt-in setting `haptic_beat_pulse`, **off by default**, plays one short controller pulse per published tell beat, on the same `(tell_epoch, tell_beat)` edge and under the same one-per-poll, no-catch-up, nothing-on-restore rules as the audio beat (AA-4, AA-15). It carries **the same information as the audio beat and nothing more**, which is why it is not an Attention indicator: fixed duration `HAPTIC_BEAT_MS` and strength `HAPTIC_BEAT_STRENGTH_PM`, never scaled by `tier`, tell progress, beat index or `level_pm`; the onset pulses once because the onset carries beat 0 (AA-14); no pulse for the bed, a tier step, the release, the breach entry, the alarm pulses, pre-tell creaks or Horror. The setting's UI is Alpha (Accessibility Settings, undesigned); `design/accessibility-requirements.md` is to be authored later with `/ux-design accessibility` and is not created here. OQ-AA-2 and OQ-AA-15 are resolved by this rule. *Pillar.* P3, P2. *Decided by.* `ART:954`, `AT:1331` (gains this exception: refresh owed, X-4), user rulings D44 and D51.

**AA-43. Settings boundaries (rules now, UI Alpha).**
*Rule.* Per-group sliders exist for Music (`mus`), Ambience (`amb`), UI (`ui`) and Voice (`vox`). The **World group is every in-world family: `wit`, `ann`, `run`, `attn`, `hor`, `suit`, `foot`, `ia`, `asm`, `body`, `pod`, `p1c`, `hal`, and shares one slider**. The Ambience slider covers `amb` only (a static room tone); the INTEGRITY room tone is `run`, so it sits on the World slider and no setting can strip a consequence-channel sound with the Ambience slider. So no setting can strip the `hal` SOUND leak or the tell independently of the witness sounds. No setting lowers the tell bed below its margin relative to other world sounds (the margin is enforced relatively, D.6). Mono downmix keeps the bed. Reduce-flashing and reduce-motion never reduce audio (M1; FT E20, E21). No setting displays or sonifies the Attention level, and none removes a leak mode (PF-15). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* The one tactile exception is the opt-in `haptic_beat_pulse` (AA-42), which carries the audio beat's information and nothing more.
*Pillar.* P3, P4. *Decided by.* M1, M2, `ART:954` item 15; PF-15 ("never below two").

**AA-44. Data-driven assets and loudness.**
*Rule.* Every authored level, loudness (LU), radius, interval and class table lives in `assets/data/audio/tell_audio.json` (and sibling data files), never in code (coding standard). The data path differs from Attention's reference `assets/data/attention/tell_audio.json` (`AT:1101`); the path refresh is owed to Attention (CX-A8). Asset spec (audio-director, after approval): 48 kHz throughout (the project mix rate is pinned to 48000, `[UNV]` setting name), mono for positional sources and stereo only for the bed and ambience; loops, beds and every sound longer than `PCM_MAX_TICKS` (`[PLACEHOLDER]` 60) are OGG Vorbis, and shorter one-shots (beats, footfalls, ticks, drips, clanks, pulses) are 16-bit PCM (`AudioStreamWAV`) because decoding Vorbis for every one-shot at an unpinned rate is the wrong default; every stream is preloaded at boot, never loaded in a frame *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* `[UNV]` until the first import spike; momentary-loudness metadata stored per asset for D.5; file-size budget and per-family loudness targets `[PLACEHOLDER]`.
*Pillar.* P2. *Decided by.* run ruling R19, coding standards.

**AA-45. The band plan and masking constraint (asset-spec constraints, `[PLACEHOLDER]` values).**
*Rule.* Masking is band-local, so the identity table is paired with a band plan that the asset spec enforces by an import-time tag: (a) the tell bed occupies a low structure-borne band plus an upper-partial edge layer so it survives laptop speakers (`[PLACEHOLDER]` about 30 to 120 Hz and 200 to 400 Hz); (b) the tell beat and onset carry a transient readable on a small speaker, so the beats carry the evidence when the bed is lost (AUD-5: each channel sufficient alone); (c) the POWER running hum's fundamental sits at or above `[PLACEHOLDER]` 160 Hz, outside the bed band (AA-26); (d) the Graph's HISS, WIND and SIZZLE are broadband noise well above the bed; TICK, CLANK and CREAK are mid-band impulses; (e) the `hal` fringe partial occupies a narrow reserved mid band (`[PLACEHOLDER]` about 1200 to 1800 Hz) that **no other family may occupy** (AA-6 f); (f) Horror's creep and strike overlap the bed band by design and are accepted because they are positional and shorter, and the bed holds its margin against them as a non-tell sound. Two families may share a primary band only if their temporal envelopes differ (impulse versus sustained). The lint stores each asset's spectral centroid and 80-per-cent-energy band and enforces `family -> allowed band`; **MVP default: the tag-only lint plus the blind tests (AC-AA-28, AC-AA-58); the spectral-centroid and band lint is a Vertical Slice upgrade** (CD-GDD-ALIGN, OQ-AA-5), adopted when the spike proves it feasible and not before.
*Pillar.* P2, P4. *Decided by.* M2, M6, run ruling R2; the systems-designer's band-plan risk (small speakers, bed band overlap).

**AA-46. Per-listener scope and co-op addressing `(provisional — Co-op Session undesigned)`.**
*Rule.* Every request carries a `listener_scope`: the tell bed is addressed to **ALL_LIVING** players (AT-15) and is the same at every client; positional layers are per listener; `hal` requests are `OWNER_PRIVATE` per seat; the P1 cue is ALL_EXCEPT_SOURCE; true sounds are identical on every client so "do you hear that too?" is a valid cross-check. A dead listener (VS-B) is addressed no bed. In the solo MVP there is one listener. Listener context (home-ship body, derelict, a pod in transit, a `NOT_LIVE` home-ship) changes only the alarm clock reference (AA-20), the door penalties (AA-10) and the pod leg bed (AA-33); the tell bed, the beats, the witness classes and the `hal` rules are identical in every context.
*Pillar.* P1, P2. *Decided by.* AT-15, SN-26.

**AA-47. Source lints (all build-breaking; each is a named acceptance criterion in part 2).**
*Rule.* L-1: no symbol `sanity.perception`, `tell_remaining`, `tell_progress_pm` or `breach_phase` in the audio source; `sanity_view` is read only by the P1-cue module. L-2: `level_pm` appears only as the argument of `ambient_cue_step`; no continuous audio parameter references it. L-3: only `hal` assets carry the `leak` tag; no other family's asset uses a `FILTER_AUDIO_CLASSES` id. L-4: asset tag families are pairwise disjoint (M6) and the `hal` fringe band is occupied by `hal` only. L-5: no code path lets a `hal` request modify, delay, duck or remove a request of another family. L-6: `ann` assets are reachable only from a descriptor with `alarm_eligible`; `attn` breach-entry and pulse assets only from the `attention.view` BREACH path; both run on the shared alarm grid (`LSG:1110` plus CX-A1). L-7: no request is generated for a descriptor with `medium == NONE` (WORN). L-8: no compressor, limiter or sidechain targets a tier-1 bus (M3), and there is no Master clip guard either: the D.19 peak relation (R-e) and the `master_peak_warn` DIAGNOSTIC replace it *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. L-9: `release_cue_relation` is true at data load. L-10: every pre-tell asset tag differs from every beat asset tag, and the berth bed's hull-strain timbre tag differs from every `attn` tag (beat, bed, tier stem, pre-tell, breach entry, pulse). L-11: the M4 and M2 proxies hold for the asset set (an offline computation). L-12: no symbol of `AudioServer`, `AudioStreamPlayer*` or a bus in Attention's source (AUD-9, owned by Attention). L-13: no request path silences a `medium != NONE`, `sev >= 1` descriptor whose mode has no Graph-defined `intensity_pm`, or a SEAL_STRAIN in its HEAT/TICK branch (`LSG:649`). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* L-14: every `ann` asset and every `attn` grid-pulse asset, tail included, is at most `W` ticks, and the breach-entry (pulse 0) asset, tail included, is at most `BREACH_ENTRY_TAIL_MAX_TICKS`, which is below `ALARM_MIN_GAP_TICKS` (D.2). L-15: no `Tween`, `Timer`, `create_tween` or wall-clock call drives a world voice (AA-5). L-16: the D.19 relations R-a, R-d, R-e and R-h hold at data load, and R-b, R-c, R-f and R-g are logged as DIAGNOSTICs until the listening pass. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* L-17: the only attenuation applied to an `ann` voice is the D.20 step-down (a level offset sampled at the pulse start from published state, depth at most `ANN_STEPDOWN_DB`); no sidechain, ducking or dynamics processor targets it. L-18: vibration calls are reachable only from the beat-pulse module (when `haptic_beat_pulse` is true, from the D.1 BEAT edge, with the fixed constants) and from the Suit's own-body-cue path (AA-42).
*Pillar.* P1, P2, P4. *Decided by.* M3, M6, `LSG:1110`, SN-23.

**Formula ids this section names** (written in Formulas, D.1 to D.20): D.1 cue dedupe and the per-poll classifier · D.2 alarm pulse phase, duty and minimum gap · D.3 release relation and bed release · D.4 restore bed fade · D.5 breach-entry loudness bound · D.6 bed audibility margin · D.7 running-timbre mapping · D.8 witness level and rate · D.9 room-graph transmission · D.10 voice allocation, reserve sizing and stealing order · D.11 REACH resolve scheduling · D.12 `outcome_cue_gate` usage · D.13 pre-tell creak predicate · D.14 footfall cadence and gait loudness · D.15 `hal` level and breathing · D.16 hull anchor derivation · D.17 pod leg bed envelope · D.18 ducking · D.19 audibility under masking and the peak budget *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* · D.20 annunciator step-down under sustained eligibility *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.

### States and Transitions

Audio owns **no sim state** and persists nothing. The machines below are *per-listener presentation memory*, rebuilt from published state after any restore; every transition is caused by a **change in a published fact** (never by a timer in Audio, AUD-1), and the "who may cause it" column names the owner of that fact. Audio itself causes no transition in any system.

#### S1. Cue kinds, and what each does at restore, pause and death

| Cue kind | Members | Trigger edge | On restore | On pause | On death stub | De-click / fade |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Sustained, steady | `run` loops, the INTEGRITY room tone, `wit` classes of live descriptors, `suit` breath loops, an in-progress seam-cut TOOL loop, the `p1c` loop (VS-B), the positional hull-stress layer | the state is present (`N/<id>` and `L/<loop>/<anchor>` keys for running loops; the descriptor set woken by `F/<id>` keys for `wit` classes; `B/<cid>` keys for the INTEGRITY room tone; `phase == TELL` with `tier >= 2`) | resume at steady level from published state | held with sim `T` | stop (nothing is published) | `DECLICK_TICKS` |
| Sustained, tell bed | the `attn` bed and its tier stems | `phase == TELL` | **fade in over `RESTORE_FADE_TICKS`**; beats resume at the next published beat | held | stop | `RESTORE_FADE_TICKS` |
| Sustained, lie | the `hal` SOUND leak | an active Filter request (ON or LEVEL) | resume with the lie at full leak level on the first frame | held | stop | `DECLICK_TICKS` only |
| Sustained, scheduled | the REACH resolve while `reach_end_T` is in the future | a published `reach_end_T` | start with `seek` if inside the lead-in; the lead-in ends at `reach_end_T`; no impact without `strike_T` | frozen (it counts sim `T`) | stop | `DECLICK_TICKS` |
| One-shot | onset, beat, release, breach entry, alarm pulse, subside, pre-tell creak, strike, ingress/egress creak, `ia_*` terminals, assembly outcome, `BODY_*`, `POD_*` | a strictly greater counter or a new event | **never replays**; the poll primes last-seen values | held | no sound | n/a |
| UI | `ui` | a UI event | n/a | continues | n/a | n/a |

#### S2. The `attn` cue machine, as consumed from `attention.view`

Attention owns the phases, timings and outcomes; this table lists only what Audio does on each *observed* delta. Audio reads `phase`, never `breach_phase` (AC-AT-67).

| From (observed `phase`) | To | Observed delta | Audio action | Voice | Who causes it |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CALM | CALM | quantised cue step change (`ambient_cue_step`) | the next creak predicate uses `CREAK_MEAN_TICKS[step]` and `CREAK_MIN_GAP_TICKS[step]` (AA-22) | pool, tier 4 | `attention.step` (level crossing a band edge) |
| CALM | TELL | new `tell_epoch`, `phase == TELL` | **onset (carries beat 0)**; the bed starts at tier layer 1; no new pre-tell creak starts | reserved impulse voice plus the bed stream | `attention.step` (Attention's level and arming) |
| TELL | TELL | `(tell_epoch, tell_beat)` strictly greater | **one beat** (at most one per poll) | reserved impulse voice | `attention.step` (`beat_schedule`) |
| TELL | TELL | `tier` increases | crossfade to the next bed layer over `TIER_XFADE_TICKS`; add the positional hull-stress layer at tier 2 | bed stream / pool tier 3 | `attention.step` (`TIER2_TICKS`, `TIER3_TICKS`, on the escalation clock that freezes while `L < theta_HOLD`; no tier change while frozen, and a tier never falls inside a TELL) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| TELL | CALM | `tell_outcome == ABORTED` | beats stop; the bed decays over `RELEASE_TICKS`; the release cue | reserved impulse voice | the player's restraint, through Attention (`ABORT_HOLD_TICKS`) |
| TELL | BREACH | `phase == BREACH` | the bed stream fades out over `BED_END_TICKS` (no hard stop); the **breach-entry event is pulse 0**, with the bounded step (D.5); the alarm cadence begins *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | reserved impulse voice | `attention.step` (`breach_due_T`) |
| BREACH | BREACH | the alarm clock reaches a pulse start (D.2) and the D.2 precedence rule admits it *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | one alarm pulse, at most `ALARM_DUTY_MAX_PM` of the sweep | reserved impulse voice | the tick grid (a pure function of `T`, `breach_enter_T`, the listener's body) |
| BREACH | SUBSIDING | `phase == SUBSIDING` | the alarm stops; one collapse; **no "danger over"** | reserved impulse voice | `attention.step` |
| SUBSIDING | CALM | `phase == CALM` | **nothing** (never an all-clear) | none | `attention.step` |
| CALM | BREACH | a hitch skipped TELL | breach entry as above; no stale beat, no onset | reserved impulse voice | `attention.step` |
| any | any (restore) | the post-restore notification | prime all last-seen values; no one-shot; the bed fades in if TELL; alarm pulses resume on the next grid tick if BREACH; nothing plays if CALM after ABORTED | the bed stream | Save/Load, Kernel |

#### S3. The alarm clock's branch (a small sub-machine inside BREACH)

| State | Entry | Exit | Behaviour | Who causes it |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ALIGNED | at a pulse boundary: the debounced BEACON read is live (`BEACON_LIVE_HOLD_TICKS`) **and** the listener is on the home-ship's body *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | at a pulse boundary: either condition false | pulses at `T mod BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS == 0` | the Emitter (beacon commanded at T+1, off at SUBSIDING entry), the listener's body; the first choice is made at `breach_enter_T + BEACON_LIVE_HOLD_TICKS + 2`, so on the home ship pulse 0 is followed by an ALIGNED interval and never by a FALLBACK-then-switch |
| FALLBACK | at a pulse boundary: otherwise (derelict, `NOT_LIVE` home-ship, beacon absent or not yet commanded) | at a pulse boundary: ALIGNED conditions true | pulses at `(T - breach_enter_T) mod BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS == 0` | the listener's body, the Emitter |

A pulse in either state follows the D.2 precedence rule *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: pulse 0 always emits; at most one `attn` and one `ann` start per tick (a same-tick pair stacks); every other start needs `ALARM_MIN_GAP_TICKS` against the latest alarm-family start on an earlier tick (`last_alarm_T`, shared with the Graph annunciator). The branch is re-evaluated only at a pulse boundary, and the debounced beacon read changes only after the raw read has differed for `BEACON_LIVE_HOLD_TICKS` ticks.

#### S4. The `hal` cue machine, as consumed from the Perception Filter `(designed; re-checked at the X-6 scan; states are Sanity's, mapped to sound by PF-12)`

| State | Entry (Filter request) | Audio action | Exit | Who causes it |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| NONE | no request for the ref | silent | an ON request | Sanity decides *whether, when, for whom*; the Filter emits the request |
| ONSET | ON on the first ACTIVE frame (`lie_tau = 0`) | the SOUND leak starts at once, no later than the glide becomes visible | `lie_tau >=` the arrival time | the Filter (PF-12, PF-15) |
| HOLD | the glide has arrived | the leak is sustained at the D.15 level with the 4 to 6 s breathing | the entry leaves `sanity.perception` | the Filter |
| RETURN | the entry left `sanity.perception` (expired, debunked or invalidated: indistinguishable) | the level follows the remaining discrepancy, with the over-reaching tail; never below the ACTIVE minimum before CLEAR; **no sting, no verdict, no relief cue** | the discrepancy is below the Filter's threshold, then OFF | the Filter (PF-12, PF-14) |
| restore | the post-restore notification | resume in whatever state the Filter reports from the first frame (no clean frame) | as above | Save/Load, the Filter |

A lie with no SOUND mode in force has no request, so no machine instance exists for it. A stage change never ends a lie (SN-16) and so never ends this machine.

#### S5. The tell bed's lifecycle (per listener)

| State | Entry | Exit | Behaviour |
|---|---|---|---|
| ABSENT | `phase != TELL` and no release in progress | `phase == TELL` | no bed |
| RUNNING (tier 1/2/3) | onset; restore into TELL (with the `RESTORE_FADE_TICKS` fade) | tier step (layer crossfade) or TELL exit | base bed; layers added at tiers 2 and 3; unaffected by zoom, camera, room, transit, reduce-flashing |
| RELEASING | TELL to CALM with ABORTED | `RELEASE_TICKS` elapsed | the level falls to silence by `BED_RELEASE_DEPTH_DB` over `RELEASE_TICKS`; a release cue sounds |
| ENDED | TELL to BREACH | the stream has faded over `BED_END_TICKS` | the stream fades out from the entry tick and then stops; the breach-entry event's tail carries the transition *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

#### S6. Listener contexts (no change to cue content)

Listener on the home-ship's body, on a derelict, in a pod (LEG) or on a `NOT_LIVE` home-ship changes only (a) the alarm clock reference (S3), (b) the room-graph and door penalties (AA-10) and (c) the pod leg bed (AA-33). The tell bed, the beats, the witness classes and the `hal` rules are identical in every context (AA-46).

### Interactions with Other Systems

**Reading the tables.** Audio writes **nothing** to the simulation in any row below: "data out" is "none" everywhere, because its only output is sound. "Data in" lists the exact read view, dirty key, event or request the counterpart fixed. "Names fixed elsewhere" lists the hook, mutator, view or key names that Audio consumes verbatim and never renames. Each row **quotes the counterpart's own Interactions row** (trimmed) so bidirectional consistency can be checked; where the counterpart carries a stale token (it says "no file", "undesigned", "Soft", or cites a dangling `AUD-13`/`AUD-14`), the row says so and the correction is a *refresh owed* in Cross-References (rows RF-1 to RF-11), never an edit made here. "(provisional)" marks every neighbour that is in flight or absent.

Emitter: nothing new (`emitters()` already publishes `output_pm` per emitter).

## Formulas

**Conventions.** Cue *existence*, identity and timing are integers: published counters, sim ticks `T` and the shared integer presentation hash (`HASH_SEED`, Attention, AT-28; shared with Lighting, LO-19); gains, pitches and rates are presentation floats (the presentation layer never feeds the sim, CR-11) and tests compare them to 0.01 dB or 0.01 semitone. **Levels are in dBr**: authoring-reference decibels where 0 dBr is the Witness bus nominal peak. dBr is a mixing scale, not a loudness unit; the two loudness-bounded rules (D.5, D.6) are verified by offline capture, because Godot has no loudness meter. Attention's `beat_schedule` (F-12), `ambient_cue_step` (F-24) and `tell_window_contract`, Emitter's `beacon_aim` (EM-16), Assembly's `outcome_cue_gate`, the Graph's band tables (LS-7) and the Controller's odometer (PCI D.11) are **cited by name and never restated** beyond the one expression a worked example needs. Every constant marked `[PLACEHOLDER]` is Audio's, with its safe range in Tuning Knobs; locked names (`BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS`, `ABORT_HOLD_TICKS`, `BEAT_MIN_GAP_TICKS`, `MIN_BEATS_IN_FLOOR`, `UNFOLD_LO`, `LIES_MAX`, `OUTCOME_CUE_WINDOW_TICKS`, `TILE_PX`) are used at their registered values. Where the two specialist drafts differed the choice is written `(orchestrator ruling: ...)`.

**D.1 Cue dedupe and the per-poll classifier**

The `cue_dedupe` formula is defined as:

`lex_gt = (e > e_seen) OR (e == e_seen AND b > b_seen)` ; event (rows tested in order, first match wins): `ph == TELL AND e > e_seen` -> ONSET (this *is* beat 0) ; `ph_seen == TELL AND ph == TELL AND e == e_seen AND b > b_seen` -> BEAT ; `ph_seen == TELL AND ph == CALM AND out == ABORTED` -> RELEASE ; `ph_seen in {TELL, CALM} AND ph == BREACH` -> BREACH_ENTER ; `ph_seen == BREACH AND ph == SUBSIDING` -> SUBSIDE ; any other pair or a restore -> none ; **the rows are mutually exclusive on the published `(ph, e, b)`** (each tests a different `ph`, and ONSET `e > e_seen` excludes BEAT `e == e_seen`), so a poll classifies the *net* change from `seen` to `now` and at most one row can match; the intermediate transitions of a hitch are not observable and are not played (the lost-cue consequences are E-AA-67 and E-AA-68) ; after **every** poll, event or not, `seen := now`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `e`, `e_seen` | int | 0 to 2^31 - 1 | `attention.view` `tell_epoch` / presentation memory | Epoch published now / last seen; +1 at each TELL entry |
| `b`, `b_seen` | int | 0 to about 21 | `attention.view` `tell_beat` / presentation memory | Beat index within the epoch (an 1800-tick tell has about 20.6 beats, `beat_schedule`) |
| `ph`, `ph_seen` | enum | CALM, TELL, BREACH, SUBSIDING | `attention.view` `phase` / memory | **Never `breach_phase`** (lint L-1; AC-AT-67) |
| `out` | enum | NONE, ABORTED, BREACHED | `attention.view` `tell_outcome` | Distinguishes an abort from a breach |
| `lex_gt` | bool | | derived | Lexicographic pair compare; a new tell resets `b` to 0 while `e` rises |
| result | event id or none | 0 or 1 per poll | derived | At most one `attn` event per poll (AUD-3) |

**Output Range:** 0 or 1 event per poll. The ceiling on beat cues is `TICK_RATE / BEAT_MIN_GAP_TICKS` = 2 per second; the nominal closing interval `BEAT_END_TICKS` gives 1.33 per second. A poll interval above 500 ms can coalesce beats: accepted as "one observable change", which is why AC-AT-47's audible half is captured at nominal frame rate. `seen := now` (never `seen + 1`) means a skipped beat is never replayed (AUD-3, AC-AT-115), and a restore that returns an *earlier* epoch cannot leave `seen` ahead of the next real onset (a false calm).
**Example:** `attention.step` at `T` = 12000 enters TELL and publishes `tell_epoch` 7, `tell_beat` 0 in the same run (AUD-2); the next poll sees `(7, 0)` with `ph_seen` CALM: ONSET, `seen := (7, 0, TELL)`. Beats at jitter 0 fall at 150, 292, 425 and 551 ticks after entry (`beat_schedule`, F-12). A 3-second hitch spanning ticks 12292 to 12472 publishes `(7, 2)` and `(7, 3)`; the next poll sees `(7, 3)` with `seen = (7, 1)`: **one** BEAT, beat 2 never sounds, `seen := (7, 3)`. A poll that reads `ph` BREACH at `T` = 12900 with a stale higher `b`: BREACH_ENTER only, no stale beat. A restore that returns `(7, 3, TELL)`: no event, `seen := (7, 3, TELL)`; the next published `(7, 4)` plays. A new tell `(8, 0)` after `(7, 9)`: `8 > 7`, ONSET (comparing `b` alone would drop it).

**D.2 Alarm pulse phase, duty and minimum gap**

The `alarm_pulse_phase` formula is defined as:

`S = BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS` ; `W = floor(S * ALARM_DUTY_MAX_PM / 1000)` ; `ALARM_MIN_GAP_TICKS = S div 2` ; `beacon_live_f` = the Emitter read `beacon_live`, filtered so that it changes only after the raw value has differed from it for `BEACON_LIVE_HOLD_TICKS` consecutive ticks ; `mode_next = ALIGNED if (beacon_live_f AND listener_body == home_ship_body), else FALLBACK`, evaluated at `breach_enter_T + BEACON_LIVE_HOLD_TICKS + 2` for the first interval and thereafter at the tick of each emitted `attn` grid pulse ; `hit(T) = (T mod S == 0)` if ALIGNED, `((T - breach_enter_T) mod S == 0)` if FALLBACK ; **precedence rule, evaluated once per tick `T`** with `prev(T)` the latest alarm-family start tick strictly before `T` (either identity) and `gap_ok(T) = (T - prev(T) >= ALARM_MIN_GAP_TICKS)` : `emit_attn(T) = (T == breach_enter_T) OR (phase == BREACH AND hit(T) AND gap_ok(T))`, where the first term is **pulse 0, exempt from the gap by identity** ; `emit_ann(T) = ann_hit(T) AND gap_ok(T)` with `ann_hit(T) = hit(T)` while `phase == BREACH` and `(T mod S == 0)` otherwise (only for an `alarm_eligible` descriptor and a listener on its body, AA-27) ; at most one `attn` and one `ann` start per tick, `last_alarm_T := T` when either starts, and an `attn`/`ann` pair on one tick is **one rhythm with two stacked timbres** ; **on a restore into BREACH** the debounce is primed to the raw read, `mode_next` is evaluated at the restore tick and `last_alarm_T` is primed to the latest `hit` of that mode at or before `T` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `S` (`BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS`) | int ticks | registered | registry (Emitter) | One alarm pulse per sweep (M1); not restated |
| `ALARM_DUTY_MAX_PM` | int permille | 100 to 400 (default 250) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Share of each sweep the alarm family may sound (M3 cap) |
| `W` | int ticks | 12 to 51 (32 at 250) | derived | Longest authored grid-pulse window (`attn` grid pulses and `ann`); lint L-14 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `ALARM_MIN_GAP_TICKS` | int ticks | `S div 2` | derived | Smallest spacing of two alarm-family pulses (shared across `attn` and `ann`) |
| `beacon_live` | bool | | derived from Emitter `emitters()` | `exists e in emitters() with e.kind == BEACON and e.body_id == home-ship body and e.output_pm > 0` (EM-27; CX-A15 provisional) |
| `listener_body` | body id | | Body | The body the listening avatar is on |
| `breach_enter_T`, `T` | int ticks | >= 0 | `attention.view`; Kernel | Entry tick; current sim tick |
| `last_alarm_T` | int ticks | >= 0 | presentation memory | Last pulse of either identity (`attn`, `ann`); primed on restore (below) |
| `prev(T)` | int ticks | < `T` | presentation memory | Latest alarm-family start tick strictly before `T` (either identity); the gap test is made against it, so a same-tick pair passes or fails together *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `BEACON_LIVE_HOLD_TICKS` | int ticks | 2 to 64 (default 12) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Debounce of the Emitter read, and the offset of the first mode choice (`set_beacon` runs at T+1, AT-11); a beacon whose `output_pm` toggles faster than this cannot flap the clock. `[UNV]`: Emitter's first-publish and ramp timing *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `BREACH_ENTRY_TAIL_MAX_TICKS` | int ticks | 12 to `ALARM_MIN_GAP_TICKS - 1` (default 48) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Longest pulse-0 asset including its tail (the entry event is deliberately longer than `W`); lint L-14 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

**Output Range:** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* Counting the entry event as pulse 0, a BREACH of length `B` has `floor((B - 1) / S) + 1` pulses in FALLBACK: 8 at `BREACH_MIN_TICKS` (900) and 29 at `BREACH_MAX_TICKS` (3600). On the home ship the first mode choice is ALIGNED (made at entry + 14), so the first grid hit lies 64 to 191 ticks after entry (a mean of 128 over the 128 phases of `breach_enter_T`), which gives 8 or 7 pulses at 900 (first hit at 64 or 191) and 29 or 28 at 3600; the range over both clocks is therefore 7 to 29, and it holds for a run with at most one mode switch (each further switch can shift the count by at most one; the debounce allows at most one switch per `2 * BEACON_LIVE_HOLD_TICKS` ticks). Within one mode the spacing is exactly `S`; the first pulse after a mode choice is the first hit with gap >= `ALARM_MIN_GAP_TICKS` against the latest start, so the spacing after the entry event (home ship) or after a mode switch lies in `[ALARM_MIN_GAP_TICKS, 3 * ALARM_MIN_GAP_TICKS - 1]` ticks, 64 to 191 at the registered sweep. **Precedence, stated once.** Pulse 0 always emits (by identity, not by tick order); at most one `attn` and one `ann` start per tick; a same-tick pair is one rhythm with two stacked timbres; every other start needs the gap against the latest start on an earlier tick. *At entry:* an `ann` pulse that started 1 to `ALARM_MIN_GAP_TICKS - 1` ticks earlier finishes (no cut) and overlaps pulse 0 for at most `W - 1` ticks; an `ann` hit on the entry tick sounds only if its own gap test passes (it stacks with pulse 0), an `ann` hit 1 to 63 ticks after entry is suppressed, and the first hit at least 64 ticks after entry sounds. *At exit:* the `ann` returns to the absolute grid `T mod S == 0` and its first pulse obeys the same gap test, so the phase jump is bounded to the same 64 to 191. **Duty, restated over the real lengths.** Every grid pulse asset (`attn` and `ann`) is at most `W` ticks and pulse 0 including its tail is at most `BREACH_ENTRY_TAIL_MAX_TICKS` (48, below `ALARM_MIN_GAP_TICKS`), both enforced by lint L-14, so pulses started on different ticks never overlap except the one sanctioned `ann` stack at entry. The steady cadence is one pulse per sweep, so its duty is at most `W / S` = `ALARM_DUTY_MAX_PM / 1000` (25 per cent). Two starts at least `ALARM_MIN_GAP_TICKS` apart can sit in one S-tick window only in the sweep of the entry or of a mode switch, where the windowed union is at most `(W + max(W, BREACH_ENTRY_TAIL_MAX_TICKS)) / S` = 80/128 = 62.5 per cent at the defaults: a one-off, the price of exempting pulse 0, recorded for Attention's reading of M3 (OQ-AA-41). The earlier statement that the shared gap alone bounds the union duty at `W / S` held only if every asset, pulse 0 included, was at most `W` ticks, which nothing enforced. The annunciator `ann` uses the absolute grid `T mod S == 0` outside BREACH and the listener's `hit(T)` while `phase == BREACH`. `beacon_aim(T)` is Emitter's pure function of absolute `T` and returns to its authored phase exactly at `T mod S == 0` (EM-16, EM D.10), so the aligned grid needs no `beacon_phase` read.
*(orchestrator ruling: the mode for the next pulse is chosen at the previous pulse, not continuously. A continuous choice lets a late switch stretch the gap to 255 ticks; this form makes the written 64 to 191 bound exactly true.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The first choice is the one exception: it is made at `breach_enter_T + BEACON_LIVE_HOLD_TICKS + 2`, because choosing it at pulse 0 would always read the beacon as not yet live (it is commanded at T+1) and make the ALIGNED worked examples unreachable on the home ship. Between the entry tick and `breach_enter_T + BEACON_LIVE_HOLD_TICKS + 2` no mode choice has been made; the FALLBACK `hit(T)` applies by default, which only matters on the entry tick itself, and no hit can be admitted in the 14 ticks after pulse 0 (the gap test needs 64), so the interval is otherwise immaterial *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
**Example:** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* `S` 128, `ALARM_DUTY_MAX_PM` 250, so `W` = 32 ticks (0.53 s). *FALLBACK* (a derelict, no beacon), `breach_enter_T` = 20000: pulses at 20000 (the entry event), 20128, 20256, 20384. *ALIGNED* (the home ship: the beacon is commanded at T+1, so the first mode choice is made at 20000 + 12 + 2 = 20014 and reads ALIGNED), `breach_enter_T` = 20000 (`20000 mod 128` = 32): entry at 20000, the first grid hit is 20096 (96 ticks after, >= 64, so it sounds) and later hits are 20224 and 20352. *ALIGNED*, `breach_enter_T` = 20060: the hit at 20096 is 36 ticks after entry and is skipped by the gap test; the next pulse is 20224 (gap 164, inside 64 to 191). *Mode switch*: the listener leaves the home-ship body at `T` = 20500 after an aligned pulse at 20480 (`20480 = 160 * 128`): the mode for the next pulse was evaluated at 20480 as ALIGNED, so the next pulse is 20608; at 20608 the mode is re-evaluated as FALLBACK (grid `20000 + 128 k`): the hit at 20640 is 32 ticks after and is skipped, the next is 20768 (gap 160). *Restore* at `T` = 20200 in ALIGNED: `last_alarm_T := 20096`, next pulse 20224. *Entry stack (FALLBACK at entry)*: `breach_enter_T` = 19988 and an eligible `ann` that started on the absolute grid at 19968 (it sounds until 20000): pulse 0 emits at 19988 regardless and the two overlap 12 ticks; the `ann` hit on the entry tick (gap 20) is suppressed; the next `ann` start is 20116 (the FALLBACK grid, gap 128). *Same-tick stack*: `breach_enter_T` = 20096 (an absolute-grid tick) with the last `ann` start at 19968 (128 earlier): pulse 0 and an `ann` pulse both start at 20096, and at 20224 (FALLBACK or ALIGNED) an `attn` and an `ann` pulse both start: one rhythm, two timbres. *Boundary offsets* (ALIGNED, `ann` hit at entry + d): d = 0 stacks (gap to the previous `ann` start is 128), d = 20 and d = 63 are suppressed, d = 64 and d = 100 sound.

**D.3 Release relation and bed release**

The `release_cue_relation` formula is defined as:

`release_cue_relation := (RELEASE_TICKS < ABORT_HOLD_TICKS) AND (STRAND_RELEASE_TICKS == RELEASE_TICKS)` ; bed level during a release `L_bed(t_r) = L_bed0 - BED_RELEASE_DEPTH_DB * t_r / RELEASE_TICKS` for `0 <= t_r <= RELEASE_TICKS`, the stream stopping at `t_r = RELEASE_TICKS`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `RELEASE_TICKS` | int ticks | 30 to 119 (default 90) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` ★ | Bed decay length on an abort; below 30 it is shorter than one beat gap and is not heard as a decay |
| `ABORT_HOLD_TICKS` | int ticks | registered (a multiple of 6) | registry (Attention) | The hold that aborts a tell; `RELEASE_TICKS` must stay below it |
| `STRAND_RELEASE_TICKS` | int ticks | = `RELEASE_TICKS` | Attention (not a registry row) | The presenter's strand release; equality is the requirement |
| `BED_RELEASE_DEPTH_DB` | float dB | 36 to 60 (default 48) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Total drop over the release |
| `t_r` | int ticks | 0 to `RELEASE_TICKS` | derived | Sim ticks since the abort notification (frozen by pause) |

**Output Range:** `release_cue_relation` is a boolean that must be true at data load; a false value is a boot error (lint L-9). `L_bed` falls linearly in dB from `L_bed0` to `L_bed0 - BED_RELEASE_DEPTH_DB`, then the stream stops. `TELL_REARM_TICKS` (600) exceeds `RELEASE_TICKS`, so a release can never overlap the next onset.
**Example:** 90 < 120 and 90 == 90: true, with 30 ticks of margin. At `t_r` = 45 the bed is 24 dB down. `RELEASE_TICKS` 119 is true; 120 is false (`120 < 120`); `RELEASE_TICKS` 90 with `STRAND_RELEASE_TICKS` 91 is false.

**D.4 Restore bed fade**

The `restore_fade` formula is defined as:

`g(k) = 1` if `RESTORE_FADE_TICKS == 0`, otherwise `g(k) = sin( (pi/2) * min(1, k / RESTORE_FADE_TICKS) )` ; applied to the tell bed and its tier stems only

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `k` | int ticks | 0 to `RESTORE_FADE_TICKS` | derived | Sim ticks since the post-restore notification (frozen by pause) |
| `RESTORE_FADE_TICKS` | int ticks | 0 to 180 (default 60) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Fade-in length; 0 disables the fade |
| `g` | float | 0.0 to 1.0 | derived | Linear amplitude multiplier on the bed |

**Output Range:** 0.0 at `k` = 0, 0.707 (-3 dB) at half the fade, 1.0 from `k` >= `RESTORE_FADE_TICKS`. Beats are unaffected (each channel is sufficient alone, AUD-5) and resume at the next *published* pair (D.1). *(orchestrator ruling: the equal-power sine, the systems-designer's form, over the audio-director's linear ramp; a linear amplitude ramp on a single stream reads as a dip, the sine as a swell.)*
**Example:** restore into TELL with `RESTORE_FADE_TICKS` 60: `k` = 15 gives `sin(pi/8)` = 0.383 (-8.3 dB); `k` = 30 gives 0.707 (-3.0 dB); `k` = 45 gives 0.924 (-0.7 dB); `k` = 60 gives 1.0. A pause at `k` = 20 holds `k` at 20.

**D.5 Breach-entry loudness bound**

The `breach_entry_trim` formula is defined as:

`P_bed_end = rho * P_bed` with `rho = BED_END_TICKS / (3 * 24)` for `BED_END_TICKS <= 24` (the mean power left in the 400 ms momentary window, 24 ticks, by a bed that fades out linearly in amplitude over `BED_END_TICKS` from the entry tick) ; `LU_step = 10 * log10( (P_entry + P_bed_end + P_amb) / (P_beat + P_bed + P_amb) )` must be `<= BREACH_ENTRY_STEP_LU_MAX` ; `P_entry_max = 10^(BREACH_ENTRY_STEP_LU_MAX / 10) * (P_beat + P_bed + P_amb) - (P_bed_end + P_amb)` ; `trim_dB = min(0, 10 * log10( P_entry_max / P_entry ))` applied as gain on the entry event *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `P_beat` | float, linear power | > 0 | asset metadata (K-weighted mean square, 400 ms momentary window) | Loudest beat asset played in this tell |
| `P_bed` | float | > 0 | asset metadata | The tier stems playing at the last beat (tier 1, 2 or 3) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: the earlier reading, that `TELL_MIN_TICKS` equals `TIER2_TICKS` so the earliest entry is already at tier 2, held while the tier ran on `tell_elapsed`; with the escalation clock frozen while `L < theta_HOLD` (X4) a tell can reach `breach_due_T` still at tier 1, so the proxy covers all three bed states (Attention confirms the reachable set) |
| `P_bed_end` | float | 0 to `P_bed` | derived | The bed's residual power in the post-entry window: the bed no longer stops at entry, it fades over `BED_END_TICKS`, so it enters the second window at `rho * P_bed` and not at `P_bed` (the first draft kept `P_bed` in both windows although the bed stops, which understated the step and let the stop click) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `BED_END_TICKS` | int ticks | 6 to 24 (default 12) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Fade-out of the tell bed and its stems from the BREACH entry tick (AA-20); a pure function of sim `T` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `P_amb` | float | >= 0 | asset metadata | Concurrent non-tell ambience (the same in both windows) |
| `P_entry` | float | > 0 | asset metadata | The breach-entry asset (pulse 0) |
| `BREACH_ENTRY_STEP_LU_MAX` | float LU | 4 to 12 (default 8) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` ★ | M4 bound on the step from the last beat to the entry event |
