**Output Range:** `LU_step` is 0 when `P_entry = P_beat` and rises with the entry asset; `trim_dB` is 0 or negative. When no beat played in the epoch (CALM to BREACH after a hitch skipped TELL) there is no beat to step from and no trim is applied; on a restore into BREACH the entry event does not play at all (D.1). The proxy is evaluated offline for every (beat asset, tier 1, 2 or 3 bed, entry asset) triple *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* and fails the build on violation (lint L-11); the live trim only guards authoring drift. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The bed's fade (`BED_END_TICKS`) replaces the hard stop, so the stop has no click and the post-entry window carries only the bed's residue.
**Example:** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* normalise `P_bed` = 1.0, `P_amb` = 0.1, `P_beat` = 2.0, `P_entry` = 5.0 and `BED_END_TICKS` = 12 (`rho` = 12 / 72 = 0.167, so `P_bed_end` = 0.167): `LU_step` = 10 log10(5.267 / 3.1) = 2.30 LU, inside 8. The ceiling at 8 LU is `10^0.8` = 6.31, so `P_entry_max` = 6.31 * 3.1 - 0.267 = 19.29. A mis-authored `P_entry` = 25 gives `LU_step` 9.11 LU and `trim_dB` = 10 log10(19.29 / 25) = -1.13 dB.

**D.6 Bed audibility margin and tier steps**

The `bed_margin` formula is defined as:

`L_bed,b(t) = BED_BASE_DB + (t - 1) * TIER_STEP_DB` for tier `t` in 1..3 ; `L_nc,b = 10 * log10( sum_i 10^(L_i,b / 10) )` over the worst allowed concurrent non-tell set ; requirement for each bed band `b` in {B_LOW, B_EDGE}: `L_bed,b(1) - L_nc,b >= TELL_AUDIBILITY_MARGIN_DB`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `BED_BASE_DB` | float dBr | -24 to -12 (default -18) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Tier-1 bed level in each bed band at the listener (the bed is non-positional: no distance, door or zoom term, AA-16) |
| `TIER_STEP_DB` | float dB | 0 to 4 (default 2) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Bed loudness added per discrete tier step (AA-18) |
| `B_LOW`, `B_EDGE` | bands | about 30 to 120 Hz; about 200 to 400 Hz `[PLACEHOLDER]` | AA-45 band plan | The two bands holding the bed's energy; the edge layer keeps the bed audible on small speakers |
| `L_i,b` | float dBr | | asset metadata | Level of concurrent non-tell sound `i` in band `b` |
| worst set | | | AA-16 | `RUN_MAX_VOICES` running loops at `RUN_CEIL_DB`; one TOOL-class channel loop at `TOOL_LOOP_CEIL_DB`; the ambience at `AMB_TONE_DB`; the Suit fan at `SUIT_FAN_CEIL_DB`; the berth bed (while BERTHING) at `BERTH_BED_CEIL_DB`; the INTEGRITY room tone at `INTEGRITY_TONE_DB` (NORMAL band); up to `WIT_VOICE_CAP` sustained witness classes at `WIT_CEIL_DB - WIT_BED_BAND_REJECT_DB` (a broadband hiss, wind or sizzle sits above 2 kHz and the asset spec holds its in-band energy `WIT_BED_BAND_REJECT_DB` below its total) |
| `TELL_AUDIBILITY_MARGIN_DB` | float dB | 3 to 9 (default 6) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` ★ | M2 margin |

**Output Range:** the margin is a floor, not a target; it is checked at tier 1 (the quietest state) and is larger at tiers 2 and 3. The data-load check computes `L_nc,b` from the knobs and refuses a set that cannot meet the margin; the *audible* result is a listening test with a retained capture on headphones and on laptop speakers (M2; not headless). Beats carry the evidence independently if the bed is lost (AUD-5). *(orchestrator ruling: the margin is power-summed over the concurrent set, which is stricter than the audio-director's single-loudest-bed form; the sustained witness classes are in the set only through their in-band energy, because a consequence-channel sound may not be attenuated to protect the tell, AA-11.)*
**Example:** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* `RUN_CEIL_DB` -36 with `RUN_MAX_VOICES` 4 contributes 4 * 10^-3.6 = 1.005e-3; the TOOL loop at -36 contributes 2.51e-4; the ambience, the Suit fan and the berth bed (`BERTH_BED_CEIL_DB` -44) at -44 contribute 3.98e-5 each; eight witness voices at -9 - 30 = -39 dBr contribute 8 * 10^-3.9 = 1.007e-3; the INTEGRITY room tone at `INTEGRITY_TONE_DB` -44 (NORMAL) contributes 3.98e-5. The sum is 2.4223e-3, so `L_nc,b` = -26.16 dBr, and with `BED_BASE_DB` -18 the tier-1 margin is 8.16 dB, which is >= 6 (2.16 dB spare). At tier 3 the bed is -14 dBr and the margin 12.16 dB. A POWER hum at 100 Hz placed in `B_LOW` at -30 dBr would add 1.0e-3 (sum 3.4223e-3, -24.66 dBr) and drop the margin to 6.66 dB, with almost no spare once the other loops move: this is why the hum's fundamental is held at or above 160 Hz (AA-26, AA-45). The room tone was -38 in the first draft; it is -44 so that the quiet-scene masker `L_room` leaves the D.19 relations a 3 dB margin.

**D.7 Running-timbre mapping and the INTEGRITY room tone**

The `run_timbre` formula is defined as:

`audible = (state in {OK, DEGRADED}) AND (served_pm > 0)` ; `L_run = RUN_FLOOR_DB + (RUN_CEIL_DB - RUN_FLOOR_DB) * served_pm / 1000` ; `pitch_cents = RUN_PITCH_SPAN_CENTS * (served_pm - 1000) / 1000` ; per-tick change of `L_run` limited to `(RUN_CEIL_DB - RUN_FLOOR_DB) / RUN_SLEW_TICKS` ; **the running set** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: at most `RUN_MAX_VOICES` running loops sound, ranked by the key `(near, L_listener, -node_id)` taken in descending order, where `near = 1` if the node is within `NEAR_PX` of the listener and 0 otherwise, `L_listener = L_run - A_d - A_t` is the post-attenuation level (D.9) and the id breaks ties (lowest id wins) ; **switching**: a loop outside the set replaces the weakest member only when its `L_listener` exceeds that member's by `RUN_SWITCH_MARGIN_DB` for `RUN_SWITCH_HOLD_TICKS` consecutive ticks, except that a `near` loop displaces a non-`near` one at once ; every loop starts and stops with a fade of `RUN_FADE_TICKS` (a FAILED, DESTROYED, SALVAGED or ABSENT node fades out at its `N/<id>` edge and is never hard-cut) ; an audible loop that is not in the set raises the DIAGNOSTIC `run_cap_drop` ; INTEGRITY room tone `L_tone = INTEGRITY_TONE_DB - TONE_THIN_DB[band_p]` with `TONE_THIN_DB = {NORMAL: 0, LOW: 6, DECOMP: 12, VACUUM: silent}`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `served_pm` | int permille | 0 to 1000 | Graph `node(id)` (LS-19) | Fraction of demand served; the same true value that dims the lamp (`LSG:213`) |
| `state` | enum | OK 0, DEGRADED 1, FAILED 2, DESTROYED 3, SALVAGED 4, ABSENT 5 | Graph `node(id)` | FAILED and above are silent; the failing sound is the descriptor's acoustic class (D.8), never a completion cue |
| `RUN_FLOOR_DB`, `RUN_CEIL_DB` | float dBr | -42 and -36 | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Per-source level at `served_pm` 1 and 1000, at zero distance; `RUN_CEIL_DB` is bounded by D.6 and `RUN_FLOOR_DB` by D.19 R-b (a stalled machine within `NEAR_PX` stays audible over the room tone; the first draft's -45 sat 6 dB under it) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `RUN_PITCH_SPAN_CENTS` | int cents | 0 to 600 (default 300) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Pitch sag at `served_pm` 0 (never reached: a node with 0 is not running) |
| `RUN_SLEW_TICKS` | int ticks | 6 to 120 (default 30) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Slew over the Graph's divisor-6 publish (`graph.propagate.power` -> `.atmo` -> `.failure`) |
| `RUN_MAX_VOICES` | int | 2 to 6 (default 4) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Count cap that stands in for a limiter (AA-11 forbids attenuating tier 1) |
| `RUN_SWITCH_MARGIN_DB`, `RUN_SWITCH_HOLD_TICKS` | float dB, int ticks | 1 to 6 (default 3); 12 to 90 (default 30) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Hysteresis of the running set: a listener crossing the point equidistant between two machines does not make the set flip each frame *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `RUN_FADE_TICKS` | int ticks | 6 to 60 (default 12) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Fade of a running loop entering or leaving the set or stopping on a node edge; a pure function of sim `T` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `band_p` | enum | NORMAL, LOW, DECOMP, VACUUM | Graph `compartment(cid)` (LS-19), woken by `B/<cid>` | Pressure band of the listener's compartment |
| `INTEGRITY_TONE_DB` | float dBr | -50 to -32 (default -44) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Room tone of a pressurised hull; one pool-exempt stream per listener, never creaks; lowered from -38 so the quiet-scene masker leaves D.19 R-a and R-b their margin *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

**Output Range:** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* `L_run` lies in -42 to -36 dBr and `pitch_cents` in -300 to 0; the sum of `RUN_MAX_VOICES` loops at the ceiling is `RUN_CEIL_DB + 10 log10(RUN_MAX_VOICES)` = -30.0 dBr, which D.6 keeps below the bed. `L_tone` is -44, -50, -56 dBr in NORMAL, LOW, DECOMP and silent in VACUUM; the steps change only on the band event, so the tone is a discrete function of a true band, not a continuous meter. Coolant and O2 differ in timbre (AA-26 table), not in this mapping. The cap is ranked by post-attenuation level, not by pixel distance: a stalled machine beside the listener (`near`) is never displaced by a louder one further off, and the set changes only through the switching margin, so a listener walking across the point equidistant between two machines hears one fade, not a flutter. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* The ranking key uses the unclamped `L_listener`; the D.9 running-loop audibility floor (`RUN_AUDIBLE_FLOOR_DB` inside `R_RUN_AUDIBLE_PX`) is applied after the set is chosen, so a floor-lifted far loop never outranks a nearer one.
**Example:** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* the O2 scrubber with `served_pm` 750 plays at -42 + 6 * 0.75 = -37.50 dBr and -75 cents. When the Graph publishes `served_pm` 500 the target is -39.0 dBr, 1.5 dB lower; at 6 / 30 = 0.2 dB per tick the slew takes 7.5 ticks. The node going FAILED fades the loop out over `RUN_FADE_TICKS` (12) at the `N/<id>` edge and the descriptor's HISS or HUM_DROP takes over. A compartment moving NORMAL to LOW on a `B/<cid>` key drops the room tone 6 dB; in VACUUM it is silent while the descriptor sounds (HISS, WIND, CREAK) still play. Five audible loops, the fifth at 2 dB over the weakest member for 30 ticks: it does not enter (the margin is 3 dB); at 3.5 dB for 30 ticks it replaces the weakest, both fading over 12 ticks and raising `run_cap_drop` once.

**D.8 Witness level and rate, and the anti-suppression floors**

The `witness_level` formula is defined as:

`silent if iota == 0` **only for flow-fraction descriptors** (LEAK and hole-area BREACH flow, AIRFLOW: `LSG:158`; SEVERED: `LSG:772`; SEAL_STRAIN in its GAS/CREAK branch only: `LSG:649`), where 0 is the owner's "the drip stops" (`LSG:752`) ; **the SEAL_STRAIN HEAT/TICK branch (|dT| > `HATCH_DT_FROST_MC` with |dP| <= `WELD_DP_WARN`, `LSG:649`) carries an `intensity_pm` that measures |dP|, not its own cause, so it is driven by `sev` like the no-`iota` modes and is never silent while `sev >= 1` and `medium != NONE`** ; **for the mode-5 STRUCT pre-stage and FT-30's nine ids that have no `intensity_pm` (2 SHORT, 7 OVERLOAD, 3 OVERHEAT, 9 SCRAM, 4 FREEZE, 20 to 23 ATMO_COLD/HOT/THIN/DECOMP; `FT:197`), level is driven by severity, `L_w(sev) = G_SEV1_FLOOR_DB + (WIT_CEIL_DB - G_SEV1_FLOOR_DB) * (sev - 1) / 3`, and rate by `since_steps` and FT-30's per-row LIVE predicate; they are never silent while `sev >= 1` and `medium != NONE`** `(provisional — Graph adoption of FT-30 item c)` ; otherwise (a flow-fraction descriptor with `iota >= 1`) `L_w(iota) = G_SEV1_FLOOR_DB + (WIT_CEIL_DB - G_SEV1_FLOOR_DB) * (iota - 1) / 999` ; class driver by the table below ; **floors relation, two clauses** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (a data load that violates either is refused): **(i) budget**: `FLOOR_BUDGET_DB = G_SEV1_FLOOR_DB - WIT_AUDIBLE_FLOOR_DB - TRANSMISSION_MAX_DB - FLOOR_RELATION_MARGIN_DB >= 0` (a negative budget would put the radius bound below `NEAR_PX`, which the single-inequality form let through) and `WIT_AUDIBLE_FLOOR_DB >= AUDIBLE_CULL_DB` ; **(ii) per class** `c` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: `R_AUDIBLE_PX[c] <= R_AUDIBLE_MAX_PX = floor( NEAR_PX * 2^( FLOOR_BUDGET_DB / DIST_SLOPE_DB ) )` (the log-distance law of D.9 inverted; the previous linear law gave `NEAR_PX + TILE_PX * FLOOR_BUDGET_DB / FALLOFF_DB_PER_TILE`), which is the same, up to the integer floor, as `G_SEV1_FLOOR_DB - ( DIST_SLOPE_DB * log2( max(R_AUDIBLE_PX[c], NEAR_PX) / NEAR_PX ) + TRANSMISSION_MAX_DB ) >= WIT_AUDIBLE_FLOOR_DB + FLOOR_RELATION_MARGIN_DB`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `iota` (`intensity_pm`) | int permille | 0 to 1000; defined only for flow-fraction descriptors | Graph descriptor (LS-14) | The leak's, hole's, airflow's, severed-run's or seal-strain's (GAS/CREAK branch) true flow fraction (`LSG:158`, `LSG:649`, `LSG:772`); for those, 0 is the owner's silence (`LSG:752`: a valve closed upstream leaves the descriptor and sets `iota` to 0, "the drip stops"), not a suppression. A descriptor whose mode has no Graph-defined `intensity_pm` (mode-5 STRUCT pre-stage; SHORT, OVERLOAD, OVERHEAT, SCRAM, FREEZE, ATMO_*; `FT:197`) has no `iota` and is driven by `sev` (below) |
| `sev` | int | 0 to 4 | Graph descriptor | The anti-suppression guarantee applies for `sev >= 1` and (`iota >= 1` or the mode has no `iota`) (`LSG:1110`); for the no-`iota` modes `sev` 1 to 4 drives `L_w(sev)`: -21.0, -17.0, -13.0, -9.0 dBr *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `G_SEV1_FLOOR_DB` | float dBr | -30 to -18 (default -21) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Source gain at zero distance at `iota` 1: the anti-suppression floor at the source. Raised from -24 in the review: it is the one move that gives the floors relation a 3 dB margin at the class radius (288 px under the linear law it was first set against, 576 px under the log law) and puts a sev-1 witness at that radius above the room tone (D.19 R-a) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `WIT_CEIL_DB` | float dBr | -12 to -6 (default -9) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Source gain at `iota` 1000 (bounded by D.6 through `WIT_BED_BAND_REJECT_DB`) |
| `WIT_AUDIBLE_FLOOR_DB` | float dBr | -60 to -40 (default -54) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Post-attenuation floor for a `sev >= 1` witness inside its class radius (D.9) |
| `AUDIBLE_CULL_DB` | float dBr | -60 to -48 (default -56) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | A positional cue below this is not started |
| `R_AUDIBLE_PX[c]` | int px | table, each <= `R_AUDIBLE_MAX_PX` | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` data | Per-class guaranteed radius |
| `NEAR_PX`, `DIST_SLOPE_DB`, `TRANSMISSION_MAX_DB` | px, dB per doubling, dB | 96; 4.5; 18 | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | D.9 constants used by the relation; `DIST_SLOPE_DB` replaces `FALLOFF_DB_PER_TILE` (3 dB per tile) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| `FLOOR_RELATION_MARGIN_DB` | float dB | 0 to 6 (default 3) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | The margin by which the defaults clear the floors relation (the first draft held it with equality, 0 dB) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

Class drivers (continuous mapping from Graph **true values** is permitted and wanted, P1; the ban is on `level_pm` only):

| Class (id) | Driven parameter | Range | `R_AUDIBLE_PX` default |
|---|---|---|---|
| HISS 1 | level by `L_w`; pitch `+HISS_PITCH_SPAN_CENTS * iota / 1000` | 0 to +600 cents | 576 |
| DRIP 2 | rate `DRIP_RATE_MIN_HZ + (DRIP_RATE_MAX_HZ - DRIP_RATE_MIN_HZ) * iota / 1000` (0.5 to 4.0 Hz); impulse times from the presentation hash with the descriptor id as stream | 0.5 to 4.0 Hz | 288 |
| HUM_DROP 3 | pitch sag `-HUM_SAG_SPAN_CENTS * iota / 1000`, layered on the machine's running timbre (which sags with `served_pm`, D.7) | 0 to -600 cents | 576 |
| CLANK 4 | one-shot at the descriptor's first step (`since_steps == 0`), level by `L_w`; no recurrence | one event | 576 |
| WIND 5 | level by `L_w`; stereo from `dir` (0 to 7, 8 = none) | | 576 |
| TICK 6 | rate by `iota` only for a TICK whose descriptor is flow-fraction: `HULL_TICK_HZ_MIN + (HULL_TICK_HZ_MAX - HULL_TICK_HZ_MIN) * iota / 1000` (0.5 to 3.0 Hz); the flow-zero hull pre-stage TICK and the SEAL_STRAIN thermal TICK take rate from `sev` and `since_steps` (`HULL_TICK_HZ_MIN` at sev 1, `HULL_TICK_HZ_MAX` at sev 4); the flow-zero hull TICK is the only cue at zoom k = 1 (`FT:1381`) | 0.5 to 3.0 Hz | 288 |
| SIZZLE 7 | level by `L_w` | | 384 |
| CREAK 8 (SEAL_STRAIN) | rate `SEAL_CREAK_HZ_MIN + (SEAL_CREAK_HZ_MAX - SEAL_CREAK_HZ_MIN) * iota / 1000` (0.2 to 1.0 Hz), positional at the anchor | 0.2 to 1.0 Hz | 480 |

*(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* The `R_AUDIBLE_PX` defaults in the last column are the log-law values (previously 288, 288, 288, 288, 240, 192, 144, 144 for HISS, WIND, HUM_DROP, CLANK, CREAK, SIZZLE, DRIP, TICK): the previous ratios (1, 1, 1, 1, 0.83, 0.67, 0.5, 0.5) on a 576 px base. Each is at most `R_AUDIBLE_MAX_PX` = 609 (derivation in D.9). The wider radii enlarge the candidate set of audible witnesses; `WIT_VOICE_CAP` and the D.10 stealing order still bound the voices (E-AA-78).

**Recurrence of the rate classes (stateless, AUD-1)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. DRIP, TICK and CREAK start their events by a hash predicate exactly as D.13 does, never by a countdown: with `rate_hz` from the table, `M_c = floor(TICK_RATE / rate_hz)` and `G_c = floor(M_c / 2)`; on each scanned tick `t` (at most `CREAK_SCAN_MAX_TICKS` per poll, primed to the current `T` on a restore and on a resume from pause) an event is eligible when `t - last_T >= G_c` and `H(t, HASH_SEED, descriptor_id) mod (M_c - G_c) == 0`; at most one event starts per descriptor per poll, and the mean spacing is `M_c` ticks. A change of `iota` or `sev` changes `M_c` from the next scanned tick. Example: DRIP at 4.0 Hz gives `M_c` 15, `G_c` 7, modulus 8; at 0.5 Hz, `M_c` 120, `G_c` 60, modulus 60. The shared presentation hash (`HASH_SEED`) is used, never `rng_derive`. AC-AA-144.

**Output Range:** `L_w` is -21 dBr at `iota` 1 and -9 dBr at `iota` 1000 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*, never below `G_SEV1_FLOOR_DB` for `iota >= 1`; a flow-fraction descriptor at `iota` 0 is silent; a no-`iota` descriptor at `sev >= 1` plays at `L_w(sev)` (-21.0, -17.0, -13.0, -9.0 dBr for `sev` 1 to 4) and is never silent while `medium != NONE`. A WORN descriptor (`medium` NONE, `acoustic` NONE) produces **no request** (`LSG:1111`). The request depends on the descriptor, never on light: a hiss in an unlit compartment plays at its true level (`FT:207`, E23). The Graph publishes at divisor 6, so `iota` changes at most every 6 ticks; the mapper slews level, pitch and rate over `SLEW_TICKS` but may not delay the *start* of a change beyond the poll that sees it ("audio may lead visuals"). The sustained rates of TICK (0.5 to 3.0 Hz) and SEAL_STRAIN CREAK (0.2 to 1.0 Hz) overlap the tell beat rate (0.4 to 1.3 Hz, `beat_schedule`); they are told apart by being **positional at the descriptor anchor** where beats are non-positional, and by lint L-10 and the identity table (a `wit` timbre tag never equals an `attn` tag).
*(orchestrator ruling: the audio-director's 768 px class radius with a -45 dBr floor cannot satisfy a floor relation (a 21 dB budget against 42 dB of distance cost); the radius is therefore derived from the floors and the per-class values are data below that bound. The checkpoint inequality is the load check, and the post-attenuation clamp (D.9) only guards rounding.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* Under the log law a 768 px radius costs 13.5 dB against the 12 dB budget, so the audio-director's radius is still refused, by 1.5 dB rather than by 21 dB.
**Example:** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* HISS at `iota` 600: `L_w` = -21 + 12 * 599 / 999 = -13.805 dBr (-13.80 at two decimals), pitch +360 cents. `iota` 1: -21.0 dBr. A seam weep at `iota` 80: -21 + 12 * 79 / 999 = -20.051 dBr (-20.05 at two decimals; a test comparing at the 0.01 dB convention must use these two-decimal values). The relation with the defaults *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: `FLOOR_BUDGET_DB` = -21 + 54 - 18 - 3 = 12, so `R_AUDIBLE_MAX_PX` = floor(96 * 2^(12 / 4.5)) = floor(96 * 6.3496) = **609 px** (the linear law gave 288); the HISS radius 576 gives -21 - (4.5 * log2(6) + 18) = -21 - (11.63 + 18) = -50.63, which is 0.37 dB above the required -51 (`WIT_AUDIBLE_FLOOR_DB` + `FLOOR_RELATION_MARGIN_DB`) and 3.37 dB above the floor itself (-54): the relation holds with its margin. A data row `R_AUDIBLE_PX[HISS]` = 768 gives -21 - (4.5 * log2(8) + 18) = -21 - 31.5 = -52.5 < -51 and the load is refused; a `G_SEV1_FLOOR_DB` of -30 gives a budget of 3 dB and a bound of floor(96 * 2^(3 / 4.5)) = 152 px, so the 576 px rows are refused; a budget below 0 is refused outright.

**D.9 Room-graph transmission, distance and pan**

The `transmission_attenuation` formula is defined as:

`A_d = DIST_SLOPE_DB * log2( max(d, NEAR_PX) / NEAR_PX )` (the log-distance law: 0 inside `NEAR_PX`, `DIST_SLOPE_DB` more for every doubling of distance beyond it; replaces the linear `FALLOFF_DB_PER_TILE * max(0, d - NEAR_PX) / TILE_PX`) ; `open_pm(e) = clamp( floor(1000 * area / area_max), 0, 1000 )` from the Graph `opening(edge_id)` read for a door or a hull hole `e`, **1000 if a door's state is unknown** ; `A_air(P) = DOOR_ATTEN_DB * sum over doors in P of (1000 - open_pm) / 1000 + HULL_ATTEN_DB * sum over hull boundaries b in P of (1000 - hole_pm(b)) / 1000` with `hole_pm(b) = max over hole openings e on b of open_pm(e)` and **0 where `b` has no hole** (an intact hull costs the full `HULL_ATTEN_DB`; a hole costs less in proportion to its open area and a fully open hole costs 0) ; `A_str(P) = HULL_ATTEN_DB * STRUCT_FACTOR_PM / 1000 * sum over hull boundaries b in P of (1000 - hole_pm(b)) / 1000` ; `A_t = clamp( min over room-graph paths P of A_class(P), 0, TRANSMISSION_MAX_DB )` ; `L = L_src - A_d - A_t` ; for a `wit` source with `sev >= 1` and `d <= R_AUDIBLE_PX[class]`: `L := max(L, WIT_AUDIBLE_FLOOR_DB)` ; for a running loop (D.7 `audible`) with `d <= R_RUN_AUDIBLE_PX`: `L := max(L, RUN_AUDIBLE_FLOOR_DB)` (the running-loop audibility floor, applied after the D.7 set is ranked) ; `audible = (L >= AUDIBLE_CULL_DB)` ; `pan = clamp( dx / (PAN_SPAN_TILES * TILE_PX), -1, 1 )` (horizontal offset only: no pan exists for the y axis, so a source's y position reaches the ear through level alone) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `d`, `dx` | int / float px | >= 0 / signed | derived | Distance and horizontal offset from the **listener avatar** (never the camera, its lead or zoom) to the source, both composed through `section_offset` and the body transform into one frame; **no zoom term exists** |
| `area`, `area_max` | int | `area_max` > 0 | Graph `opening(edge_id)` (published, LS-19 additions `LSG:206`; `{area, area_max, door_state}`) | True opening state; a hole in the hull is an opening with the hole's area. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| hull boundaries of `P` | int | 0 to 8 | Layout `room_at`, `segment_at` | Hull boundaries crossed by path `P`, each weighted by its hole term in the formula above (this replaces the unweighted count `n_hull(P)`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `DOOR_ATTEN_DB` | float dB | 4 to 15 (default 9) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | A fully closed door; the term falls continuously to 0 as the door opens |
| `HULL_ATTEN_DB` | float dB | 8 to 24 (default 15) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | One hull boundary, airborne |
| `STRUCT_FACTOR_PM` | int permille | 0 to 500 (default 250) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Structure conducts: structure-borne sources (the positional hull-stress layer, the seam-cut TOOL loop, Horror's ingress and egress creak) take this fraction of the hull term and **ignore doors** |
| `TRANSMISSION_MAX_DB` | float dB | 12 to 30 (default 18) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Cap on geometry: transmission may soften, never delete |
| `PAN_SPAN_TILES` | float | 3 to 10 (default 6) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Offset at which a source is hard-panned |
| `DIST_SLOPE_DB` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | float dB per doubling | 3 to 6 (default 4.5) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER — confirm at the listening pass]` | Distance attenuation per doubling of distance beyond `NEAR_PX`; replaces `FALLOFF_DB_PER_TILE`; derived below |
| `RUN_AUDIBLE_FLOOR_DB` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | float dBr | -60 to -48, at least `AUDIBLE_CULL_DB` (default -54) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Post-attenuation floor of a running loop inside `R_RUN_AUDIBLE_PX`; a running loop is never culled by geometry there |
| `R_RUN_AUDIBLE_PX` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | int px | `NEAR_PX` to `R_AUDIBLE_MAX_PX` (default 576) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Radius inside which the running-loop floor applies |

**Output Range:** `A_t` lies in 0 to `TRANSMISSION_MAX_DB`; with an unknown door state `A_t` takes the door as open (0 dB), so the **default is audible** (R10). The tell bed is non-positional and exempt from every term. Distance is in world px and is identical at every step of the integer zoom ladder (k in [1, k_auto+2]) and at every camera lead (AA-9). A cull happens only when `audible` is false, never from voice count (D.10). No `Area2D` `audio_bus_override` zones are used (the 4.7 `area_mask` default 0). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* `A_d` is 0 inside `NEAR_PX` and grows by `DIST_SLOPE_DB` per doubling of distance beyond it (4.5 dB at 192 px, 9.0 at 384, 13.5 at 768); it is monotone and has no cliff, and a witness or a running loop inside its radius is never culled by `A_d` (the floors above).
**Derivation of `DIST_SLOPE_DB` and `R_AUDIBLE_MAX_PX` (user ruling, OQ-AA-32 (b)).** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *Constraints.* (C1) the D.8 budget: `A_d(R) <= FLOOR_BUDGET_DB` = 12 dB at every class radius `R`, because the floors relation spends `TRANSMISSION_MAX_DB` (18) and `FLOOR_RELATION_MARGIN_DB` (3) of the 33 dB between `G_SEV1_FLOOR_DB` (-21) and `WIT_AUDIBLE_FLOOR_DB` (-54); (C2) D.19 R-a: `G_SEV1_FLOOR_DB - A_d(R) >= L_room + AUDIBILITY_MARGIN_DB`, that is `A_d(R) <= -21 + 39.23 - 3` = 15.23 dB, which C1 already implies (12 < 15.23); (C3) the fantasy: the loud classes (HISS, WIND, HUM_DROP, CLANK) reach `TARGET_RANGE_PX` = 576 px, 12 tiles, two rooms of about six tiles (the room size is an assumption, `[PLACEHOLDER]`: Layout fixes no room size, and the listening pass checks it). *Solution.* With `A_d(R) = K * log2(R / NEAR_PX)`, C1 at 576 px gives `K <= 12 / log2(6)` = 12 / 2.585 = 4.642 dB per doubling. `K` = 4.5 is the largest half-dB step inside that bound; 6 dB per doubling (the free-field inverse-distance law) would give `R_AUDIBLE_MAX_PX` = 96 * 2^(12 / 6) = 384 px, 8 tiles, short of C3, so 4.5 is chosen as a reverberant-steel-hull value and is `[PLACEHOLDER — confirm at the listening pass]`. *Result.* `R_AUDIBLE_MAX_PX = floor(NEAR_PX * 2^(FLOOR_BUDGET_DB / K))` = floor(96 * 2^(12 / 4.5)) = floor(96 * 6.3496) = **609 px** (the linear law gave 288). The class radii keep their previous ratios on a 576 px base (D.8), each at most 609. *Check at the HISS radius:* `A_d(576)` = 4.5 * 2.585 = 11.63 dB, so a sev-1 witness at the edge behind the `TRANSMISSION_MAX_DB` cap is -21 - 11.63 - 18 = -50.63 dBr, above the required -51 by 0.37 dB; and R-a gives -21 - 11.63 = -32.63 dBr against the required -36.23, 3.60 dB spare.
**Derivation of the running-loop audibility floor (user ruling, OQ-AA-32 (b)).** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* A running loop arrives at `RUN_FLOOR_DB` (-42) to `RUN_CEIL_DB` (-36) minus `A_d` and `A_t`. A loop at `served_pm` 1 behind a closed door (`A_t` 9) falls under `AUDIBLE_CULL_DB` (-56) when `A_d` exceeds 5 dB: at 96 + 16 * 5 = 176 px (3.7 tiles) under the linear law, which is the review's "culled about three tiles behind a closed door", and at 96 * 2^(5 / 4.5) = 207 px (4.3 tiles) under the log law, still short of a room. So the floor is the sibling of the witness clamp. *Constraints:* `RUN_AUDIBLE_FLOOR_DB >= AUDIBLE_CULL_DB` and `R_RUN_AUDIBLE_PX <= R_AUDIBLE_MAX_PX`. *Defaults:* `RUN_AUDIBLE_FLOOR_DB` = -54 (the witness floor, 2 dB over the cull) and `R_RUN_AUDIBLE_PX` = 576. *What it does and does not claim:* the clamp guarantees only that the loop's voice is not culled by geometry (a lift of at most -54 - (-42 - 11.63 - 18) = 17.63 dB, E-AA-77). It does **not** claim the loop is heard over the quiet-scene masker; that is D.19 R-g (a DIAGNOSTIC), `RUN_AUDIBLE_FLOOR_DB >= L_room + AUDIBILITY_MARGIN_DB - TONAL_ADVANTAGE_DB` = -42.23, which at the placeholders is 11.77 dB short. In the open a loop at `RUN_CEIL_DB` clears that tonal threshold only to `A_d <= 6.23` dB, that is 96 * 2^(6.23 / 4.5) = about 250 px (5.2 tiles). Whether a pump "two rooms away" is heard is therefore a mix decision for the listening pass (OQ-AA-44): raise `RUN_CEIL_DB` inside D.6 (at most the value for which the tier-1 margin stays at least 6 dB), lower `INTEGRITY_TONE_DB`, shrink `R_RUN_AUDIBLE_PX`, or lower `DIST_SLOPE_DB`.
**Example:** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* the HISS above (`iota` 600, -13.80 dBr, exactly -13.805), listener 6 tiles (288 px) away in the same room: `A_d` = 4.5 * log2(288 / 96) = 4.5 * 1.585 = 7.13 dB, `L` = -20.94 dBr (the linear law gave -25.80). Behind one fully closed door: `A_t` = 9, `L` = -29.94. The door half open (`open_pm` 500): `A_t` = 4.5, `L` = -25.44. Door half open plus one intact hull boundary: 4.5 + 15 = 19.5, capped at 18, `L` = -13.805 - 7.132 - 18 = -38.94 dBr, still above the cull (-56). The same hull boundary with a hole at `open_pm` 600: 15 * 0.4 = 6 dB; a fully open hole costs 0 dB (an `open_pm` above 1000 is closed by the clamp). A `iota` 1 hiss (`L_src` -21) at the 576 px class radius behind two closed doors and a hull boundary: `A_d` = 4.5 * log2(6) = 11.63, `A_t` = 18 (capped), `L` = -21 - 11.63 - 18 = -50.63 dBr, 0.37 dB above the required -51 and 3.37 dB above `WIT_AUDIBLE_FLOOR_DB` (-54): the clamp changes nothing and the sound is audible. Law checkpoints: `A_d` is 0 at 96 px and inside, 4.50 at 192, 7.13 at 288, 9.00 at 384, 11.63 at 576 and 12.00 at 609. The seam-cut TOOL loop (structure-borne) across one hull boundary behind a shut door: `A_t` = 15 * 0.25 * 1 = 3.75 dB, the door ignored. A door whose `opening()` read is missing counts as open. *Running loop:* the COOLANT pump at `served_pm` 1000 (-36 dBr) at 576 px behind one closed door: `A_d` 11.63 + `A_t` 9 gives -56.63, below the cull (-56) and so not started without the floor; with the floor it is requested at `RUN_AUDIBLE_FLOOR_DB` (-54), a 2.63 dB lift. At `served_pm` 1 (-42) behind an 18 dB cap: -42 - 11.63 - 18 = -71.63, clamped to -54 (a 17.63 dB lift, E-AA-77). Outside `R_RUN_AUDIBLE_PX` (576) the cull applies as before: the same pump at 700 px behind the door is -36 - 12.90 - 9 = -57.90 and is not started.

**D.10 Voice allocation, reserve sizing and stealing order**

The `voice_alloc` formula is defined as:

`RESERVED_TELL_IMPULSE_VOICES >= max(1, ceil(BEAT_TAIL_MAX_TICKS / BEAT_MIN_GAP_TICKS))` ; **voice cost of a witness** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: `V_wit(c)` = 1 for a sustained class (HISS, WIND, HUM_DROP, SIZZLE) and for the CLANK one-shot, and `ceil( rate_max_hz(c) * TAIL_TICKS[c] / TICK_RATE )` for a rate class (DRIP `ceil(4.0 * 24 / 60)` = 2, TICK `ceil(3.0 * 18 / 60)` = 1, CREAK `ceil(1.0 * 60 / 60)` = 1), and `WIT_VOICE_CAP` counts **voices**, not descriptors ; **pool invariants** (data-load): `VOICE_POOL_SIZE >= RESERVED_TELL_IMPULSE_VOICES + RUN_MAX_VOICES + WIT_VOICE_CAP` (refused if false) and `VOICE_POOL_SIZE >= D123` (a DIAGNOSTIC warning if false) with `D123 = RESERVED_TELL_IMPULSE_VOICES + HULL_STRESS_VOICES + RUN_MAX_VOICES + WIT_VOICE_CAP + SUIT_VOICE_CAP + FOOT_VOICE_CAP + HOR_VOICE_CAP + LIES_MAX(stage 3)`, and a second DIAGNOSTIC when `VOICE_POOL_SIZE < D_peak + TIER4_RESERVE_VOICES`, where `D_peak` is the recorded peak of the measured worst-case scene including transient voices (AC-AA-80) and, once recorded, supersedes `D123` (the caps cannot all be reached at once) ; allocation for a request `r`: take a free general voice if one exists ; else the victim `v*` is `argmax` over active, non-PROTECTED voices `v` with `tier_v > tier_r` of the key `(tier_v, -sev_v, -L_listener(v), -start_T(v), cue_id(v))` (highest tier number, then lowest `sev` for a witness, then quietest, then oldest, then `cue_id` lexicographic) ; if none exists the request is dropped, except a tier-1 request, which steals the non-PROTECTED tier-1 voice with the lowest `(sev_v, L_listener(v))` that is quieter than itself and raises `voice_tier1_steal` (a request quieter than every tier-1 voice is itself dropped and raises `voice_drop_tier1`) ; `PROTECTED` = the reserved impulse voices, the tell bed and tier stems (pool-exempt streams), the positional hull-stress layer, the INTEGRITY room tone (pool-exempt), `hal` voices while their count <= `LIES_MAX`, and the seam-cut TOOL loop against requests of tier >= 4 ; **tier-4 degrade order** (first dropped to last): `ia_loop` and the press pre-play, `body` and `pod` one-shots, non-terminal `asm` cues, `ia` terminals and the weld bead settle, pre-tell creaks (last, being the only Pillar 3 channel in CALM) ; every drop raises a named DIAGNOSTIC (`voice_drop_<family>`)

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `VOICE_POOL_SIZE` | int | 32 to 64 (default 32) `[UNV]` | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` ★ | Pooled players (run ruling R19 fixed 32; recommendation 48, OQ-AA-9) |
| `RESERVED_TELL_IMPULSE_VOICES` | int | 1 to 3 (default 2) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Never lent to another family; M5's "one" is the floor |
| `BEAT_TAIL_MAX_TICKS` | int ticks | 30 to 90 (default 60) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Longest audible tail of a beat asset (asset-length lint) |
| `BEAT_MIN_GAP_TICKS` | int ticks | registered | registry (Attention) | Minimum spacing of beats; not restated |
| `RUN_MAX_VOICES`, `WIT_VOICE_CAP`, `SUIT_VOICE_CAP`, `FOOT_VOICE_CAP`, `HOR_VOICE_CAP`, `HULL_STRESS_VOICES` | int | defaults 4, 8, 6, 2, 4, 1 | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Per-family voice caps; `WIT_VOICE_CAP` is the **tier-1 witness cap** (it includes the `ann` annunciator) |
| `LIES_MAX` | int | 1 / 2 / 4 by stage 1 / 2 / 3 | registry (Sanity) | Per-seat live-or-pending class-(b) lies; a `hal` voice is never stolen while the count is within it |
| `TAIL_TICKS[c]` | int ticks | defaults DRIP 24, TICK 18, CREAK 60 | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Longest audible tail of a rate-class witness asset (asset-length lint); sets the transient voices a descriptor consumes *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `TIER4_RESERVE_VOICES` | int | 3 to 10 (default 6) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Voices the pool must leave for tier-4 content beyond the worst measured tier 1 to 3 demand (DIAGNOSTIC, not a refusal) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `tier`, `L_listener`, `start_T`, `cue_id` | int, dBr, tick, id | tiers 1 to 5 | request | The sort key; deterministic, with `cue_id` ordering via sorted keys (no Dictionary-order dependence) |

**Output Range:** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* `D123` with the defaults is 2 + 1 + 4 + 8 + 6 + 2 + 4 + 4 = **31**, so a pool of 32 holds tiers 1 to 3 with **one** voice for all tier-4 content (the pool invariant and the first DIAGNOSTIC are both satisfied at 32, because 32 >= 31; the real defect is the one spare voice, so the second DIAGNOSTIC, which needs `D_peak + TIER4_RESERVE_VOICES` = at most 37 at the caps, fires at 32 until a measurement shows the scene never needs that many). `D123` is an upper bound on caps that cannot all be reached at once (stage 3, a REACH, a TOOL channel and six Suit cues together are rare), not a prediction: the pool is sized from the measured worst-case scene including transient voices (AC-AA-80), the families that degrade first are the tier-4 order above, and 48 is adopted only after the audio-thread decode and underrun measurement (OQ-AA-9). The systems-designer's plausible worst-case scene (derelict B, stage 3, a told breach with a REACH, a TOOL channel active) demanded 37 voices with the three bed stems pooled and seven running voices; making the bed pool-exempt (AA-12) and capping running loops at `RUN_MAX_VOICES` brings the same scene to 31 before transient voices. The reserve is `ceil(60 / 30)` = 2 at the defaults and 1 if the beat tail is bounded at 30 ticks. A tier-1 steal is a bug in a CI soak (the audibility cull of D.9 must fire before the count cull).
**Example:** pool 32, 31 tiers 1 to 3 voices busy and one free: a tier-4 `ia_done` takes the free voice; a second tier-4 request finds no strictly lower victim and is dropped. A tier-1 `CLANK` with a full pool steals the quietest, oldest tier-4 voice. A pre-tell creak (tier 4) finding the pool full of tier 1 to 3 voices is dropped. With `VOICE_POOL_SIZE` 48 the same scene has 17 free voices. A pool of 12 fails the refusal check (2 + 4 + 8 = 14). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *Ninth witness voice* (cap 8, eight playing, no tier-2 to tier-5 voice stealable): a sev-3 HISS requests a voice; the victim is the sev-1 DRIP with the lowest level, which is quieter than the requester, so the DRIP is stolen and `voice_tier1_steal` increments; a sev-1 requester quieter than all eight is itself dropped and `voice_drop_tier1` increments; neither happens in the CI soak of the worst-case scene at the shipped pool size. *Fifth running loop*: handled by D.7, not by the allocator, and announced as `run_cap_drop`.

**D.11 REACH resolve lead-in and the strike ordering guarantee**

The `reach_resolve_start` formula is defined as:

`start_T = reach_end_T - REACH_RESOLVE_PRE_TICKS` ; `seek = max(0, T_notify - start_T)` ; constraint `REACH_RESOLVE_PRE_TICKS <= UNFOLD_LO` ; the lead-in **reaches its end at `reach_end_T` with no impact transient and then holds its final sustain (no new energy)** until the frame poll sees `strike_T` (the strike starts at `strike_T` over the hold) or sees REACH left, for at most `REACH_RESOLVE_HOLD_MAX_TICKS` = `HORROR_RUN_TICKS - 1` = 4 ticks, after which it decays over `DECLICK_TICKS` ; if `horror.view` leaves REACH at tick `t_leave` without a `strike_T`, it stops at `t_leave` with a fade of `DECLICK_TICKS` ; the strike cue is requested by the frame poll that first sees `strike_T` and starts at `strike_T` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `reach_end_T` | int ticks | `T_reach + UNFOLD_LO` to `T_reach + UNFOLD_HI` | `horror.view` (published on the run REACH begins) | The tick at which the REACH predicate is re-checked |
| `REACH_RESOLVE_PRE_TICKS` | int ticks | 30 to 95 (default 90) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` ★ | Length of the non-percussive lead-in asset; at most `UNFOLD_LO` so `start_T` is never before the REACH began |
| `UNFOLD_LO`, `UNFOLD_HI` | int ticks | registered | registry (Horror) | Shortest and longest REACH telegraph; not restated |
| `T_notify` | int ticks | | Kernel | Tick of the notification that reads `reach_end_T` (equals the REACH entry run in normal play) |
| `seek` | int ticks | 0 to `REACH_RESOLVE_PRE_TICKS` | derived | Late-start offset, nonzero only on a restore into the lead-in |
| `strike_T` | int ticks | >= `reach_end_T` | `horror.view` (STRIKE entry run) | Published only if the predicate held; the strike is the sole impact |
| `DECLICK_TICKS` | int ticks | 1 to 6 (default 2) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | De-click length |
| `HORROR_RUN_TICKS` | int ticks | 5 | Horror (DERIVED from the divisor, HE-4), cited | Horror's run length; durations are multiples of it, so `reach_end_T` is a run tick and `strike_T = reach_end_T` in normal play *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

**Output Range:** `start_T` lies in `[T_reach + 5, T_reach + 90]`, so `seek` is 0 in normal play for every legal unfold. The lead-in is scheduled against sim `T` and a pause freezes it. **A fizzle (HE-15/HE-17; `SUBSIDE_CANCELS_REACH`; a restore that finds the predicate false) publishes no `strike_T` and so produces zero strike-cue requests**: the telegraph built and nothing struck, which is the truth. Between `reach_end_T` and `strike_T` there is at most one horror run; the strike cue is never pre-scheduled. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* Horror runs at divisor 5 and lints every unfold to a multiple of 5, so `reach_end_T` is itself a run tick and `strike_T` equals it in normal play (gap 0); the hold is defence in depth, bounding the gap between the end of the lead-in and the strike to at most 4 ticks (67 ms) if `reach_end_T` were ever off the run grid, instead of the silence a lead-in that ended at `reach_end_T` would have left. The strike's "within 1 tick (17 ms)" (`HE:1285`) is a call-order guarantee: the request is created by the frame poll of the frame whose notification published `strike_T` and the cue is positioned to start at `strike_T`; the measured offset is the audio-versus-screen delta of AC-AA-6 (OQ-AA-4, OQ-AA-37).
**Example:** `horror.step` enters REACH at `T` = 7000 with an unfold draw of 140: `reach_end_T` = 7140, `start_T` = 7050, `seek` 0. A restore at `T` = 7100 starts the lead-in with `seek` 50 and a `DECLICK_TICKS` de-click (the restore fade is the bed's only, D.4). If the predicate holds, `strike_T` = 7140 (the first run with `T >= reach_end_T`) and the strike cue is requested on that notification. If `SUBSIDE_CANCELS_REACH` fires at 7100, the lead-in stops at 7100 and is silent by 7102 with zero strike requests. With the draw at `UNFOLD_LO` 95: `reach_end_T` = 7095 and `start_T` = 7005, five ticks in.

**D.12 Outcome cue gate usage and edge detection for interaction and assembly**

The `outcome_cue_usage` formula is defined as:

`cue_plays = (last_result.T != last_seen_T) AND (T_now - last_result.T <= OUTCOME_CUE_WINDOW_TICKS)` (Assembly's `outcome_cue_gate`, cited) ; after every poll `last_seen_T := last_result.T` even when the gate is false ; after every restore `last_seen_T := last_result.T` ; for `ia_*` keys: edge on `transition_seq` and `result_seq`, only the **terminal** transition's cue plays

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `last_result.T` | int ticks | >= 0 | Assembly / Interaction | Tick of the latest result |
| `last_seen_T` | int ticks | >= 0 | presentation memory | Latest result tick already consumed |
| `OUTCOME_CUE_WINDOW_TICKS` | int ticks | registered (range 12 to 180) | registry (Assembly) | Staleness window; not restated |
| `transition_seq`, `result_seq` | int | >= 0 | Interaction `channel_view` | Counters; a strictly greater value is an edge |

**Output Range:** a boolean per poll; at most one outcome cue per result. Priming on restore is defence in depth: Assembly clears `last_result` on restore, and a producer that does not clear cannot replay a ten-tick-old result after a load.
**Example:** a result at `T` = 1000; the poll at 1010 sees the gate true and plays the cue; the poll at 1011 sees `last_seen_T` = 1000 and plays nothing. Assembly's `post_restore` clears `last_result`, so no outcome cue can follow a load; Audio's priming `last_seen_T := last_result.T` is defence in depth for any producer that does not clear (Interaction results). Cancel, abort and "nothing happened" are three terminals with three different sounds (AA-32).

**D.13 Pre-tell creak predicate (stateless, floor-gated, bounded scan)**

The `creak_predicate` formula is defined as:

for each tick `t` in `( max(scan_T, T - CREAK_SCAN_MAX_TICKS), T ]`: `eligible(t) = (phase == CALM) AND (s >= 1) AND (t - last_creak_T >= CREAK_MIN_GAP_TICKS[s]) AND ( H(t, HASH_SEED, s) mod (CREAK_MEAN_TICKS[s] - CREAK_MIN_GAP_TICKS[s]) == 0 )` ; the first eligible tick starts **one** creak, sets `last_creak_T := t` and ends the scan for this poll ; the creak's plate is `H(t, HASH_SEED, s, 1) mod n_plates(listener_section)` ; after every poll `scan_T := T` ; **on a restore and on a resume from pause `scan_T := T`** (no backlog scan) ; load check for every step: `CREAK_MEAN_TICKS[s] > CREAK_MIN_GAP_TICKS[s]`, the means strictly decreasing, each at least 1.3 times denser than the last

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `s` | int | 0 to `CUE_STEPS` | `ambient_cue_step(level_pm, s_prev)` (Attention F-24; floor `AMBIENT_CUE_FLOOR_PM`, hysteresis `CUE_HYST_PM`) | The quantised step; **the only function of `level_pm` anywhere in Audio** (lint L-2); the value and `phase` at the poll are used for every scanned tick |
| `CREAK_MEAN_TICKS[s]` | int ticks | 360 to 2400, defaults 1800 / 1200 / 780 / 480 for s = 1..4 | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Mean spacing at step `s` |
| `CREAK_MIN_GAP_TICKS[s]` | int ticks | `< CREAK_MEAN_TICKS[s]`, defaults 600 / 480 / 360 / 240 | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Minimum spacing at step `s` (no burst) |
| `CREAK_SCAN_MAX_TICKS` | int ticks | 30 to 120 (default 60) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Cap on the ticks evaluated per poll: a long hitch is a bounded loop |
| `H` | int | 0 to 2^31 - 1 | shared presentation hash (`HASH_SEED`, AT-28; Lighting LO-19), **never `rng_derive`** | Pure integer hash; a presentation stream, not a sim stream |
| `last_creak_T`, `scan_T` | int ticks | >= 0 | presentation memory | Last creak start; last tick already scanned |
| `n_plates(listener_section)` | int | >= 1 | Layout `plate_positions` | HULL_SEGMENT plates of the **listener's own section** (a locus-anchored pre-tell creak would localise the future tell, AA-22) |

**Output Range:** 0 or 1 creak per poll (no catch-up). Four discrete densities, constant within a step: mean creaks per minute `3600 / CREAK_MEAN_TICKS[s]` = 2.0, 3.0, 4.6 and 7.5 for s = 1..4 (successive ratios 1.5, 1.54 and 1.63, all >= 1.3); nothing at s = 0 (below `AMBIENT_CUE_FLOOR_PM`). The per-tick fire probability after the gap is `1 / (CREAK_MEAN_TICKS[s] - CREAK_MIN_GAP_TICKS[s])`, so the mean spacing is the table value. Creaks are never suppressed in the ticks before a possible onset (a suppression would itself predict the tell). A creak in flight at onset finishes (its tail is bounded by `PRETELL_TAIL_MAX_TICKS`). The predicate holds the fallback of AA-22 in reserve.
**Example:** step 3 (780 / 360, modulus 420), `last_creak_T` = 5000: no creak before `T` = 5360; from then each scanned tick fires iff `H(t, HASH_SEED, 3) mod 420 == 0`. A poll at `T` = 5400 after a 12-tick hitch scans ticks 5389 to 5400. A 3-second hitch (180 ticks) scans only `(T - 60, T]`. A restore at `T` = 9000 sets `scan_T` = 9000: nothing is scanned retroactively and no creak plays on load.

**D.14 Footfall cadence and gait loudness**

The `footfall_cadence` formula is defined as:

`cycle_pos = floor( odometer / (STRIDE[gait] * 256) )` (PCI D.11; the odometer is in 1/256 px) ; `frame = posmod(anim_counter, 8)` (PCI D.11) ; a footfall fires on the poll in which `frame` becomes a member of `FOOT_CONTACT_FRAMES` ; `interval_ticks = (TICK_RATE / ANIM_FPS) * (8 / |FOOT_CONTACT_FRAMES|)` ; `gain_dB = FOOT_REF_DB + FOOT_RUN_DELTA_DB * [gait == RUN]` ; `FOOT_RUN_DELTA_DB = round( 10 * log10(NOISE_RUN / NOISE_WALK) )`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `odometer` | int, 1/256 px | >= 0 | Body (PC-11, PC-14); post-collision moved pixels, diagonal weight `ODO_W` 181 | Walking into a wall adds 0; a standing avatar on a moving body adds 0; facing changes add 0 |
| `STRIDE[gait]` | int px | WALK 10, RUN 15, CARRY 5 | Controller (PC-11), cited | Base px per animation frame |
| `ANIM_FPS` | int | 12 | Controller (PC-11: "12 fps at every gait"), cited | Animation frames per second |
| `FOOT_CONTACT_FRAMES` | set of frame indices | default {0, 4} of the 8-frame cycle | animation data, owned with the art-director `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Frames at which a foot lands |
| `FOOT_REF_DB` | float dBr | -12 to -3 (default -9) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Walk level |
| `FOOT_RUN_DELTA_DB` | float dB | 3 to 9 (default 6) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Mirrors the pushed noise ratio `NOISE_RUN : NOISE_WALK` of 4 : 1 (6.02 dB), presented as a true state |
| `NOISE_WALK`, `NOISE_RUN`, `NOISE_CARRY` | int | 1, 4, 1 | Controller (PC-10), cited; registry `NOISE_PER_TICK` (walk 1 / run 4 / carry 1) | Pushed units |

**Output Range:** with the defaults `interval_ticks` = 5 * 4 = **20 ticks (3 per second) at every gait**: `STRIDE_WALK` 10 at `V_WALK` 2, `STRIDE_RUN` 15 at `V_RUN` 3 and `STRIDE_CARRY` 5 at `V_CARRY` 1 each advance one animation frame per 5 ticks. **Gait changes the level and the timbre, never the rate:** the run is -3 dBr, the walk and the carry -9 dBr (carry footfall is flat and never scales with carried mass, `IDB:991`). A slowed avatar steps proportionally slower, and a time-stepped state (stagger, work, idle, down: PC-11 steps them at 12 fps from committed tick counts, not from the odometer) fires **no** footfall, the Suit's stagger gasp being the sound of a stagger; a diagonal keeps the cadence because the odometer weights each axis. The footstep is never a function of `level_pm`, locus distance, `phase`, Horror state or sanity.
**Example:** run 120 ticks (2 s): the odometer advances 360 px, which is 24 frames, three 8-frame cycles, so 6 footfalls at -3 dBr. Walk 120 ticks: 240 px, 24 frames, 6 footfalls at -9 dBr. Carry 120 ticks: 120 px, 24 frames, 6 footfalls at -9 dBr. Over 40 ticks each gait produces exactly 2. An avatar pressed into a wall for 120 ticks: the odometer is unchanged, 0 footfalls.

**D.15 Hallucination level, breathing and start**

The `hal_level` formula is defined as:

`L_peak = HAL_FLOOR_DB + (HAL_CEIL_DB - HAL_FLOOR_DB) * leak_amplitude_pm / 1000` ; `m(t) = 1 - (HAL_BREATH_DEPTH_PM / 1000) * (1 - cos(2 * pi * t / HAL_BREATH_PERIOD_TICKS)) / 2` ; `L_hal(t) = L_peak + 20 * log10( m(t) )` ; the breathing phase never phase-locks to `tell_beat` or the alarm sweep: its normalised cross-correlation against each stays below `BREATH_LOCK_CEIL` (Filter, AC-PF-27; cited, not restated) ; start `start_T <= glide_start_T` ; relations (data-load): `HAL_FLOOR_DB + 20 * log10(1 - HAL_BREATH_DEPTH_PM / 1000) >= HAL_ACTIVE_MIN_DB` and `HAL_CEIL_DB <= WIT_CEIL_DB - HAL_BUS_REL_DB`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `leak_amplitude_pm` | int permille | 0 to 1000 | Filter `perceived(ref, viewer)` *(provisional, PF-8)* | Remaining discrepancy; the level follows it through RETURN, never below the ACTIVE minimum before CLEAR; it arrives in steps of `AUDIO_LEVEL_STEP_PM` |
| `SOUND_LEAD_MAX_MS` | int ms | 0 to 200 (Filter default 100) | Filter (PF D.15), cited | The latest the audible start may follow the first ACTIVE frame |
| `AUDIO_LEVEL_STEP_PM` | int permille | 10 to 50 (Filter default 20) | Filter (PF D.15), cited | The smallest change in `leak_amplitude_pm` the Filter re-sends as a LEVEL request; Audio slews between received levels and never extrapolates |
| `HAL_FLOOR_DB`, `HAL_CEIL_DB` | float dBr | -42 and -18 | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Peak at amplitude 0 and 1000 |
| `HAL_RADIUS_PX` | int px | 96 to 288 (default 192) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Audible radius of a world-surface `hal` leak: `R_hal(ref) = min(HAL_RADIUS_PX, legibility_range_px(ref))`; beyond it the `hal` voice is culled over `HAL_FADE_TICKS`; no effect on a card or HUD reading or on any true family (AA-36) |
| `HAL_BREATH_DEPTH_PM` | int permille | 200 to 600 (default 400) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Depth of the slow amplitude breathing |
| `HAL_BREATH_PERIOD_TICKS` | int ticks | 240 to 360 (default 300) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | The 4 to 6 s breathing period of the violet fringe, cited `ART:563` |
| `HAL_ACTIVE_MIN_DB` | float dBr | -54 to -42 (default -48) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Lowest level of an ACTIVE leak: the leak is audible at its trough |
| `HAL_BUS_REL_DB` | float dB | 3 to 12 (default 6) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Hallucination bus maximum below the witness nominal, so a lie cannot out-shout a true witness |
| `glide_start_T`, `start_T` | int ticks | | Filter request (PF-12, PF-15) | The SOUND request goes ON on the first ACTIVE frame, no later than the glide (`SN:1256`) |

**Output Range:** `L_hal` lies in `HAL_FLOOR_DB + 20 log10(1 - depth)` to `HAL_CEIL_DB`; with the defaults -46.4 to -18 dBr. For a world-surface ref the leak is audible only where the avatar-to-surface distance `d <= R_hal(ref) = min(HAL_RADIUS_PX, legibility_range_px(ref))` (default 192 px, 4 tiles; AA-36); beyond `R_hal` no `hal` voice exists, whatever `L_hal` is, and the boundary crossing fades over `HAL_FADE_TICKS`. A lie whose SOUND mode is dropped has **no request**, so no voice exists (at MVP `MODE_DROP_PM` is 0 and every lie has a sound). `HAL_FADE_TICKS` governs the OFF at CLEAR with no sting.
**Example:** `leak_amplitude_pm` 400: `L_peak` = -42 + 24 * 0.4 = -32.4 dBr; at the breathing trough (`t` = `HAL_BREATH_PERIOD_TICKS / 2`) `m` = 0.6, `L_hal` = -32.4 - 4.44 = -36.8 dBr. Relations: -42 - 4.44 = -46.4 >= -48 holds; -18 <= -9 - 6 = -15 holds. If the Filter's request has `start_T` 40998 and the glide starts at 41000, the leak sounds 2 ticks before the needle moves.

**D.16 Hull anchor derivation** *(provisional — Layout has no `hull_anchor(section_id)`; CX-A4)*

The `hull_anchor` formula is defined as:

`hull_anchor(section_id) = section_offset(section_id) + ( floor( sum_x / n ), floor( sum_y / n ) )` where the sums run over the `plate_positions` of the lowest-id HULL_SEGMENT in the section and `n` is the plate count (integer division, section-local base px composed with the offset)

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `section_offset` | int px pair | | Layout (`LY:153`, `LY:309`) | Composition offset of the section |
| `plate_positions` | list of int px pairs | n >= 1 | Layout (`LY:266`) | Section-local base px of each plate of a HULL_SEGMENT |
| `locus_section_id` | id | | `attention.view` | The only place the tell is positional (AA-17) |

**Output Range:** an integer px pair, deterministic; Layout's own read supersedes it when added.
**Example:** the lowest-id segment has plates at section-local (96, 144) and (144, 144): centroid (floor(240 / 2), 144) = (120, 144); with `section_offset` (480, -96) the anchor is (600, 48).

**D.17 Pod leg bed envelope**

The `leg_bed_envelope` formula is defined as:

`F = max(1, min(LEG_BED_FADE_TICKS, n div 2))` ; `g(T) = clamp( min( (T - t0) / F, (t0 + n - T) / F, 1 ), 0, 1 )` for `n >= LEG_BED_MIN_TICKS`, and **no leg-bed request at all for `n < LEG_BED_MIN_TICKS`** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `t0`, `n` | int ticks | `n` > 0 | `POD_DEPARTED{t0, n}`; `web.leg_timing()` (Web Map's confirmed name, CX-A19; `{t0, n}` only while LEG) | Departure tick and leg length |
| `LEG_BED_FADE_TICKS` | int ticks | 0 to 180 (default 60) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` ★ | Fade length (Web Map cites it as Audio's); 0 or `n` = 1 makes the envelope a step via the `max(1, ...)` guard |
| `g` | float | 0.0 to 1.0 | derived | Pod leg bed gain; the **tell bed is independent and unchanged in transit** (V13) |
| `LEG_BED_MIN_TICKS` | int ticks | 6 to 60 (default 12) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Shortest leg that is given a bed. For `n` = 1 the first draft's guard `F = 1` gave `g` = 0 at both ends, so the bed silently never played; the omission is now an explicit rule *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

**Output Range:** 0 at `t0` and `t0 + n`; a plateau of 1 for `n >= 2F`; on a short leg `F = n div 2` makes a triangle that still peaks at 1. If `web.leg_timing()` is unavailable on a restore mid-leg the bed starts at steady level 1.0.
**Example:** `t0` 1000, `n` 600, `F` 60: `T` = 1030 gives 0.5, `T` = 1300 gives 1.0, `T` = 1590 gives 10 / 60 = 0.167. `n` = 90: `F` = 45, a triangle peaking at 1.0 at `T` = `t0` + 45. `n` = 1 (below `LEG_BED_MIN_TICKS`): no request; `n` = 12: `F` = 6, a triangle peaking at 1.0 at `t0` + 6. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
**Berth bed envelope (AA-33 (g)).** The berth bed has no `t0` and no `n` (the web read surface carries no progress datum, `WM:300`), so its envelope is edge-driven and uses the same `F = max(1, LEG_BED_FADE_TICKS)`: `g_berth(T) = clamp( (T - T_in) / F, 0, 1 )` from the edge `T_in` at which `transit_state` becomes BERTHING, and `g_berth(T) = clamp( 1 - (T - T_out) / F, 0, 1 )` from the edge `T_out` at which it leaves BERTHING (the fade-out overlaps the start of the contact cue, which is a separate one-shot). A restore into BERTHING starts at `g_berth` 1.0. Example: `T_in` 2000, `F` 60: `T` 2030 gives 0.5; `T_out` 5000: `T` 5045 gives 0.25. The envelope is a function of a state edge, never of progress, so it cannot be read as an ETA.

**D.18 Ducking**

The `bed_ducking` formula is defined as:

`duck_db(v, T)` for a victim `v` (a sustained bed of tier >= 4 that is not exempt) follows a piecewise-linear (in dB), retriggerable envelope driven by the latest tier-2 impulse start `t_i`, with breakpoints at `t_i` (the current level `g_0`), `t_i + DUCK_ATTACK_TICKS` (`-DUCK_DB`), `t_i + DUCK_ATTACK_TICKS + DUCK_HOLD_TICKS` (`-DUCK_DB`) and `t_i + DUCK_ATTACK_TICKS + DUCK_HOLD_TICKS + DUCK_RELEASE_TICKS` (0 dB) ; an impulse that arrives before the release has finished **re-attacks from the current level `g_0`** (never from 0 dB) and restarts the hold, so overlapping impulses never sum and the depth never exceeds `-DUCK_DB` ; the gain is written to the victim voice's `volume_db` by the frame poll, as a function of sim `T`, with no bus effect *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| victim set | | | AA-11 | Ambience, the TOOL-class channel loops **except the seam-cut TOOL loop**, the pod leg bed, the berth bed (AA-33 (g)) |
| exempt set | | | AA-11, AA-12 | Tiers 1 to 3, every PROTECTED voice (tell family, `hal`), the seam-cut TOOL loop (the far-side victim's tell, AA-33), Horror's cues, UI and Voice |
| tier-2 impulse | | | AA-2 | A beat, a strike, a breach pulse |
| `DUCK_DB` | float dB | 3 to 9 (default 6) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Duck depth |
| `DUCK_ATTACK_TICKS`, `DUCK_HOLD_TICKS`, `DUCK_RELEASE_TICKS` | int ticks | 3 to 12; 6 to 60; 12 to 60 (defaults 6, 24, 30) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Envelope |

**Output Range:** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* 0 to -`DUCK_DB`. Beats never duck under music (empty at MVP). No compressor, limiter or sidechain may attenuate a tier-1 cue (M3, lint L-8). At beat spacings of `DUCK_ATTACK_TICKS + DUCK_HOLD_TICKS + DUCK_RELEASE_TICKS` (60) or more the victim returns to 0 dB between beats; at the closing spacing (`BEAT_END_TICKS` 45) the release is only half done when the next beat re-attacks, so the level cycles between -3 and -6 dB (a 3 dB flutter at 1.33 Hz); between 45 and 60 it returns partway. The duck is therefore a **shallow, fixed-depth dip per beat**, audible as a dip and not hidden; the first draft's claim that it holds continuously at `DUCK_DB` and never pumps did not follow from its breakpoints. A continuous duck for a whole tell is a mix option (`DUCK_HOLD_TICKS` at or above `BEAT_START_TICKS` minus the attack), logged for the listening pass (OQ-AA-28).
**Example:** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* a beat at tell tick 150 ducks the ambience from 0 to -6 dB over 6 ticks (to tick 156), holds to tick 180 and releases over 30 (to tick 210); the next beat at 292 finds the ambience restored. At spacing 45: a beat at tick 0, level -6 by tick 6, hold to 30, release 30 to 60; the next beat at 45 finds the level at -3 dB (halfway down the release) and re-attacks from -3 to -6 over 6 ticks. A seam-cut TOOL loop playing under the same beat is not ducked, and the beat stays audible over the other TOOL-class loops at the D.6 margin. A footfall (tier 1, Foley bus) beside an `ia_done` (tier 4, Foley bus) under a beat: only the `ia_done` voice is ducked, because the duck is per voice.

**D.19 Audibility under masking, and the peak budget** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (added by the design review: the anti-suppression guarantees held on paper but not in the ear)

The `audibility_masking` formula is defined as:

`dBr 0 = DBR0_DBFS` dBFS ; `L_room = 10 * log10( 10^(INTEGRITY_TONE_DB/10) + 10^(AMB_TONE_DB/10) + 10^(SUIT_FAN_CEIL_DB/10) )` (the quiet-scene masker; same-band worst case at MVP, per-band from import metadata at Vertical Slice) ; data-load relations, **REFUSED** (R-a, R-d, R-e, R-h) or a **DIAGNOSTIC** until the listening pass of OQ-AA-35 (R-b, R-c, R-f, R-g) : **R-a** (a witness over the room, for every class `c`): `G_SEV1_FLOOR_DB - A_d(R_AUDIBLE_PX[c]) >= L_room + AUDIBILITY_MARGIN_DB` (unobstructed; the geometry-capped case keeps only the D.8 existence floor) ; **R-b** (a stalled or under-served machine within `NEAR_PX`): `RUN_FLOOR_DB >= L_room + AUDIBILITY_MARGIN_DB - TONAL_ADVANTAGE_DB` ; **R-c** (a lie against the truth it disproves): `HAL_CEIL_DB <= G_SEV1_FLOOR_DB + HAL_OVER_TRUE_MAX_DB` ; **R-d** (the beat over the loudest transient and over the sustained set): `BEAT_REF_DB >= FOOT_REF_DB + FOOT_RUN_DELTA_DB - FOOT_BED_BAND_REJECT_DB + AUDIBILITY_MARGIN_DB` and `BEAT_REF_DB >= L_nc,b + AUDIBILITY_MARGIN_DB` for each bed band ; **R-e** (peak): `DBR0_DBFS + 10 * log10( sum over i in PEAK_SET of 10^(PEAK_DB[i]/10) ) <= PEAK_BUDGET_DBFS`, with `PEAK_SET` = one breach-entry event at `BEAT_REF_DB + BREACH_ENTRY_STEP_LU_MAX`, one `ann` pulse at `ANN_PEAK_DB`, one RUN footfall at `FOOT_REF_DB + FOOT_RUN_DELTA_DB`, `WIT_VOICE_CAP` witnesses at `WIT_CEIL_DB`, one Horror strike at `HOR_PEAK_DB`, `SUIT_VOICE_CAP` Suit cues at `SUIT_PEAK_DB`, one `hal` voice at `HAL_CEIL_DB` and the D.6 sustained set at `L_nc,b` ; **R-f** (footfall masking) is recorded, not relaxed: while a RUN footfall sounds, a sev-1 witness is masked at those instants, which is the intended trade (AA-31: stillness is how you hear more) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* ; **R-g** (a running loop over the masker, DIAGNOSTIC) `RUN_AUDIBLE_FLOOR_DB >= L_room + AUDIBILITY_MARGIN_DB - TONAL_ADVANTAGE_DB` ; **R-h** (the annunciator after its step-down, REFUSED) `ANN_PEAK_DB - ANN_STEPDOWN_DB >= L_room + AUDIBILITY_MARGIN_DB` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `DBR0_DBFS` | float dBFS | -12 to -6 (default -9) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Where 0 dBr (the Witness bus nominal peak) sits against full scale; the headroom above it is what R-e spends *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `AUDIBILITY_MARGIN_DB` | float dB | 0 to 6 (default 3) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | The margin by which a sound must clear its masker *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `TONAL_ADVANTAGE_DB` | float dB | 0 to 10 (default 6) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` `[UNV]` | How far below broadband noise a tonal machine sound is still detected; calibrated at the listening pass *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `HAL_OVER_TRUE_MAX_DB` | float dB | 3 to 12 (default 9) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | The most a lie's peak may exceed the quietest true sound that disproves it (the sev-1 witness floor); assumes a stalling machine carries a `sev >= 1` descriptor (OQ-AA-36) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `BEAT_REF_DB` | float dBr | -15 to -3 (default -9) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Authored peak of the loudest tell beat (the knock itself); the breach-entry peak is this plus `BREACH_ENTRY_STEP_LU_MAX` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `FOOT_BED_BAND_REJECT_DB` | float dB | 6 to 24 (default 12) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | How far below its total a footfall's energy sits in the bed's low band (asset spec) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `ANN_PEAK_DB`, `HOR_PEAK_DB`, `SUIT_PEAK_DB` | float dBr | -6, -6, -12 | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Authored peaks of an annunciator pulse, a Horror strike and one Suit cue *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `PEAK_BUDGET_DBFS` | float dBFS | -6 to -1 (default -3) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Ceiling of the worst concurrent peak sum, 3 dB under full scale *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

**Output Range:** a relation is true or false at data load; R-a, R-d, R-e and R-h refuse the boot, the others log. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* At runtime a development build raises the DIAGNOSTIC `master_peak_warn` when the Master bus peak exceeds -1 dBFS *([UNV]: the `AudioServer` bus-peak reads)*, and a non-zero count in the worst-case soak (AC-AA-135) is a data bug; no limiter is added (L-8). The relations bound the placeholder levels, they do not prove the mix: the listening pass (OQ-AA-35) calibrates `TONAL_ADVANTAGE_DB`, the room-tone level and the beat reference on headphones and laptop speakers before any of them is locked. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
**Example:** `L_room` with the room tone at -44 and the ambience and fan at -44 is 10 log10(3 * 3.98e-5) = -39.23 dBr. R-a: a sev-1 witness at the 576 px HISS radius is -21 - 11.63 = -32.63 dBr >= -39.23 + 3 = -36.23: holds, 3.60 dB spare (3.23 dB at 288 px under the linear law). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* R-b: -42 >= -39.23 + 3 - 6 = -42.23: holds, 0.23 dB spare. R-c: -18 <= -21 + 9 = -12: holds, 6 dB spare. R-d: -9 >= -9 + 6 - 12 + 3 = -12: holds, 3 dB spare, and -9 >= -26.16 + 3 = -23.16. R-e: entry -1 dBr (0.794), `ann` -6 (0.251), RUN footfall -3 (0.501), eight witnesses at -9 (1.007), Horror strike -6 (0.251), six Suit cues at -12 (0.379), `hal` -18 (0.016) and the sustained set -26.16 (0.002) sum to 3.20, which is +5.05 dBr, or -3.95 dBFS at `DBR0_DBFS` -9: holds, 0.95 dB spare. R-g: -54 >= -39.23 + 3 - 6 = -42.23 is false by 11.77 dB, so it logs a DIAGNOSTIC at the placeholders; that is the finding the listening pass closes (D.9, OQ-AA-44). R-h: -6 - 6 = -12 >= -36.23 holds with 24.23 dB spare. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* **The first draft's defaults fail** R-a and R-b (computed under the superseded linear law): with the room tone at -38 (`L_room` -36.23), `G_SEV1_FLOOR_DB` -24 puts the 288 px witness at -36 against a required -33.23 (2.8 dB short) and `RUN_FLOOR_DB` -45 sits 5.8 dB under the required -39.2, which is the review's finding (the witness floor 10 to 16 dB under the room tone, a stalled machine the quietest thing in the room). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*



**D.20 Annunciator step-down under sustained eligibility** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* (OQ-AA-33 resolved, option (b): keep the rhythm, step the level down after N sweeps, restore on a new descriptor or a rise in `sev`)

The `ann_stepdown` formula is defined as:

`E(T)` = the set of descriptors on the listener's body with `alarm_eligible` and `sev >= ALARM_SEV_MIN` ; `T_e` = the latest **reset event**, that is the latest tick at which `E` became non-empty, gained a member, or its maximum `sev` rose (taken from the Graph's published `since_steps` of the newest member where that read exists, and otherwise the first poll that saw the event; on a restore `T_e := T_restore`) ; `sweeps_held(T) = floor( (T - T_e) / BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS )` ; `ann_trim_db(T_p) = -ANN_STEPDOWN_DB` if `sweeps_held(T_p) >= ANN_STEPDOWN_SWEEPS`, else `0`, **sampled once at the start tick `T_p` of each `ann` pulse and held for that pulse** ; relation **R-h**: `ANN_PEAK_DB - ANN_STEPDOWN_DB >= L_room + AUDIBILITY_MARGIN_DB` (D.19)

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ANN_STEPDOWN_SWEEPS` | int sweeps | 4 to 24 (default 8) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Sweeps of unchanged eligibility before the level steps down; 8 * 128 = 1024 ticks, 17.07 s at the registered sweep |
| `ANN_STEPDOWN_DB` | float dB | 3 to 12 (default 6) | Audio `[PLACEHOLDER]` | Depth of the step; the review's example (6 dB after 8 sweeps) is the default; bounded by R-h |
| `ALARM_SEV_MIN`, `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS` | int | cited | Graph / Attention registry | The eligibility floor and the 128-tick sweep (cited, never restated as Audio values) |
| `T_e` | tick | `T_e <= T` | derived from published state | The latest reset event; rebuilt from published state after any restore (`T_restore`, conservative) |

**Output Range:** `ann_trim_db` is 0 or `-ANN_STEPDOWN_DB`; the pulse rhythm, count and timing (D.2) are untouched, so this is a level policy and not a suppression, a ducking or a dynamics process (AA-27; lint L-17). A restore never plays the alarm quieter than the state calls for, because `T_e := T_restore` gives the full level back until `ANN_STEPDOWN_SWEEPS` sweeps have again passed. The step affects the `ann` identity only; the `attn` breach cadence (AA-20) is bounded by `ALARM_DUTY_MAX_PM` and is not stepped.
**Example:** a descriptor becomes eligible at `T` 40 on the listener's body (`T_e` 40; `ann` pulses on the grid 128, 256, ..., 1024, 1152). At `T_p` 1024, `sweeps_held` = floor(984 / 128) = 7 and the trim is 0 dB; at 1152, floor(1112 / 128) = 8 and the trim is -6 dB. A second descriptor becomes eligible at `T` 1500 (`T_e` 1500): the pulse at 1536 has `sweeps_held` 0 and plays at full level (a `sev` rise does the same). A restore at `T` 3000 into a stepped-down state sets `T_e` 3000: pulses at 3072 to 3968 play at 0 dB (`sweeps_held` 0 to 7) and the pulse at 4096 (floor(1096 / 128) = 8) plays at -6 dB. R-h at the defaults: -6 - 6 = -12 >= -39.23 + 3 = -36.23, 24.23 dB spare.


## Edge Cases

*Format: "If [condition]: [exact outcome]. [rationale]". Ids `E-AA-n` are numbered in reading order. Themes: the tell and cue delivery; the alarm clock; witness and running sounds; Horror; footsteps; save, restore, pause and death; hallucination interplay; geometry, listener contexts and zoom; pool, mix and data; co-op hooks.*

### The tell and cue delivery

- **E-AA-1. If a poll observes `phase` BREACH with a `tell_beat` above `b_seen`**: BREACH_ENTER plays and no stale beat does (D.1 precedence). A knock after the breach event would read as a second breach.
- **E-AA-2. If a hitch coalesces beats 2 and 3**: one BEAT plays, for beat 3, and `seen := (e, 3)`; beat 2 never sounds and is never replayed. The floor `MIN_BEATS_IN_FLOOR` is a *published-beat* guarantee (AUD-5); a catch-up burst is forbidden (AUD-3).
- **E-AA-3. If the frame time exceeds about 500 ms across an armed tell**: the audible beat count may fall below `MIN_BEATS_IN_FLOOR`. The audible half of AC-AT-47 is therefore captured at nominal frame rate; the headless half asserts the published count.
- **E-AA-4. If a new `tell_epoch` arrives while `b_seen` is higher than the new `tell_beat` (a new tell resets `b` to 0)**: the lexicographic compare fires ONSET, which carries beat 0, and no second beat-0 cue plays. Comparing `b` alone would drop the onset.
- **E-AA-5. If a hitch skipped TELL and the poll sees CALM to BREACH**: BREACH_ENTER plays with no onset, no stale beat and no D.5 trim (there is no beat to step from, and the bed never started). Whether such a breach was fair is Attention's rule, not Audio's.
- **E-AA-6. If a pre-tell creak is in flight when ONSET arrives**: it finishes (its tail is at most `PRETELL_TAIL_MAX_TICKS`) and no new creak starts. A cut at onset would be an audible anomaly ahead of beat 0.
- **E-AA-7. If `ambient_cue_step` is 0 (below `AMBIENT_CUE_FLOOR_PM`)**: no pre-tell creak plays. If `level_pm` sits on a step edge, Attention's `CUE_HYST_PM` stops the step flickering, so the density changes only on a real step change. Audio never maps `level_pm` to anything else (lint L-2).
- **E-AA-8. If `level_pm` stays high in CALM after an abort (a rearm is pending)**: creaks continue at the step's fixed density and nothing else changes; `TELL_REARM_TICKS` governs when a new TELL may start. The step is the only readout, and it is quantised.
- **E-AA-9. If the player aborts a tell**: the beats stop, the bed decays over `RELEASE_TICKS` (90) and a release cue sounds, with no sting. `TELL_REARM_TICKS` (600) exceeds `RELEASE_TICKS`, so a release and a new onset can never overlap.
- **E-AA-10. If an abort and a breach resolve in the same poll**: Attention decides the outcome and publishes one net `ph`; Audio classifies the net change from `seen` to `now` (D.1 rows are mutually exclusive), so it plays BREACH_ENTER if the published `ph` is BREACH and RELEASE only if it is CALM with `out == ABORTED`. A breach is never masked by a release because a published BREACH is never also a published CALM.
- **E-AA-67. If a hitch spans an abort, `TELL_REARM_TICKS` and a new onset (added by the Acceptance Criteria review, QA finding F-2)**: the poll sees the new epoch in TELL against `seen` in TELL of the older epoch and plays ONSET only; the RELEASE of the older tell is not played, because its transition is not observable from the net change. The bed is re-requested by the new onset. Accepted: a 600-tick rearm plus a hitch that long is a failure of the frame loop, not of a rule, and the new tell is still told.
- **E-AA-68. If `ph` goes from BREACH to CALM with SUBSIDING skipped by a long hitch (added by the Acceptance Criteria review, QA finding F-2)**: no SUBSIDE collapse is played (the row needs `ph == SUBSIDING`), the alarm cadence stops, and `seen := now`. Nothing is replayed and no all-clear is ever sounded (AA-21); the silence is the same silence the subside would have left.

### The alarm clock

- **E-AA-11. If `breach_enter_T` coincides with a grid hit (`T mod BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS == 0`, or the FALLBACK grid, which always has a hit at entry)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: one `attn` pulse plays. At most one `attn` pulse starts on a tick and the entry event (pulse 0) is that pulse by identity: it is not suppressed by a gap against itself and the grid hit is not a second pulse. (The earlier reading, "`ALARM_MIN_GAP_TICKS` suppresses the grid hit at gap 0", would have suppressed pulse 0 itself under the D.2 formula as it was then written.) An `ann` hit on the same tick stacks with pulse 0 when its own gap test passes. ALIGNED cannot be in force at pulse 0 (the beacon is commanded at T+1, a derelict has none), so the coincidence seen in play is with the `ann` grid, or, in ALIGNED, from the second interval on.
- **E-AA-12. If the first aligned grid hit lands 36 ticks after entry**: it is skipped (under `ALARM_MIN_GAP_TICKS`) and the next pulse is the following hit, 164 ticks after entry, inside the 64 to 191 bound (CX-A14: accepted, because the entry event is itself pulse 0). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (Home ship: ALIGNED is chosen at entry + 14, before this hit; the choice does not change the arithmetic.)
- **E-AA-13. If the listener changes body mid-BREACH (a pod hop, a detach)**: the clock for the *next* pulse is chosen at the last pulse, so the spacing after the switch stays within 64 to 191 ticks (D.2 example: 20608 then 20768). A mid-sweep switch can never produce two stings inside a second.
- **E-AA-14. If the home-ship is `NOT_LIVE`, the beacon is commanded a tick late or is absent, or reduce-flashing is on**: the alarm uses the FALLBACK or ALIGNED clock by the `beacon_live` rule and its content is unchanged. Reduce-flashing never reduces audio (M1; FT E20, E21). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* A beacon whose `output_pm` toggles faster than `BEACON_LIVE_HOLD_TICKS` never changes the clock (E-AA-70).
- **E-AA-15. If BREACH_ENTER, an `alarm_eligible` annunciator pulse and a Horror strike land in the same poll** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: all three are requested in the same frame in the order `ann` (tier 1), strike (tier 2), BREACH_ENTER (tier 2), on free and reserved voices. Pulse 0 always emits; the `ann` pulse stacks with it when its own gap test passes (D.2), and when it does not, the earlier `ann` pulse finishes and no second `ann` starts. A same-tick `attn` and `ann` start is one rhythm with two stacked timbres, and the steady union duty stays within `ALARM_DUTY_MAX_PM` because every grid asset is at most `W` ticks (L-14); the first sweep after entry is bounded separately (D.2, OQ-AA-41).
- **E-AA-16. If a descriptor becomes `alarm_eligible` outside BREACH**: its witness class and the Suit's body cues sound on the poll that sees it, and the `ann` pulse waits for the next `T mod BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS == 0` tick (at most one sweep). This is not a suppression: the annunciator is presentation layered on a consequence sound that was never delayed (CX-A18). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The `ann` sounds only for a listener on the descriptor's body (AA-27); a listener elsewhere hears nothing of it.
- **E-AA-17. If a descriptor stops being `alarm_eligible` mid-pulse**: the pulse in progress completes (at most `W` ticks) and no further pulse starts. A cut mid-pulse would be an audible anomaly. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* This includes an `ann` pulse in flight when BREACH is entered: it finishes and overlaps pulse 0 by at most `W - 1` ticks (E-AA-71).
- **E-AA-18. If SUBSIDING returns to CALM**: nothing plays, never an all-clear. STRESS can outlive SUBSIDING (`AT:1210`), and the failing-loop `wit` sounds carry the ongoing wrongness (AUD-10).
- **E-AA-70. If the Emitter's BEACON `output_pm` toggles every few ticks during BREACH** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the debounced read `beacon_live_f` changes only after the raw read has differed for `BEACON_LIVE_HOLD_TICKS` (12) ticks, so a toggle faster than that changes nothing, and a toggle slower than that changes the clock at most once per `2 * BEACON_LIVE_HOLD_TICKS` ticks, only at a pulse boundary and with the 64 to 191 gap bound. Two sweeps of silence from a flapping clock cannot occur.
- **E-AA-71. If an `ann` pulse is in flight when BREACH is entered, or BREACH is left while an `ann` pulse is in flight** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: it finishes (no cut, E-AA-17). At entry pulse 0 emits regardless and the two overlap by at most `W - 1` ticks; the `ann` hit on the entry tick, and any hit within 63 ticks after it, is suppressed unless its own gap test passes. At exit the `ann` returns to the absolute grid and its first pulse obeys the gap test against the last alarm-family start, so the phase jump at either boundary is bounded to 64 to 191 ticks.
- **E-AA-72. If a descriptor is `alarm_eligible` on the home ship while the listener is on a derelict** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: no `ann` is requested for that listener (AA-27 listener scope); the descriptor's witness class, if inside the listener's body, is unaffected. The alarm family the derelict listener hears is the `attn` cadence of a BREACH only.

### Witness and running sounds

- **E-AA-19. If a LEAK descriptor's `intensity_pm` is 0 because its valve is closed upstream**: its class is silent while the descriptor persists. This is the owner's semantics (`LSG:752`), not a suppression; the `LSG:1110` lint is read with that carve-out. The carve-out covers the flow-fraction descriptors of D.8 only (LEAK, hole-area BREACH flow, AIRFLOW, SEVERED, and SEAL_STRAIN in its GAS/CREAK branch): a no-`iota` descriptor (mode-5 STRUCT pre-stage, SHORT, OVERLOAD, OVERHEAT, SCRAM, FREEZE, ATMO_*) and the SEAL_STRAIN HEAT/TICK branch (whose `intensity_pm` measures |dP|, not its thermal cause, `LSG:649`) are never silenced this way (D.8, L-13).
- **E-AA-20. If a `sev >= 1` hiss at `intensity_pm` 1 is 6 tiles away behind two closed doors and a hull boundary** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: `A_t` is capped at `TRANSMISSION_MAX_DB`, `L` = -51 dBr, 3 dB above `WIT_AUDIBLE_FLOOR_DB` (-54), and it is audible (D.9 example). Geometry softens a witness; it never removes one inside its class radius.
