- **E-AA-21. If the compartment has zero lit emitters (a dark room)**: the hiss still plays at its full true level and no mist is drawn. This is expected, not a bug (`FT:207`, E23): the request depends on the descriptor, never on light.
- **E-AA-22. If a descriptor is WORN (`medium` NONE, `acoustic` NONE)**: no request exists and no sound plays (`LSG:1111`; lint L-7).
- **E-AA-23. If a door is animating (`area / area_max` = 0.4)**: the door term is `9 * 0.6` = 5.4 dB and follows the true area continuously. If `opening(edge_id)` is not yet published, the door is treated as **open** (R10): a guessed muffle that hid a real hiss would be a suppression by default. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The Graph now publishes `opening(edge_id)` (`LSG:206`); open-by-default remains the fallback for an edge the read does not return. A hole in the hull is read the same way and lowers the hull term in proportion to its open area (D.9).
- **E-AA-24. If the listener's own compartment is in the VACUUM pressure band (`compartment(cid).band_p`)**: the INTEGRITY room tone is silent, and airborne sources reach the listener by the structure-borne model (`A_str`, contact with the suit and hull) instead of `A_air`; no witness is removed (P1). The timbre of a suit-conducted sound is the sound-designer's. *(orchestrator ruling: a deterministic transmission class rather than a silent mute; OQ-AA-11.)*
- **E-AA-25. If a running node goes FAILED, DESTROYED, SALVAGED or ABSENT**: its running timbre stops at the `N/<id>` edge with no completion cue; the descriptor's acoustic class (the failing variant) takes over. "The reward is the absence" (`FT` E7).
- **E-AA-26. If more than `RUN_MAX_VOICES` running loops are audible** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the set is the `RUN_MAX_VOICES` highest by the D.7 key `(near, post-attenuation level, lowest node id)`; a loop outside the set replaces the weakest member only after exceeding it by `RUN_SWITCH_MARGIN_DB` (3) for `RUN_SWITCH_HOLD_TICKS` (30) ticks, except that a loop within `NEAR_PX` displaces at once; every entry and exit fades over `RUN_FADE_TICKS` (12); a loop left out raises `run_cap_drop`. The cap stands in for a limiter because a limiter on tier 1 is forbidden (AA-11), and the INTEGRITY room tone is a separate pool-exempt stream. A listener crossing the point equidistant between two machines hears one fade after the hold, never a flutter.
- **E-AA-27. If the player plays with the screen dark (by sound alone)**: every consequence sound is present (witness at anchors, running loops, tell bed and beats). This strategy is protected, not degenerate (`LSG:800`).

### Horror

- **E-AA-28. If a REACH fizzles (the predicate fails at the last tick, the phase has returned to CALM, `SUBSIDE_CANCELS_REACH`, or a restore finds the predicate false)**: no `strike_T` is published, so **zero strike-cue requests** are made; the non-percussive lead-in ends at `reach_end_T` (or stops over `DECLICK_TICKS` if cancelled earlier). A pre-committed impact would be a true-family sound asserting a strike that never happened.
- **E-AA-29. If the REACH is the shortest legal unfold (`UNFOLD_LO` 95)**: the lead-in (`REACH_RESOLVE_PRE_TICKS` 90) starts 5 ticks in with `seek` 0; the constraint `REACH_RESOLVE_PRE_TICKS <= UNFOLD_LO` guarantees `start_T` is never before the REACH began.
- **E-AA-30. If the strike lands (`strike_T` published)**: the strike cue is requested by the frame poll of the frame whose notification published `strike_T` and starts at `strike_T` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*, positional at the seats and hull segments hit; the lead-in already ended at `reach_end_T`. Strike one is witnessed by the sound; it is not pre-announced by an impact.
- **E-AA-31. If a hallucination hides a real horror's body**: its floor-borne creep keeps sounding (`CON:243`, `SN:306`); Audio never silences a real entity because the viewer is lied to. Phantom horrors (VS-A) use `hal` only.
- **E-AA-32. If Horror's marks, L3 dips or disturbances change**: nothing plays; they are silent facts (AA-28). There is no proximity cue and no state stinger.

### Footsteps

- **E-AA-33. If the avatar walks into a wall**: the Controller's odometer adds 0 (post-collision pixels only, PCI D.11), so there are 0 footfalls; facing changes also add nothing.
- **E-AA-34. If the avatar stands still on a moving body (a pod in transit, a section rotating during a weld)**: 0 footfalls, because the odometer counts ship-local moved pixels, not frame motion.
- **E-AA-35. If the player walks, runs or carries for 40 ticks**: each gait produces exactly 2 footfalls (a footfall every 20 ticks at every gait); only the level (-9, -3, -9 dBr) and timbre differ. A test that expects a faster run cadence is wrong; the run is told by level and the pack rattle.
- **E-AA-36. If the avatar carries a heavy item**: the footfall is flat and never scales with carried mass (`IDB:991`). A sound that encoded a hidden value would be a meter by ear.
- **E-AA-37. If the avatar is in a slowed zone**: the odometer advances slower, so footfalls come proportionally slower at the same level. **If the avatar is staggered, working, idle or down** (the time-stepped states of PC-11): no footfall fires, because no odometer cycle is crossing a contact frame; the Suit's stagger gasp (AA-30) is the single sound of a stagger. One sound per true state.

### Save, restore, pause and death

- **E-AA-38. If a restore lands mid-TELL**: no onset or beat replays; the bed fades in over `RESTORE_FADE_TICKS`; beats resume at the next *published* pair; `seen := now`.
- **E-AA-39. If a restore lands mid-BREACH**: the entry event does not replay; `last_alarm_T` is primed to the latest grid hit at or before `T`; pulses resume on the next grid hit with the gap rule.
- **E-AA-40. If a restore lands in CALM with `tell_outcome` ABORTED**: nothing plays (AUD-6). If it returns an *earlier* epoch than `e_seen`, `seen := now` with no cue, so the next real onset (a higher epoch) still plays and is never mistaken for a false calm.
- **E-AA-41. If a restore lands mid-lie**: the SOUND leak resumes with the lie at full leak level on the first frame; the Filter keeps no clean frame (PF-33), so a restore must not briefly drop a leak mode.
- **E-AA-42. If a restore lands inside a REACH lead-in or in STRIKE**: the lead-in starts with `seek = T_notify - start_T` and only a `DECLICK_TICKS` de-click (AA-5); in STRIKE the strike cue does not replay (the strike was an entry-run fact, E-HE-38).
- **E-AA-43. If a restore finds a result 10 ticks old (`last_result.T` = 4990 at `T` = 5000; a producer that does not clear)**: Assembly's `post_restore` clears `last_result`, so no outcome cue can follow a load; Audio's priming `last_seen_T := last_result.T` is defence in depth for any producer that does not clear (Interaction results), and no weld-done or terminal cue plays after the load (D.12).
- **E-AA-44. If the game is paused**: sim `T` is held, so every sim-derived sound pauses (the scheduled lead-in is frozen, the bed holds, `k` and `t_r` hold) and UI sounds continue; the sim is never paused by audio (CR-11). On resume the creak `scan_T := T`.
- **E-AA-45. If the player dies and the death stub restores**: no sound and no screen (`SL:23`). The stub is a restore like any other; `restore_reason` is not read and no content depends on it. Saving plays no sound.

### Hallucination interplay

- **E-AA-46. If the sanity stage is 0, 1, 2 or 3**: the request list of every TRUE family is bit-identical for the same published sim state (a property test); only the `hal` list differs, by Filter requests. No mix, EQ, ducking or ambience parameter reads the local player's own sanity (AA-38).
- **E-AA-47. If the Filter drops the SOUND leak mode of a lie at stage 2 or 3**: that lie has no audio request at all, and still keeps two leak modes (PF-15, never below two). At MVP `MODE_DROP_PM` is 0 and every lie has a sound.
- **E-AA-48. If more than `LIES_MAX` lies reach Audio**: the extras are dropped with a DIAGNOSTIC (a Filter bug), and no PROTECTED voice is stolen to make room.
- **E-AA-49. If the machine under a lying instrument has a real fault**: the machine's real hiss or hum still plays, because a lie can only add a fringed sound and never replace a true one. This is the falsifier (P4).
- **E-AA-50. If a lie is debunked, expires or is invalidated**: the three look identical to the ear: the level follows the remaining discrepancy, then OFF over `HAL_FADE_TICKS`, with no sting, no verdict and no relief cue (PF-12).
- **E-AA-51. If the Filter declines to emit audio requests**: MVP class-(b) lies play no reading-lie sound and HR-5's sound mode is unmet; that is a P1/P4 finding against the Perception Filter, not something Audio designs around (creative-director at CD-GDD-ALIGN: the Filter must emit audio requests for every MVP class-(b) lie while `MODE_DROP_PM` is 0; a refusal reopens PF-20, not AA-35). Audio keeps no reading-lie sound plus the CONFLICT diagnostic (CX-A3).
- **E-AA-69. If the viewer stands beyond `R_hal` of a lying world-surface instrument whose machine has a real fault or a normal running sound**: the `hal` leak is silent (culled, no voice), and the machine's true witness or running sound is heard at its ordinary level and audible floor; stepping inside `R_hal` fades the leak in over `HAL_FADE_TICKS` with no sting and no change to any true request. A card or HUD reading is non-positional and is not radius-gated. (Added at CD-GDD-ALIGN; the id is out of reading order.)
- **E-AA-52. If a viewer asks for the sound of another player's lie**: none is rendered; the Filter returns the honest record for any remote `PlayerId` (PF-10), and `hal` requests are `OWNER_PRIVATE`.

### Geometry, listener contexts and zoom

- **E-AA-53. If the zoom step changes (any k in [1, k_auto+2]) or the camera leads**: every request, gain and pan is unchanged, because attenuation and pan use world px from the avatar and no zoom term exists. A property test sweeps k.
- **E-AA-54. If the listener is on a derelict**: the alarm uses the FALLBACK clock and audio carries the breach alone (derelicts have no beacon, AUD-10); the bed, beats and witness classes are identical to the home-ship.
- **E-AA-55. If the listener is in a pod in transit (LEG)**: the tell bed is non-positional and unchanged; the pod leg bed follows D.17 and ducks under beats; positional sources are composed through the pod body's transform. **If a BERTHING runs while a TELL is active** (the hop and the berth are allowed during a tell, WM-26): the berth bed and the tell bed play together, the berth bed is a tier-4 victim and ducks under each beat, the tell bed is unchanged and L-10 keeps their timbres disjoint.
- **E-AA-56. If a seam is cut and the seam is closed**: the seam-cut TOOL loop is structure-borne and audible on both sides (door term ignored); it is never ducked and not stealable by tier >= 4 requests, because it is the far-side victim's tell (ASK AU1).
- **E-AA-57. If a source sits on a body that moves relative to the listener's body**: both anchors are composed in world px each frame, so a hull-mounted hiss stays on its hull while the ship drifts.

### Pool, mix and data

- **E-AA-58. If the voice pool is exhausted** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: tier 4 and 5 requests drop first, in the tier-4 order of D.10 (`ia_loop` and press pre-play, then `body`/`pod`, then non-terminal `asm`, then `ia` terminals and the bead settle, pre-tell creaks last); a tier-1 request first steals by the D.10 key (highest tier number of 2 to 5, then lowest `sev`, then quietest, then oldest); only if no such victim exists does it steal the tier-1 voice with the lowest `(sev, level)` that is quieter than itself and raise `voice_tier1_steal` (a bug in a CI soak), and a tier-1 request quieter than every tier-1 voice is itself dropped with `voice_drop_tier1`; the tell family, the reserved impulse voices, the room tone, the bed and in-budget `hal` voices are never stolen. A ninth witness voice, a fifth running loop and a DRIP at its maximum rate (two voices) are each covered by AC-AA-136.
- **E-AA-59. If a data file sets `RELEASE_TICKS` to 120 or more, or `STRAND_RELEASE_TICKS` differs from `RELEASE_TICKS`**: `release_cue_relation` is false and the boot refuses the file.
- **E-AA-60. If a data file gives a witness class a radius above `R_AUDIBLE_MAX_PX` (768 px against a derived 609), a `CREAK_MEAN_TICKS[s]` at or below `CREAK_MIN_GAP_TICKS[s]`, a `REACH_RESOLVE_PRE_TICKS` above `UNFOLD_LO`, or a pool below `RESERVED_TELL_IMPULSE_VOICES + RUN_MAX_VOICES + WIT_VOICE_CAP`**: the load is refused with the named relation. A silent clamp would hide a P1 break. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The refusals are for **global relations** whose violation would hide or delay a true sound (P1, P2). A **per-reference** bound is different: `R_hal(ref) = min(HAL_RADIUS_PX, legibility_range_px(ref))` is a runtime `min()` by design (AA-36), so a `HAL_RADIUS_PX` above one surface's legibility range is not a data fault; it is applied as the `min()` and logged once as a DIAGNOSTIC, never silently. This is the reading AC-AA-129 tests, and it removes the apparent clash between that criterion (clamp) and this case (refuse).
- **E-AA-61. If the worst-case concurrent set cannot meet `TELL_AUDIBILITY_MARGIN_DB` (D.6)**: the data load is refused; the listening test then verifies the shipped set. A POWER hum authored at 100 Hz fails the lint, which is why its fundamental is held at or above 160 Hz.
- **E-AA-62. If any bus is muted or the audio driver is the headless dummy**: `state_hash` is unchanged (AUD-9) and every cue request is still produced, since requests are a pure function of published state.
- **E-AA-63. If an asset is tagged with two families or two assets share a sound id across families**: the identity-table lint fails the build (AA-6 a, b, h); `ann` stays disjoint from `attn` even though both run on the alarm grid.

### Co-op hooks (VS-B, provisional)

- **E-AA-64. If a listener is dead *(provisional — Co-op Session undesigned)***: no bed request is addressed to that seat (`ALL_LIVING`); the tell remains audible to every living player.
- **E-AA-65. If two crewmates both have `tell_on` (VS-B)**: two P1 cues play from their suits with different hash phases; neither plays on its own source's client (no self-sign, SN-14) and neither is altered by any listener's sanity.
- **E-AA-66. If a crewmate stands in a room two doors away**: their footsteps and suit sounds attenuate by D.9 like any airborne sound; true sounds are identical on every client, so "do you hear that too?" is a valid cross-check.


### Cases added by the 2026-10-27 user rulings *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

- **E-AA-73. If a restore lands while the klaxon is stepped down (D.20)**: `T_e := T_restore`, so the first `ANN_STEPDOWN_SWEEPS` sweeps after the load play at full level, and the step-down returns only if the descriptor is still eligible and unchanged; a restore never plays a quieter alarm than the state calls for (AA-5, AA-27).
- **E-AA-74. If the eligible set changes while an `ann` pulse is in flight**: the trim sampled at the pulse start holds for that pulse (no mid-pulse level change and so no click); the reset event takes effect at the next pulse start. A set that empties and refills is a new run (`T_e` reset).
- **E-AA-75. If a restore, a pause or a hitch lands while the escalation is frozen (X4)**: Audio re-primes `tier` and `(tell_epoch, tell_beat)` from the published view (AA-4, AA-5); a restore into a TELL whose escalation is frozen plays the bed at the published tier with the D.4 fade and replays no tier crossfade. A published tier never falls inside a TELL, so Audio has no tier-down transition; an observed drop is treated as a restore re-prime, not a cue (AC-AA-150).
- **E-AA-76. If the haptic beat pulse is on and a hitch coalesces beats, a restore lands in TELL, or no controller is present** (AA-42): exactly one pulse for the net new `(tell_epoch, tell_beat)`, matching the audio beat; none for beats skipped inside the hitch and none on a restore (like the audio, no catch-up); with no controller, or `haptic_beat_pulse` off, nothing is called and no error is raised. On the headless dummy driver the request is still produced in the same list as the audio requests.
- **E-AA-77. If a running loop sits inside `R_RUN_AUDIBLE_PX` behind the 18 dB geometry cap** (D.9): its level is the floor (-54 dBr), so two loops of different `served_pm` behind the same geometry can sound equal; the loop is present (its existence is true state) and the level difference returns as the listener approaches; beyond `R_RUN_AUDIBLE_PX` the D.9 cull applies as before.
- **E-AA-78. If the wider witness radii of the log law (D.8) add candidate witnesses**: the audible set grows (a leak twelve tiles off now qualifies); `WIT_VOICE_CAP` and the D.10 stealing order still bound the voices, nothing is added to the cap, and the D.10 worst case is recounted under the new radii at the voice spike (AC-AA-80).


## Dependencies

Status vocabulary: **designed** (a GDD on disk with header Status "Designed"); **in-flight** (a draft on disk, header Status "In Design"); **undesigned-provisional** (no GDD on disk; this GDD states a request and re-checks it when the neighbour lands). Glob of `design/gdd/` at the time of writing: 23 files (re-read 2026-10-27: 26 GDDs, all headed `Designed`). "Names Audio?" answers the bidirectional-consistency rule: does the counterpart's own GDD name Audio & Tells in its Dependencies or Interactions, and with what wording. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

| System | Direction | Hard or soft | Interface | Names Audio? | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Life-Support Graph | Audio depends on it | **Hard** (index) | descriptors (`acoustic`, `intensity_pm`, `sev`, `alarm_eligible`, `anchor`), `node()`, `loop_component()`, `failures()`, `compartment(cid).band_p`, the keys `N/<id>`, `L/<loop>/<anchor>`, `C/<cid>`, `B/<cid>`, `F/<id>` | yes, `LSG:270`, `LSG:865` ("provisional"; hum/wind wording stale, RF-5) | designed |
| Attention System | Audio depends on it | **Hard** (index) | `attention.view` fields of AT-16; `ambient_cue_step`; `beat_schedule` | yes, `AT:375`, `AT:977` ("provisional"; "no file", RF-1) | designed |
| Horror Entities | Audio depends on it | **Hard** (index `IDX:202`) | `horror.view` | yes, `HE:374`, `HE:926` ("undesigned"; `AUD-13`, RF-2) | designed |
| Sanity & Hallucination Rules | Audio depends on it | **Hard** in the index; "Soft" in `SN:490`, `SN:968` (CX-A6) | `sanity_view` (`tell_on` only, dormant at MVP); SN-25 authorisation | yes, `SN:490-496`, `SN:968` ("Soft downstream", RF-3) | designed |
| Command & Simulation Layer | Audio depends on it | **Hard** | `PRESENTATION` subscription, P5 notify, `read_view`, post-restore notification | yes (the subscriber table names audio nodes) | designed |
| **Perception Filter** | Audio depends on it | **Hard for the lie family**; soft otherwise (the Filter agrees, `PF:559`, `PF:1155`) | audio request `(viewer, ref or position, intent, class)`; `perceived()` leak modes; `FILTER_AUDIO_CLASSES` | yes, `PF:559`, `PF:1155` | **designed** |
| Suit & Equipment | Audio depends on it | Soft (event source) | cue-state read-view fields | yes, `SE:474` | designed |
| Player Controller & Input | Audio depends on it | Soft | gait class, position, `channel_view` | yes, `PCI:211` row 11 | designed |
| Interaction System | Audio depends on it | Soft | `ia_*` keys, `transition_seq`, `result_seq` | yes, `IA:470` | designed |
| Modular Ship Assembly | Audio depends on it | Soft | `last_result`, `outcome_cue_gate`, seam-cut loop state | yes, `MSA:509` | designed |
| Ship Body & Interior Physics | Audio depends on it | Soft | `BODY_*` events, body transform | yes, `BDY:301` | designed |
| Web Map & Transit | Audio depends on it | Soft | `POD_*`, `BERTHED`, `UNBERTHED`, `DERELICT_*`, `transit_state` (BERTHING), `web.leg_timing()` (name confirmed, CX-A19) | yes, `WM:421`, `WM:892` | designed |

| Graph `opening(edge_id)` | Audio depends on it | Soft | door and hull-hole transmission (D.9); an unknown door stays open as the fallback | Layout's ask (`LY:426`); published in the Graph's LS-19 additions (`LSG:206`) | designed (published) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query | Audio depends on it | Soft | `E/<id>` keys; `emitters()`; `beacon_aim(T)` | yes, `EM:338` | designed |

| In-World Instruments & Inspect View | both | Soft | the Filter's `perceived()` SOUND leak; UI-bus `UI_INSTRUMENT_CARD_OPEN` / `UI_INSTRUMENT_CARD_CLOSE` on `card_opened` / `card_closed`; a sound tell per IW-12 stress source (I-C row) | yes, `IW:397`, `IW:501` | designed (in-world-instruments-and-inspect-view.md) |
| Chart Table / menus | both | Soft | UI bus; pause behaviour | yes, `CT:393`, `CT:524`, `CT:570` (re-read: its own file now cites this GDD's rows by `AA:` line, which drift; cite by rule id, RF-20) | designed *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| Debug Overlay (True Values) | it depends on Audio | Soft (DIAGNOSTIC) | identity table, voice counters | yes (debug-overlay-true-values.md) | designed (debug-overlay-true-values.md) |
| Chat, Voice & Pings (VS-B) | both | Soft | `Voice` bus | no file | undesigned-provisional |
| Co-op Session (VS-B) | both | Soft | `listener_scope` | no file | undesigned-provisional |
| **Accessibility Settings** (Alpha) | **it depends on Audio** | **Hard** (reverse edge, `IDX:113`, `IDX:209`) | the boundaries of AA-41 to AA-43 | no file | undesigned-provisional |
| Settings & Input Rebinding (Alpha) | it depends on Audio | Soft | the World-group slider rule | no file | undesigned-provisional |

**Downstream dependents among the systems designed in this run:** none. The only reverse hard edge in the index is Accessibility Settings. **Cycle check:** no cycle exists. Audio is a pure sink of the dependency graph (it registers no hook, block, mutator or command, so nothing in the simulation can depend on it), and the single soft cycle in the neighbourhood (Sanity "Soft downstream" versus an index hard edge) is a wording mismatch, not a data cycle: no neighbour reads a value Audio produces.

**Index corrections routed (this GDD never edits the index):** (1) the Progress Tracker row `IDX:96` and the Design Order row `IDX:240` move to "Designed" when this GDD is approved; (2) Sanity's "Soft downstream" is to be reconciled with the index's hard edge (RF-3, RF-4); (3) the dependency list on `IDX:96` should gain Perception Filter as a hard dependency for the lie family and Emitter State (for `emitters()`/`beacon_aim`) as soft; (4) Accessibility Settings' list should cite AA-41 to AA-43.

## Tuning Knobs

*Every value below is an Audio-invented `[PLACEHOLDER]` with a proposed registry name, tuned by listening captures and the data-load relations of the Formulas section, not derived from first principles. Levels are dBr (0 dBr = the Witness bus nominal peak). **★** marks a **cross-system fact** that is a registry candidate (another GDD cites it, or its value is bound to another GDD's constant); a knob without ★ is a local Audio tunable in `assets/data/audio/tell_audio.json` (AA-44). The "Interacts with" column names the source formula, so each value links to its rationale. Locked constants (`BEAT_*`, `TELL_*`, `ABORT_HOLD_TICKS`, `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS`, `UNFOLD_LO`, `LIES_MAX`, `OUTCOME_CUE_WINDOW_TICKS` and others) are listed once at the end and are **not** knobs of this system.*

### A. Tell, release, restore and bed

| Knob | Default | Unit | Safe range | Too high | Too low | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `RELEASE_TICKS` ★ | 90 | ticks | 30 to 119 (strictly < `ABORT_HOLD_TICKS`) | collides with the 120-tick hold; `release_cue_relation` false and the boot refuses | the abort is not heard as a decay | D.3; `STRAND_RELEASE_TICKS` must equal it; `TELL_REARM_TICKS` |
| `BED_RELEASE_DEPTH_DB` | 48 | dB | 36 to 60 | the tail lingers after the web "lost interest" | the bed cuts off audibly | D.3 |
| `RESTORE_FADE_TICKS` ★ | 60 | ticks | 0 to 180 (0 = no fade) | the bed is absent for seconds after a load into TELL | the bed pops in on load | D.4; beats unaffected |
| `BED_BASE_DB` | -18 | dBr | -24 to -12 | the bed dominates the mix and fatigues | the bed is masked by tool noise and the margin fails | D.6; `TELL_AUDIBILITY_MARGIN_DB`; every `*_CEIL_DB` |
| `TIER_STEP_DB` | 2 | dB | 0 to 4 | reads as a continuous rise (a meter by ear, AT-26) | tiers are not distinguishable | D.6; AA-18 (discrete steps only) |
| `TIER_XFADE_TICKS` | 30 | ticks | 12 to 60 | a tier change is inaudible | a layer pops in | AA-18; `TIER2_TICKS`, `TIER3_TICKS` (cited) |
| `TELL_AUDIBILITY_MARGIN_DB` ★ | 6 | dB | 3 to 9 | the bed dominates the world | the bed is masked; the floor window fails for a low-vision player | D.6; M2; `RUN_CEIL_DB`, `WIT_BED_BAND_REJECT_DB` |
| `BREACH_ENTRY_STEP_LU_MAX` ★ | 8 | LU | 4 to 12 | a jump scare; breaks "the tell precedes it" (M4) | the breach under-reads against the last beat | D.5 |
| `BED_END_TICKS` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | 12 | ticks | 6 to 24 | the bed lingers over the breach entry and masks pulse 0 | the bed stops with a click | D.5 (`P_bed_end`); AA-20 |
| `BEAT_REF_DB` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | -9 | dBr | -15 to -3 | the knock dominates the mix and the entry peak (this plus `BREACH_ENTRY_STEP_LU_MAX`) approaches full scale | a beat is masked by a run footfall | D.19 R-d, R-e |
| `BEAT_TAIL_MAX_TICKS` | 60 | ticks | 30 to 90 | more tail overlap, more reserved voices | a clipped, harsh knock | D.10 reserve; asset-length lint |
| `RESERVED_TELL_IMPULSE_VOICES` ★ | 2 | voices | 1 to 3 (floor 1 = M5's literal) | idle reserved voices | a beat tail is cut by the next beat | D.10; `BEAT_MIN_GAP_TICKS` (cited); RF-1 |
| `B_LOW`, `B_EDGE` (band edges `BED_BAND_LOW_HZ`, `BED_BAND_EDGE_HZ`) | 30 to 120; 200 to 400 | Hz | `B_LOW` 25 to 140 (its upper edge stays below the POWER hum fundamental, 160 Hz, AA-26); `B_EDGE` 180 to 500 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | the bed collides with the POWER hum | the bed is lost on small speakers | D.6; AA-45 |
| `HAPTIC_BEAT_MS` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | 80 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | ms | 40 to 150 | the pulse reads as a rumble, not a knock | the pulse is not felt | AA-42; only with `haptic_beat_pulse` on |
| `HAPTIC_BEAT_STRENGTH_PM` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | 600 `[PLACEHOLDER]` | permille of motor strength | 300 to 1000 | the pulse is fatiguing | the pulse is not felt | AA-42; fixed, never scaled by tier, beat index or progress |
| `haptic_beat_pulse` (player setting) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | off | bool | off by default; a user opt-in (UI at Alpha) | n/a | n/a | AA-42; Accessibility Settings |

### B. Alarm

| Knob | Default | Unit | Safe range | Too high | Too low | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ALARM_DUTY_MAX_PM` ★ | 250 | permille of a sweep | 100 to 400 | a wall of alarm that masks the witnesses | the pulse is too short to carry | D.2 (`W` = 32 ticks at 128); M3 |
| `ALARM_MIN_GAP_TICKS` (derived) | `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS div 2` | ticks | not tuned | n/a | n/a | D.2: bounds the post-switch gap to 64 to 191 |
| `BEACON_LIVE_HOLD_TICKS` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | 12 | ticks | 2 to 64 | the clock reacts late to a real beacon | a toggling beacon flaps the clock | D.2; AA-20 c; E-AA-70 |
| `BREACH_ENTRY_TAIL_MAX_TICKS` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | 48 | ticks | 12 to `ALARM_MIN_GAP_TICKS` - 1 | pulse 0 overlaps the next pulse | pulse 0 is clipped and its tail no longer carries the transition | D.2; lint L-14 |
| `ANN_STEPDOWN_SWEEPS` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | 8 | sweeps | 4 to 24 | the klaxon fatigues before it steps down | the alarm steps down before the player has reacted (1024 ticks, 17.07 s at the default) | D.20; AA-27 |
| `ANN_STEPDOWN_DB` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | 6 | dB | 3 to 12 (bounded by D.19 R-h) | the stepped klaxon is lost under the room tone | no relief from the fatigue | D.20; D.19 R-h |

### C. Pre-tell creaks

| Knob | Default | Unit | Safe range | Too high | Too low | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CREAK_MEAN_TICKS[1..4]` | 1800 / 1200 / 780 / 480 | ticks | each 360 to 2400, strictly decreasing, each step >= 1.3x denser | creaks are rare; steps indistinct | creak spam reads as a tell or a meter | D.13; `CUE_STEPS`, `AMBIENT_CUE_FLOOR_PM` (cited) |
| `CREAK_MIN_GAP_TICKS[1..4]` | 600 / 480 / 360 / 240 | ticks | each < its mean | too regular, mechanical | bursts of creaks | D.13 load check |

| `PRETELL_TAIL_MAX_TICKS` | 30 | ticks | 15 to 60 | a creak is confused with a beat | a clipped settle | E-AA-6; lint L-10 |

### D. Ducking

| Knob | Default | Unit | Safe range | Too high | Too low | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `DUCK_DB` | 6 | dB | 3 to 9 | the dip on each beat is heard as pumping *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | a beat is buried by a TOOL loop | D.18; the D.6 margin |

## Visual/Audio Requirements

*Rule ids in this section are `AAV-n` (sync rows) and `M-n` (motion rules). Citations: `ART x.y` = the art bible section; `AA-n` = this GDD; neighbour ids as in the Cross-References citation key. Constants are cited by name only. The art-director was consulted for this section (REQUIRED for an Audio system); the art-bible refreshes it implies are listed at the end and are **routed, not applied**.*

### Scope line and sync direction rule

**Audio draws nothing.** It is a PRESENTATION subscriber that writes audio nodes only (AA-3). So this section is not a visual design. It is (1) a **sync table** that pairs every audio cue with the visual twin its neighbour already owns and fixes the ordering and shape constraints between them; (2) the motion rules that bind the pairs; (3) the art bible principles, palette pairings and channel classification; (4) the few visual-side deliverables audio implies. The art-director authors **no new sprite, VFX, shader or light** for this system. Content, mix, bus and triggers remain Audio's (`ART:962`, `ART:210`).

**Sync direction rule (one sentence, binding for every row).** For every event where both senses exist, `audio_start_tick <= visual_start_tick`, because the audio request is created in the frame poll that precedes the presenter's draw of the frame in which the fact arrived (AA-4, AA-13) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* and the visual never draws first (HR-9, AUD-2, FT-35 "audio may lead"). The reverse (visual before audio) is a defect. A visual that is absent (dark room, nothing lit, no beacon) never weakens an audio cue and an audio cue is never withheld because its visual is absent.

### Events: the sync table (AAV-1 to AAV-39)

Column key. **Twin** = the visual the neighbour owns (rule id). **Channel**: T = truth (SIM-derived, no hallucination may add, remove or alter), C = corruptible (Filter, per viewer). **Slice**: MVP, VS, A (Alpha). A row with "none" in the twin column is deliberate and accessibility-load-bearing where marked **[LB]**.
#### A. Tell layer (`attn`) and the pre-tell cue

| ID | Audio cue (AA) | Visual twin and owner | Sync / constraint | Ch. | Slice |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| AAV-1 | Pre-tell creak, quantised step 1..`CUE_STEPS` (AA-22) | Attention V2 / AV-6 static stubs at web roots in the lit rim; 3-frame stepped reveal (Attention) | Both derive from the same `ambient_cue_step` (same `CUE_HYST_PM`), so ear and eye never disagree on the step. No lead requirement (neither is a tell). Creak is positional in the **listener's own section**, never at the locus; stubs are never drawn toward a locus. With nothing lit the creak is the only pre-tell channel **[LB]**. Neither may vary continuously with `level_pm`. | T | MVP |
| AAV-2 | Onset carrying beat 0 (AA-14) | V3: first resolve with band-slide (AV-5); presenter draws nothing for an epoch before beat 0 (Attention) | The audio request is created in the frame poll and the visual is drawn after it, in the same frame *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; the onset attack transient must land inside the **first stepped frame** of the resolve (<= 5 ticks at 12 fps), never on the last. Audio is never delayed to "match" the 4-frame resolve. | T | MVP |
| AAV-3 | Beat: one unpitched knock per `(tell_epoch, tell_beat)` (AA-15) | V4: guaranteed resolve + one dust impulse, band-slide (AV-5, AV-7) | Beat transient on resolve frame 1. At most 2 per second on both senses (`BEAT_MIN_GAP_TICKS`), so both sit under the 3 Hz ceiling (HR-13 world-side). One beat per poll on both sides; no catch-up on either. A hitch that coalesces beats shows one resolve and plays one knock. | T | MVP |
| AAV-4 | Bed tier layers, crossfade over `TIER_XFADE_TICKS` (AA-18) | V5: drift / twitch / sway motion classes (AV-8) | Both step only at the published `tier` boundary. Audio is **never** the source of any strand motion and no strand motion is driven by bed level. | T | MVP |
| AAV-5 | Positional hull-stress layer at the locus hull anchor, tier >= 2 (AA-17) | **None.** Hull-stress hairlines were cut from the Tell permanently (Attention ruling 3); tell dust direction is never toward the locus (Attention shared-vocabulary note) | Deliberate asymmetry: the visual tell is global and beat-locked, so audio is the **only** localiser of the tell, and only to a section. **[LB]** for a listener who cannot see the rim. ART 2.7 still lists "hull-stress hairlines" under *Creaking*: refresh owed (see Art-bible refreshes and flags, R-1). | T | MVP |
| AAV-6 | Bed (non-positional, every living player) (AA-16) | Strands exist only inside the lit region of a player-controllable emitter (AV-14) | The bed plays when the visual cannot (nothing lit, listener at an unoccupied derelict, listener in a pod). `ART:954` item 15 accepts this: audio is the only non-visual Attention channel. **[LB]** | T | MVP |
| AAV-7 | Abort release: bed decays over `RELEASE_TICKS`, release cue (AA-19) | V7: reverse stepped retraction over `STRAND_RELEASE_TICKS`; no band-slide, no success glyph (AV-9, AV-11) | **Start ticks equal, lengths equal** (`release_cue_relation`: `RELEASE_TICKS < ABORT_HOLD_TICKS` and `STRAND_RELEASE_TICKS == RELEASE_TICKS`). At the placeholder `RELEASE_TICKS` 90 ticks and 12 fps the retraction is 18 stepped frames. Neither sense carries a reward cue; the release must not read as a beat on either. | T | MVP |
| AAV-8 | Breach entry = alarm pulse 0, loudness step bounded by `BREACH_ENTRY_STEP_LU_MAX` (AA-20) | V8: snap-taut in `TAUT_SNAP_FRAMES` stepped frames then absolute stillness (AV-10); beacon lens lit at T+1 (Emitter) | Audio at T, taut in the same frame *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*, beacon at T+1: audio leads the beacon by exactly one tick and never trails it. The held rim (AV-10) is a non-red visual phase cue that matches the audio's role on a derelict. | T | MVP |
| AAV-9 | Alarm cadence: one pulse per `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS`, duty <= `ALARM_DUTY_MAX_PM`, aligned to `T mod BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS == 0` or fallback `(T - breach_enter_T) mod BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS` (AA-20, S3) | Beacon sweep (Alarm Red, Emitter); HUD warning mark pulsing in phase with the beacon at ~50 % duty (ART 7.3, HR-13) | **Period-lock and leading-edge-lock, not coincidence.** The pulse starts on the sweep boundary (the beam's authored phase), not when the red passes the listener's screen. Audio duty (<= 25 % at the placeholder) is shorter than the mark's ~50 %; that is intended, the **leading edge** is the contract *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (the entry sweep and a mode-switch sweep can reach 62.5 %, D.2, OQ-AA-41). Same rhythm everywhere (`ART:845`): one 2.13 s clock for beacon, mark, `attn` pulse and `ann`. On a derelict (no beacon) the fallback clock carries the breach alone; the held rim and trails are the visual. | T | MVP |
| AAV-10 | Subside: one short collapse, then silence, no "danger over" (AA-21) | V10: beacon off, strands slack then stepped retraction; nothing drawn that says "all clear" (AV-11) | Neither sense signals safety. `phase == CALM` after SUBSIDING plays and draws nothing; trails and failing-loop sounds (`wit`) carry the ongoing state (`AT:1210`). | T | MVP |
| AAV-11 | Restore into any phase: bed fades in over `RESTORE_FADE_TICKS`; no one-shot replays (AA-5) | V12: picture appears already in its phase-correct state, one pop-in allowed (Attention) | Accepted asymmetry: the picture pops, the bed fades. Neither plays onset, snap, release or breach-enter. A paused frame is silent for sim-derived sound and still. | T | MVP |
| AAV-12 | Bed during pod transit unchanged (AA-33, AA-46) | V13: strands continue only in lit regions of eligible kinds (Attention) | Visual may be absent (pod lights are FIXTURE only if authored eligible); the bed is not. **[LB]** | T | MVP |

#### B. Witness (`wit`), running (`run`) and annunciator (`ann`)

| ID | Audio cue | Visual twin and owner | Sync / constraint | Ch. | Slice |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| AAV-13 | Witness classes HISS, DRIP, HUM_DROP, CLANK, WIND, TICK, SIZZLE, CREAK at the descriptor anchor, from true `intensity_pm` (AA-24) | Failure & Trail E1-E15: mist, frost, scorch, drip, hairline trails (FT) | Same anchor, same Layout source (FT AC-137). Audio may lead; FT never waits (FT-35). The sound class changes at the same descriptor step as the trail medium, never earlier than the descriptor exists. | T | MVP |
| AAV-14 | HISS in an unlit compartment, zero lit emitters (AA-24) | **None, by Principle 1.** Rooms are unlit, trails exist only under light (FT E23) | A hiss with no visible mist is expected, not a bug. **No visual substitute is permitted**: no ripple, ping, radar, glow, outline or "sound marker" in the dark. The audio is the evidence; the player must spend light to see it. **[LB]** | T | MVP |
| AAV-15 | Flow-zero hull pre-stage TICK (AA-24) | 2 px hairline (FT AC-144) that does not resolve at zoom k = 1 | At k = 1 the cue is the only channel; at higher k audio and hairline coexist. Identical at every Filter stage (consequence channel). **[LB]** | T | MVP |
| AAV-16 | DRIP stop / resume; class ends with the descriptor, no completion cue (AA-24; FT E5-E7) | Drip stops; trail relaxes | "The reward is the absence." No relief chime, no "fixed" sound. | T | MVP |
| AAV-17 | Running timbres O2, POWER, COOLANT, FUEL from `served_pm`; INTEGRITY room tone (AA-26) | Rotor, sight-glass, flow chevrons, lamp brightness from `served_pm` (Graph V/A, ART 2.3 "humming") | Both read the same true value, so the **direction of change must agree** (a sagging hum next to a full-speed rotor is a contradiction). No frame-lock between audio rate and chevron speed is required or wanted. The lamp dims honestly and the hum sags; the lamp itself is silent. | T | MVP |

Under reduce-motion (HR-8) the mark holds solid and **the sound carries the alarm**, so `ann` is load-bearing for that player. Under reduce-flashing the audio is unchanged (M1, FT E20/E21). | T | MVP |

The guttering lamp, the dying pod light and every light event in 2.2 are silent. | T | MVP |

Reserved leak signature (detuned fringe partial, 4-6 s breathing, over-reaching tail) belongs to `hal` only. #### F. Captions, settings and diagnostics (policy MVP, implementation Alpha)

| ID | Feature | Visual spec | Constraint |
|---|---|---|---|
| AAV-38 | Sound captions (AA-41) | HUD `CanvasLayer` plate, **Ground `#1C2128` opaque**, text Paper White `#DDE4EA` (about 12.6:1), instrument or reading face per 7.1; no family colour, no icon colour; never Alarm Red (HR-4), never violet or flesh (4.6); captions are an anchor (HR-6): not shaken, tinted, scaled or filtered by any Filter state | Captionable families only (`wit`, `ann`, `run`, `suit`, `foot`, `ia`, `asm`, `body`, `pod`, `ui`). **Never** the tell, breach, pre-tell, Horror, `hal`, `p1c`. Caption appears no earlier than its sound, only for sounds above the listener's audibility floor (never a radar), **no direction arrow or locator** (ART 7.6 item 12 spirit). Names the source precisely ("Hiss", "Coolant pump", "Seam cut") because `hal` ids are disjoint from every captionable family, an uncaptioned `hal` sound never sits beside a captioned twin: the caption set is not a lie oracle. |

Settings UI (Alpha) visual rules: sliders are **linear bars with a needle-mark** (7.3 hold-to-confirm grammar, never radial), chamfer-only corners (3.4), Ground plates (HR-2), no violet, no flesh, no Alarm Red; one World slider; no control displays, sonifies or meters Attention; no waveform, spectrum or "sound radar" in release builds. Debug Overlay voice counters (DIAGNOSTIC, `voice_tier1_steal`) are absent from release and use UI palette only.

### Motion constraints (M-1 to M-8)

- **M-1 Nothing is driven by audio.** No visual parameter (position, scale, brightness, hue, alpha, shake) is a function of any audio level, bus meter, cue start or voice state. Lint: no presenter reads Audio (Attention V14, AV-4; AA-3 inverse). No screen flash, vignette, camera nudge or HUD pulse on a tell beat, strike or breach pulse.
- **M-2 Audio-first is ordering, not delay.** Audio is never postponed to match a stepped visual and no visual is postponed to match an audio tail. Latency figures (device buffer, Windows stack) are a spike (OQ-AA-4); the rule is the shared notification. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The one sanctioned offset is the uniform, measured `AUDIO_LEAD_MS` hold a presenter may apply to all its visual twins (default 0, AA-13 b); "the shared notification" is read as the call order of the frame poll (AA-4).
- **M-3 One alarm rhythm.** `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS` 128 (2.13 s, inside the 2-3 s window of ART 2.8) is the single period for beacon, warning mark, `attn` pulse and `ann`. Nothing luminous or audible alarm-like runs faster; the mark never exceeds 3 Hz (HR-13); no full-frame flash, ever.
- **M-4 Reduce-motion (HR-8) and reduce-flashing.** The sweep becomes a static mark plus HUD glyph and **sound**; trail propagation is never suppressed; neither setting lowers any audio level or removes any cue (M1, FT E20/E21). The held rim (AV-10) is already still.
- **M-5 Stepped visuals, unstepped audio.** Visual twins are stepped at 12 fps (every 5th tick); audio is continuous. Audio events align to the **first stepped frame** of their twin, and audio never imitates the stepping (no 12 Hz tremolo on the bed).
- **M-6 Footfall contact frames.** The crewmate cycles are 8 frames for walk, run and carry-walk (ART 5.2; sheet budget in 5.7), at 12 fps, 5 ticks per frame, consistent with AA-31 (a contact every 4 frames = 20 ticks at every gait; gait is told by level and timbre, never rate). The animator must draw the **contact pose** (lead foot planted) on frames 0 and 4 of every cycle. **Default `FOOT_CONTACT_FRAMES` = {0, 4} for all three gaits**, exported from the Aseprite sidecar as a per-frame flag (Asset implications), not typed in code. Result of the check against ART 5.2 / 5.7: **clean, no conflict** (8-frame cycles confirmed at all three gaits). Mirrored facings reuse frames, so the flag holds for mirrored files; no left/right sound distinction exists.
- **M-7 Honest motion stays silent.** Settle, tremor and the honest digit tick (ART 7.3) have no sound. Glide has no sound of its own: the lie's sound is the `hal` signature (AAV-33), not a "glide whoosh".
- **M-8 Pixel-space invariants.** Nothing audible depends on zoom k in [1, k_auto + 2] or on camera lead; no visual twin changes its audio because a rung changed (restate at every ladder step; pattern 3).

### Palette rules (pairing rules, not hexes)

- **Loop pairing.** `run` and `wit` sounds pair only with their loop's hue and shape in the world: O2 Cyan, Power Amber, Coolant Ice, Fuel Ember, Hull Grey (integrity). A witness sound never pairs with a hue that is not its loop's. Confusable pairs are separated in sound by AA-26's property for each pair (ART 4.7) in addition to the shape backups.
- **Alarm Red pairing.** Only `ann` and the `attn` breach pulses pair with Alarm Red (the sweep and the failing-instrument warning mark). No other sound is associated with red. Alarm Red is never caption text (HR-4).
- **Sane-palette channel (violet and flesh).** `hal` is the **only** audio family that pairs with violet or flesh visuals. No TRUE family's visual twin may carry either hue, and no caption, settings control or audio UI element may carry either (4.6 divergence; the only violet that reaches screen space is a lie's gleam on a reading's glass).
- **Chemlight.** `#86B896` glow is silent; sounds belong to throw / land / wane and never to the light.
- **Tungsten / human light.** Light is silent. Lamp hum is the machine's (EM-14, `LO:429`), never a lamp sound; a crewmate's beam has no sound, so the human channel carries no audio of its own beyond foley and voice.
- **Luminance ladder analogue.** The ear's analogue of the luminance ladder (ART 4.5) is the band plan (AA-45): the tell bed low, the POWER hum above it, `hal` in its reserved narrow band that no one else may occupy. Treat it as the audio backstop in the same sense: not the primary channel, the thing that survives when the primary one collapses.
- **Depth never changes hue; depth never changes the tell contract.** Ring depth may shift only pre-tell creak density (VS), never a sound's hue pairing.
- **Verification.** The existing visual sampling tests (for example lit steel hue >= 36 degrees, ART 4.4) are unchanged by audio; audio adds only the two blind tests: hue-removed and audio-removed identification of the lying instrument (HR-5), and the achromatopsia test for the below-threshold crewmate (HR-7).

### Truth channel and corruptible channels

| Visual or audio | Class | Note |
|---|---|---|
| All `attn`, `wit`, `run`, `ann`, `hor`, `suit`, `foot`, `ia`, `asm`, `body`, `pod`, `p1c`, voice, `amb`, `ui` audio | **T** | Identical at every sanity stage and Filter state (AA-38) |
| Attention strands, stubs, band-slide, taut hold, release, beacon | **T** | Matches Attention's own classification table ("the bed, beats, release, alarm: T") |
| F&T trails, Graph-driven lamp brightness, rotor / chevrons | **T** | Consequence channel |
| Horror rig, marks, rime, hairlines, L3 dips | **T** | Only per-viewer omission of the body (VS) and phantom / class-(c) rigs are C |
| Violet gleam, glide, continuous tick on a lying reading | **C** | Filter, per viewer |
| `hal` reading-lie sound (`FA_LIE_SIG_GAUGE`, `FA_LIE_SIG_SPRITE`) | **C (PERCEIVED)** | Exactly one family; always with an audible leak |
| Class-(c) violet fringe | Filter-drawn, marks a true state | Honest marker; paired with the **T** P1 cue |
| Captions | **T** (name only true sounds) | Never name a `hal` sound |

**Designed mismatch rows (not defects).** (1) Hidden real horror: body C (omitted), sound T. (2) Lit locus: the visual tell is global, the positional hull-stress layer localises to a section. (3) Dark room: sound T, no visual by Principle 1. (4) Fringe vs P1 cue: one passes through the Filter, one does not. (5) Picture pops on restore, bed fades.

### Asset implications (specifications, no pixels, no new sprites)

1. **Foot-contact flag in the character sidecar (the only visual-side deliverable).** In `char_crew_body_alb.png`'s Aseprite export (8.5 sidecars), tag frames 0 and 4 of `walk_<facing>`, `run_<facing>` and `carry_<facing>` with a per-frame user-data flag `foot`; `FOOT_CONTACT_FRAMES` is **exported, not typed** (same rule as 8.5's per-frame offset table). Owner: art-director (animation) with the Controller.
2. **No new sprites, sheets, shaders, lights or VFX** are created by this system. Explicitly absent: no sound-visualiser, ripple, ping, subtitle-for-tell, rumble icon, waveform or meter.
3. **Alpha UI parts (implied, not scheduled):** caption plate (reuse the `ui_frame_*` Ground plate family; e.g. `ui_frame_caption.png`), settings slider (linear bar plus needle-mark; e.g. `ui_frame_slider.png`), all in the 4.6 palette and the one UI `Theme`.
4. **Audio file naming (conformance note).** AA-6's `sfx_<token>_<context>_<name>_<variant>` is a second deliberate deviation from the project's generic `[category]_[name]_[variant]_[size].[ext]` (8.5 documents the first, for visual sheets, where the trailing slot is the map suffix). For audio the trailing slot is a two-digit variant index. It is snake_case and lowercase, so it fits `naming.files`. `sfx` / `mus` / `amb` / `ui` / `vox` are not in 8.5's `cat` table (visual categories only). Recommend recording an audio addendum beside 8.5; do not block.
5. **Evidence rig (QA, not game asset).** Tick-stamped capture of audio-request tick versus first visual draw tick for the sync table, stored in `production/qa/evidence/`.

### Accessibility and reduced-effect behaviour

- **Reduce-motion (HR-8) and reduce-flashing** change nothing audible: no level, cue or rhythm is reduced (M1, FT E20 and E21). Under reduce-motion the warning mark holds solid and the sound carries the alarm, so `ann` and the `attn` pulse are load-bearing for that player (AAV-19).
- **Low vision.** The tell is audible through the bed and the beats alone (AA-15, AA-16); the dark-room hiss, the flow-zero TICK and the seam-cut loop are audio-only channels by design (AAV-5, AAV-14, AAV-15, AAV-31).
- **Deaf and hard of hearing.** The tell channel is the visual rim plus the opt-in, off-by-default haptic beat pulse (AA-42; user ruling D51), which carries the audio beat's information and nothing more; no caption of the tell is added. Captions name only true, captionable sounds and never the tell, breach, pre-tell creaks, Horror or `hal` (AAV-38). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*
- **Mono and small speakers.** The bed keeps an upper-partial edge layer so it survives a laptop speaker and a mono downmix (AA-16, AA-45); mono never removes the bed (AC-AA-92).
- **Tactile channel:** none by default; the opt-in beat pulse and the player's own body-cue haptics are the two ruled exceptions (AA-42; OQ-AA-2 and OQ-AA-15 resolved). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*
- **Colour.** No audio-bearing visual relies on hue alone (ART 4.0 principle 2); the four confusable loop pairs of ART 4.7 are separated by the AA-26 sound properties in addition to shape.

### Engine feasibility notes

- Audio is engine nodes only; nothing here is drawn, so no shader, light or particle cost is added. The Godot audio reference has no scripting or core file; the relevant file is `docs/engine-reference/godot/modules/audio.md`, and the listener policy under the 640x360 world SubViewport at integer scale is a spike (AC-AA-81, OQ-AA-1) with the manual pan-and-gain mix as the named fallback (-> ADR candidate).
- `AudioEffectSpectrumAnalyzer.tap_back_pos` is removed in the pinned version: the tell bus is never probed by spectrum for gameplay, and the band lint reads import-time tags (AA-45, OQ-AA-5).
- `Area2D` `audio_bus_override` zones are not used (the 4.7 `area_mask` default 0, D.9).

- F1. **ART:962 hand-off** is discharged in the hand-off table above; the only item still open is "heard by its own source" (game-designer).
- F2. **8.5 naming deviation** (R-2).
- F3. **ART 2.10 death dim-out vs AA-5 / AA-34.** Question for Lighting and Save/Load: is the dim-out sim-driven (then `run` sags and ends as a true consequence, nothing added) or a presentation overlay (then sound hard-stops at the cut)? Either is compatible with "death plays no sound"; the GDD should state which. Not a conflict.
- F4. **Idle pack breathing (ART 5.2) vs audible breath cadence.** The animation is fixed-rate (cannot carry state, ART 2.9). Suit may want a laboured-breath pose variant (ART 5.3 honest body states); not required by audio. Open question to Suit and art.
- F5. **`hal` breathing phase.** Recommend deriving from the Filter's lie-time; confirm with the Filter GDD (PF-20 / PFV-15). Optional phase-lock.
- F6. **Alarm duty versus mark duty.** `ALARM_DUTY_MAX_PM` placeholder (250) vs the mark's ~50 %: the contract is the leading edge (AAV-9); confirm the accessibility-specialist agrees (HR-13).
- F7. **Caption locator.** No direction arrow, no off-screen indicator for captions (ART 7.6 item 12 spirit; `HE:1342-1360`); accessibility-specialist may ask for one: it would conflict with the standing decisions for horror and tell sounds, so raise as a trade-off, not an addition.
- F8. **5.7 cycle-length check: CLEAN.** Walk, run and carry-walk are 8-frame cycles; the AA-31 default {0, 4} is consistent. No conflict to surface.
- F9. **Phase-locked sound for the fringe (class c, VS-B)** is not needed; the three modes are independent legs.

### Acceptance mapping

The art-director's ten acceptance proposals are folded into the Acceptance Criteria, not duplicated: AC-AAV-1 is AC-AA-110; AC-AAV-2 is AC-AA-118; AC-AAV-3 is AC-AA-119 (and AC-AA-35); AC-AAV-4 is AC-AA-120 (and AC-AA-27); AC-AAV-5 is AC-AA-121; AC-AAV-6 is AC-AA-28 and AC-AA-58; AC-AAV-7 is AC-AA-122; AC-AAV-8 is AC-AA-123; AC-AAV-9 is AC-AA-34; AC-AAV-10 is AC-AA-124 (and AC-AA-107).

📌 **Asset Spec** — Visual/Audio requirements are defined. This system draws nothing and authors no sprite, sheet, shader, light or VFX; its asset surface is audio only (OGG Vorbis, identity-table names `sfx_<token>_<context>_<name>_<variant>`, AA-6 and AA-44) plus one animation-sidecar flag (`foot` on frames 0 and 4 of the walk, run and carry cycles). **Preconditions:** the art bible must be approved and its audio naming addendum (RF-13) and the 2.7 *Creaking* refresh (RF-12) must have landed. Then run `/asset-spec system:adaptive-audio-and-tells` to produce the per-asset descriptions and generation prompts from this section and the identity table.

## Game Feel

*Scope note.* Audio has no verb of its own: the player never "operates" it, so the feel being specified is **the weight of the tell, the honesty of the ship's voice, and the quiet that follows restraint**. The player's body, view and controls never react to a sound: nothing about the player's character panics, the camera does not shake, the controller does not rumble (AA-42), the HUD does not pulse. If a tester says "my character got scared", the feel is wrong; the *ship* changes, the player does not (`PCI:135`, `PIPE:151`). Latencies are given at `TICK_RATE` 60 (one tick is 16.7 ms); the sim-side numbers are cited from the owners, the audio-side numbers are `[PLACEHOLDER]` values from the Tuning Knobs. **Who owns which feel:** the *tell's* dread curve and the *breach's* silence are Attention's design (Attention Game Feel owns the visual rim and the 10 to 30 s arc) and Audio owns how it sounds; the *loop and failure* feel is the Graph's and Failure & Trail's, and Audio owns the ear on it; the *own-footstep* feel is the Controller's (PC-11) and Audio owns the sound; the *channel bar and result* feel is Interaction's; the *lie's leak* feel is Sanity's and the Filter's, and Audio owns the signature. This GDD owns the sound of each, the cue's timing against its visual twin, and the rule that nothing is announced that did not happen.

### Feel Reference

*Borrowed.* Subnautica's distant leviathan call: heard everywhere, located nowhere, which is the structure-borne bed (AA-16: non-positional, every living player). Alien: Isolation's vent knock before the drop: the building announces the threat before the screen does, which is the beat that arrives ahead of the first filament (AA-13). Pandorum's groaning hull and Aliens' dripping-pipe silence: the ship has a voice that is a physical fact, not a score. Project Zomboid's own-noise radius: the player is the cause of the danger and the game lets them hear it, which is the footstep as the only noise readout (AA-31). *Anti-reference.* Dead Space's and Resident Evil 7's stingers, heartbeat and breath-rate sanity cues, and Darkest Dungeon's narrator: no sound tells the player a number about themselves, no sound scares for its own sake, and no sound congratulates. A music cue that rises before the first beat, a "danger over" chime and a proximity stinger are each a defect (AA-23, AA-21, AA-29).

### Input Responsiveness

Audio has no input of its own; the rows are the **latency from a published fact (or a player action) to its sound**. "Request" means the cue request created by the frame poll of the frame in which the P5 notification that publishes the fact arrived (AA-4); "play" means the engine call in that same poll *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. The device-buffer and Windows-stack delay on top is a measured spike (AC-AA-6), not a rule.

| Event | Max latency, fact to request | Budget | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Tell onset carrying beat 0 | the frame poll of the frame that holds beat 0 (same frame, before the draw) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | before the first filament, never after | AA-13, AA-14, AUD-2 |
| Tell beat | 0 ticks (same poll as the published pair) | at most one per poll, no catch-up | AA-4; the poll interval above 500 ms can coalesce beats, accepted |
| Breach entry (pulse 0) | the frame poll of the entry frame *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | at `breach_enter_T`, one tick ahead of the beacon lens | AA-20, AAV-8 |
| Strike cue | the same frame as the notification that publishes `strike_T`, positioned to start at `strike_T` | the request is created by the frame poll, before the presenter draws; a catch-up frame can exceed one tick (17 ms) of wall time, so the guarantee is the order | `HE:1285`; AA-13; OQ-AA-37 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| Witness class at a new descriptor | the poll that sees it (the Graph publishes every 6 ticks) | never delayed to the alarm grid | AA-25, AA-27 |
| Own footfall | the tick the animation presents a contact frame (every 20 ticks at every gait) | no step on idle, work, stagger, down or a blocked move | AA-31, M-6 |
| Interaction terminal cue (done, cancel, abort, nothing) | the same frame as the result (frame poll) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | only the terminal transition plays | AA-32; the press pre-play is the one cue allowed before a result |
| Hallucination signature ON | the first ACTIVE frame of the lie | audible within `SOUND_LEAD_MAX_MS` (100 ms) of the first ACTIVE frame; before the glide is visible (`t_glide_visible` 0.125 to 0.83 s) | PF-12, AA-36 |
| Audio-versus-screen delta of a cue and its visual twin (loopback capture beside a screen capture) | measured, p95 recorded | non-negative at p95 on the reference machine, otherwise `AUDIO_LEAD_MS` (default 0) is set from the measurement; the absolute `play()`-to-DAC delay is not claimed | AC-AA-6; OQ-AA-4 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

### Animation Feel Targets

Audio has no animations; the equivalent is the **envelope** of each cue class, in ticks at `TICK_RATE` 60, set against the stepped 12 fps visual twin (one frame is 5 ticks). Audio is unstepped (M-5) and never imitates the stepping.

| Cue | Attack / startup | Sustain / active | Release / tail | Feel goal | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Tell beat | transient inside the first 5 ticks | one unpitched knock | tail at most `BEAT_TAIL_MAX_TICKS` (60) | a structural knock, countable, never a drum | spaced `BEAT_MIN_GAP_TICKS` (30) or more apart |
| Tell onset (beat 0) | as a beat | as a beat | as a beat | the first knock is the news | lands in the first stepped frame of the resolve (AAV-2) |
| Bed tier step | `TIER_XFADE_TICKS` (30) crossfade | `TIER_STEP_DB` (2) louder per step | n/a | heavier, not faster; steps, never a ramp | tier edges `TIER2_TICKS`, `TIER3_TICKS` |
| Abort release | none | none | `RELEASE_TICKS` (90), bed down `BED_RELEASE_DEPTH_DB` (48) | the web losing interest; relief without reward | equals `STRAND_RELEASE_TICKS` |
| Breach entry and alarm pulse | step at most `BREACH_ENTRY_STEP_LU_MAX` (8 LU) | pulse at most 32 ticks (`ALARM_DUTY_MAX_PM` 250 of a 128-tick sweep); pulse 0 at most 48 ticks including its tail (`BREACH_ENTRY_TAIL_MAX_TICKS`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | short | firm and rhythmic, not a jump scare | one pulse per `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS` (128) |
| Subside | one short collapse | none | then silence | the pressure lets go, nothing is declared safe | AA-21 |
| REACH lead-in | builds over `REACH_RESOLVE_PRE_TICKS` (90) | no impact | ends at `reach_end_T` | a telegraph that asserts nothing the picture does not | fizzle decays quietly |
| Strike | at `strike_T` | single hit | short | the witness of strike one; no kill stinger | AA-28 c |
| Footfall | at the contact frame | one step | short | weight by level and timbre, not by rate | -9, -3, -9 dBr walk, run, carry |
| Ducking | `DUCK_ATTACK_TICKS` (6) | `DUCK_HOLD_TICKS` (24) | `DUCK_RELEASE_TICKS` (30) | the beat cuts through with a shallow, fixed-depth dip (a duck is heard as a duck, D.18) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | tier 4 and below only |
| Restore fade-in | `RESTORE_FADE_TICKS` (60) | steady level | n/a | the bed returns, nothing replays | sustained cues only |
| Hallucination signature | starts on the first ACTIVE frame at its D.15 level, de-click `DECLICK_TICKS` only; audible within `SOUND_LEAD_MAX_MS` | breathing at `HAL_BREATH_PERIOD_TICKS` (300) | `HAL_FADE_TICKS` (24) fade out at CLEAR, no sting | a sound no machine makes, never a sting | AA-35 |
| Berth bed | `LEG_BED_FADE_TICKS` (60) fade in at the edge into BERTHING | steady drive hum and hull strain, no rhythm | `LEG_BED_FADE_TICKS` (60) fade out at BERTHED, then the contact cue | the crossing is witnessed, not shown; never an ETA | AA-33 (g), D.17 |

### Impact Moments

The punctuation of this system is **silence and restraint as much as sound**.

| Impact type | Duration | Effect description | Configurable? |
|---|---|---|---|
| Tell onset | one beat, then the bed from tier 1 | the first knock from nowhere, before anything on screen changes | knobs of Attention (beat schedule) and Audio (`BED_BASE_DB`) |
| Abort release | `RELEASE_TICKS` (90) | the bed sags and lets go; the quiet after it is the reward | yes, with the `release_cue_relation` bound |
| Breach entry | one event, then the cadence | a firm, bounded step into rhythm; the entry is itself pulse 0 | `BREACH_ENTRY_STEP_LU_MAX` |
| The silence after a breach | the SUBSIDING-to-CALM interval | one short collapse, then nothing declares safety | not configurable by design (AA-21) |
| Death and load | none | **no sound**: no sting, no loss tone, no re-wake chime | not configurable by design (AA-34) |
| Hit-stop, screen shake, camera impact, time-scale | none | none; no audio-driven camera or time effect exists (M-1) | no |
| Controller rumble | none by default; one fixed `HAPTIC_BEAT_MS` pulse per tell beat when the opt-in is on | off by default; the opt-in pulse carries the audio beat's information and nothing more, never tier- or level-keyed (AA-42) | yes: the opt-in `haptic_beat_pulse` setting (user ruling D51; OQ-AA-2 resolved) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| Strike | one hit | the witness of strike one; consequence is shown by the Suit and the hull, not by the sound | no |

### Weight and Responsiveness Profile

- **Weight.** Heavy and slow at the tell, flat and dry at the witness. The bed and the beats are low and structural; the Graph's sounds are small, specific and physical (a hiss, a drip, a clank, a hum that sags). Nothing is bright, nothing is musical, and nothing is louder because it matters more than the thing it points at.
- **Player control.** Total over the cause, none over the sound. The player controls the world's sound by what they do (light, power, noise, restraint) and never by a setting that lowers the tell (no setting lowers the bed below its margin, AA-43). Going still is the only control, and the release is its answer.
- **Snap quality.** Crisp and binary at the events (a beat is a knock, a pulse is a pulse), smooth and analog at the true values (a loop's pitch and level follow `served_pm`, a leak's level follows `iota`), and stepped, never ramped, at the tier and creak-density steps so that no sound is a continuous meter.
- **Acceleration model.** Simulation, not arcade. Cues do not swell for effect; beats tighten because the schedule tightens, a loop sags because the node sags, and the slews (`RUN_SLEW_TICKS`, `SLEW_TICKS`) only de-zipper the Graph's 10 Hz publish.
- **Failure texture.** Fair and legible. A player who is caught was told: the knocks were audible with the screen dark, the pre-tell creaks were stepping up, the failing loop was sounding at its place, and their own run was louder than a walk. The read on why they failed is always a sound they could have acted on; the game never hides a consequence by sound and never invents one (P1).

### Feel Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] A tester playing audio-only (video hidden) says the hull is knocking and counts the beats before anything on screen changes (AC-AA-4).
- [ ] At most 1 of 5 testers describes the abort as a reward or a victory and at least 3 of 5 describe relief or the thing losing interest, including testers who went still at the first knock (AC-AA-12) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- [ ] The breach entry is not reported as a jump scare and its loudness step is within `BREACH_ENTRY_STEP_LU_MAX` (AC-AA-14).
- [ ] A tester with the lamp off points to a seam weep's side before lighting the lamp, and the mist is where the ear said (AC-AA-36).
- [ ] Testers name their own footsteps, never a meter, as what told them they were louder (AC-AA-52).
- [ ] A panel told nothing about the signature identifies the lying instrument from sound alone at 90 per cent or better (AC-AA-58).
- [ ] The negative gate holds *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: no tester can state the Attention level more precisely than one step of the creak ladder (AA-22, the intended coarse Pillar 3 readout), no tester can judge a monster's distance from any sound except its own positional creep (AA-28 a), and no tester can state their own sanity stage by sound; a continuous, numeric or non-diegetic readout is what fails (Player Fantasy, "We will know this was right if"; AC-AA-145).
- [ ] No tester reports the death or the load as accompanied by a sound, and no tester reports a "danger over" cue (AC-AA-124).

## UI Requirements

**No UI of its own at MVP, by decision.** Adaptive Audio & Tells ships no HUD element, no screen, no menu, no world-space icon, no meter and no instrument: it draws nothing (AA-3, the Visual/Audio Requirements scope line). The art bible forbids an Attention indicator in any form (`ART:741`, 7.6 item 15 at `ART:954`) and Pillar 1 makes the pipe the player reads, so no surface may display or sonify the Attention level, the sanity stage or a Horror's proximity. There is accordingly **no UX Flag** at MVP and no per-screen UI evidence is owed; AC-AA-118 (no audio-driven visual) and AC-AA-75 (settings boundaries, review) stand in for the UI gate. Two surfaces are nonetheless *required by this GDD's policy* and belong to neighbours who do not yet exist (Alpha): the sound captions (AA-41) and the audio settings (AA-43). The table lists each surface, its owner and the constraint Audio places on it; those owners must repeat the constraints or surface a CONFLICT.

| Information | Display location | Update frequency | Condition | Owner and constraint |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| The attention level, the tell's progress, beat count, tier, time left | **Nowhere.** The world carries it (the bed, the beats, the rim) | n/a | n/a | No caption, no meter, no waveform, no "sound radar" in any release build; `tell_remaining` is never read by a PRESENTATION subscriber (AC-AA-19a) |
| Sound captions *(Alpha)* | HUD `CanvasLayer` plate, Ground `#1C2128` opaque, Paper White `#DDE4EA` text, no family colour, no icon colour, no direction arrow | on the caption's sound start, no earlier | a captionable family only, above the listener's audibility floor | **HUD & Suit Layer** designed (hud-and-suit-layer.md) **/ Accessibility Settings** `(provisional — undesigned)`. Captionable: `wit`, `ann`, `run`, `suit`, `foot`, `ia` terminals, `asm`, `body`, `pod`, `ui`. Never the tell, breach, alarm cadence, pre-tell creaks, Horror, `hal` or `p1c`. Names the source precisely ("Hiss", "Coolant pump", "Seam cut") so the caption set is not a lie oracle; never Alarm Red, violet or flesh (HR-4, ART 4.6) |
| Audio settings *(Alpha)*: master, World, Ambience, UI, Voice, mono, caption toggle | the settings screen: linear bars with a needle-mark (ART 7.3), chamfer-only corners (ART 3.4), Ground plates, one UI `Theme` | on change | always | **Settings & Input Rebinding / Accessibility Settings** `(provisional — undesigned)`. One World slider covers the 13 tokens of AA-43; the Ambience slider covers `amb` only; no setting lowers the bed below its margin; mono keeps the bed; reduce-flashing and reduce-motion never reduce audio |
| Own-run caption | as the caption plate | on the run sound | provisional | Controller `PCI:821` asks for it; AA-41 treats own-action foley as provisionally captionable (CX-A7, RF-7) |
| Diagnostics: voice counters, `voice_tier1_steal`, the request log, bus meters | the **Debug Overlay** only | per frame, development builds | development only | **Debug Overlay (True Values)** designed (debug-overlay-true-values.md); absent from release builds; UI palette only; the one place audio values may be shown |
| Save and load, death | none from Audio | n/a | n/a | No sound and no indicator (AA-34); Save/Load owns the gate |
| Voice and ping surfaces *(VS-B)* | the Voice bus; a ping is a UI cue | on use | co-op only | **Chat/Voice/Pings** and **Co-op Session** `(provisional — undesigned)`; the voice channel is never processed by sanity, stage or the Filter (AA-39) |

*Stored for display?* Nothing. Audio persists nothing and replicates nothing (AA-3). *Neighbouring UI that must not absorb Audio:* HUD & Suit Layer (the chest-lamp state and suit vitals are the player's own, never the web's), In-World Instruments (a needle moves only when the ship's loop moves; the tell never moves one), Chart Table (depth rings are place, not attention). Each of those GDDs must repeat the no-indicator rule or surface a CONFLICT. When the Accessibility Settings GDD is authored, run `/ux-design accessibility` against AA-41 to AA-43 and AAV-38; no UX flag is raised now because the surfaces are not this system's to design.
